. The operad structure is given by the map

(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E})\otimes(\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\penalty\ .

Proposition 8
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. The dg
\mathbb S
-module of the Hadamard tensor product
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
is quasi-free with generators
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes U_{\mathbb S}(\mathcal{P})
.

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
1
.
∎

Notice that the canonical map
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{P}
is also an arity-wise quasi-isomorphims.

Remark 18
.

We choose, as a convention, to systematically consider
\mathcal{E}
on the left hand side of the tensor products
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
for consistency reasons that will become more apparent later. One could have chosen the opposite convention, all the results also hold.

2.3.
Another presentation of the Barratt–Eccles operad

In this subsection, we give a new presentation of the Barratt-Eccles operad and its composition. The computations introduced here will be used in Subsections
2.8
and
2.10
.

Let us describe the composition in the Barratt-Eccles operad in terms of shuffle permutations. Throughout this subsection and the next one, given two permutations
\mu\in\mathbb S_{p},\psi\in\mathbb S_{q}
and
1\leq i\leq p
,
\mu\circ_{i}\psi
refers to the composition of
\mu
with
\psi
at the
i
-th leaf. This composition is given by the partial compositions of the associative operad in the category of sets.

Definition 27

(Admissible permutations)
.

Let
p,q
be two natural integers with
p\geq 1
, let
1\leq i\leq p
and let
n=p+q-1
. We say that
a permutation
\sigma\in\mathbb S_{n}
is

(1)

(p,q,i)
-
bottom admissible
if there exists a permutation
\mu\in\mathbb S_{p}
such that

\sigma=\mu\circ_{\mu^{-1}(i)}\mathrm{Id}_{q}\penalty\ ;

(2)

(p,q,i)
-
top admissible
if there exists a permutation
\psi\in\mathbb S_{q}
such that

\sigma=\mathrm{Id}_{p}\circ_{i}\psi\penalty\ ;

(3)

(p,q,i)
-
admissible
if it is
(p,q,i)
-top admissible or
(p,q,i)
-bottom admissible;

(4)

(p,q,i)
-
non admissible
if it is not
(p,q,i)
-admissible.

Lemma 2
.

Given
p,q\geq 1
and
1\leq i\leq p
, the function

\displaystyle\mathbb S_{p}\times\mathbb S_{q}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb S_{n}

\displaystyle(\mu,\psi)

\displaystyle\mapsto\mu\circ_{\mu^{-1}(i)}\psi

is injective. In particular, the intersection of
(p,q,i)
-bottom admissible permutations with
(p,q,i)
-top admissible permutations only contains the trivial permutation.

Proof.

A permutation
\sigma
in
\mathbb S_{n}
can be written as a composition
\mu\circ_{\mu^{-1}(i)}\psi
, with
\mu\in\mathbb S_{p}
and
\psi\in\mathbb S_{q}
if and only if
\sigma^{-1}
sends the segment
\{i,\cdots,i+q-1\}
to a segment. Then the permutation
\psi
is determined by the induced function between these two segments and
\mu
is determined by the function induced by collapsing these each of these segments into one element.
∎

Notice that for every
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\mathbb S_{n}
and
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathbb S_{m}
and
1\leq 1\leq n
we have

(\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1})\circ_{i}(\mu_{2}\mu_{1})=(\sigma_{2}\circ_{\sigma_{1}(i)}\mu_{2})(\sigma_{1}\circ_{i}\mu_{1})\penalty\ .

Definition 28
.

Let
k,p\geq 1,q\geq 0
,
1\leq i\leq p
and
n=p+q-1
be natural integers and let us consider two sequences of permutations
\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb S_{p}^{k}
and
\underline{\psi}\in\mathbb S_{q}^{k}
. We define
\underline{\mu}\ltimes_{i}\underline{\psi}
as the sequence
\underline{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
given by

\left\{\hbox to178.49pt{\vbox to83.46pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 89.24638pt\lower-41.7281pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-89.24638pt}{-33.52281pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right.

One can notice that

(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k-1},\mu_{k})\ltimes_{i}(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{k-1},\psi_{k})=(\mu_{1}\circ_{\mu_{1}^{-1}(i)}\psi_{1})\sqcup((\mu_{2},\ldots,\mu_{k})\ltimes_{\mu_{1}^{-1}(i)}(\psi_{2},\ldots,\psi_{k}))\penalty\ ,

where
\sqcup
stands for the concatenation of sequences of permutations.

Lemma 3
.

If
q\geq 1
, the function
-\ltimes_{i}-:\mathbb S_{p}^{k}\times\mathbb S_{q}^{k}\longrightarrow\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
is injective.

Proof.

This follows from a straightforward induction on
k
using Lemma
2
.
∎

Definition 29

(Admissible sequence)
.

Let
p,q,k
be three natural integers such that
p,k\geq 1
, let
1\leq i\leq p
and let
n=p+q-1
. A sequence of non-trivial permutations
\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
is

(1)

(p,q,i)
-admissible
if there exists two sequences of permutations
\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb S_{p}^{k}
and
\underline{\psi}\in\mathbb S_{q}^{k}
such that
\underline{\sigma}=\underline{\mu}\ltimes_{i}\underline{\psi}
, and if for every
1\leq j\leq k
at least (and necessarily at most) one of the two permuations
\mu_{j},\psi_{j}
is trivial;

(2)

(p,q,i)
-non admissible
if it is not
(p,q,i)
-admissible.

Definition 30
.

Let
\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb S_{p}^{a}
and
\underline{\psi}\in\mathbb S_{q}^{b}

be two sequences of permutations with
p,a,b\geq 1
and let
\phi
be a

(a,b)
-shuffle. We denote
\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi}

the sequence of permutation in
\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
(where
k=a+b
and
n=p+q-1
) given by

\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi}\coloneqq\phi(\underline{\mu}\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{p}^{b})\ltimes_{i}\phi(\mathrm{Id}_{q}^{a}\sqcup\underline{\psi})\penalty\ ,

where

(1)

\phi(\underline{\mu}\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{p}^{b})\in\mathbb S_{p}^{k}
is the sequence of permutations
(\mu^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mu^{\prime}_{k})
such that
\mu^{\prime}_{j}=\mu_{\phi^{-1}(j)}
if
\phi^{-1}(j)\leq a
and
\mu^{\prime}_{j}=\mathrm{Id}_{p}
otherwise;

(2)

\phi(\mathrm{Id}_{q}^{a}\sqcup\underline{\psi})\in\mathbb S_{q}^{k}
is the sequence of permutations
(\psi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\psi^{\prime}_{k})
such that
\psi^{\prime}_{j}=\psi_{\phi^{-1}(j)-a}
if
\phi^{-1}(j)\geq a+1
and
\psi^{\prime}_{j}=\mathrm{Id}_{q}
otherwise.

Lemma 4
.

The function

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\coprod_{a+b=k}(\mathbb S_{p}-\{\mathrm{Id}_{p}\})^{a}\times(\mathbb S_{q}-\{\mathrm{Id}_{p}\})^{b}\times\mathrm{Sh}(a,b)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb S_{n}-\{\mathrm{Id}_{p}\})^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{\mu},\underline{\psi},\phi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi}}

is injective, and its image is given by
(p,q,i)
-admissible sequences.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the definition of a
(p,q,i)
-admissible sequence combined with Lemma
3
.
∎

Let again
\mathcal{E}
denote the Barratt-Eccles dg operad of
[
BF04
]
.

Definition 31
.

Let
\underline{\sigma}\coloneqq(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
be a sequence of permutations, we define
\rho(\underline{\sigma})
, an element of
\mathcal{E}(n)_{k}
, as follows
as follows

(1)

if
k=0
, then
\rho(\underline{\sigma})\coloneqq\rho(*)=(\mathrm{Id}_{n})
,

(2)

if
k\geq 1
and
\underline{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})
,

\rho(\underline{\sigma})\coloneqq(\mathrm{Id}_{n},\sigma_{k},\sigma_{k-1}\sigma_{k},\ldots,\sigma_{1}\cdots\sigma_{k})\penalty\ .

Remark 19
.

A direct computation shows that
\{\rho(\underline{\sigma})\}_{\underline{\sigma}}
for
\underline{\sigma}\coloneqq(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
forms a basis of
(\mathcal{E}(n)_{\mathrm{pl}})_{k}
for all
n,k\geq 0
as a graded
\mathbb{N}
-module. Thus it is a basis of
\mathcal{E}(n)_{k}
as a graded
\mathbb{S}
-module.

Partial compositions.
Let us recall the partial compositions of the Barratt-Eccles dg operad, constructed in
[
BF04
, Section 1.1.3]
. For two elements
x=(\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{a})\in\mathcal{E}(p)_{a}
and
y=(\psi_{0},\ldots,\psi_{b})\in\mathcal{E}(p)_{b}
, given
1\leq i\leq a
and an
(a,b)
-shuffle
\phi
, let us consider

x\circ_{i,\phi}^{\mathcal{E}}y\in\mathcal{E}(p+q-1)_{a+b}\penalty\ .

It is the sequence of permutations
(\sigma_{0},\ldots,\sigma_{a+b})
defined by

\sigma_{j}=\mu_{j_{d}}\circ_{i}\psi_{j_{u}}\penalty\ ,

where

\displaystyle j_{d}

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\mathbb N|1\leq k\leq a,\ \phi(k)\leq j\}\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle j_{u}

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\mathbb N|a+1\leq k\leq a+b,\ \phi(k)\leq j\}.

The partial compositions in
\mathcal{E}
are given by

x\circ_{i}^{\mathcal{E}}y=\sum_{\phi\in\mathrm{Sh}(a,b)}(-1)^{\epsilon(\phi)}x\circ_{i,\phi}^{\mathcal{E}}y\penalty\ ,

where the sum is taken over all
(a,b)
-shuffles and where
\epsilon(\phi)
is the sign of the permutation
\phi
.

Notation
.

For every
(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n})\in\mathcal{E}(k)_{n+1}
we denote

\mathrm{inv}((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}))=(\sigma_{n},\ldots,\sigma_{1})\in\mathcal{E}(k)_{n+1}.

Lemma 5
.

For every
\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb S_{p}^{a},\underline{\psi}\in\mathbb S_{q}^{b}
and every
(a,b)
-shuffle
\phi
, we have

(\mathrm{inv}\rho(\underline{\mu}))\circ_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i),\phi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{inv}\rho(\underline{\psi}))=\mathrm{inv}\rho(\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi})\penalty\ ,

where
\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)=\mu_{a}^{-1}\cdots\mu_{1}^{-1}(i)
. Subsequently,

(\mathrm{inv}\rho(\underline{\mu}))\circ_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)}(\mathrm{inv}\rho(\underline{\psi}))=\sum_{\phi\in\mathrm{Sh}(a,b)}(-1)^{\epsilon({\phi})}\mathrm{inv}\rho(\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi})\penalty\ ,

where the sum is taken over the
(a,b)
-shuffles and
\epsilon({\phi})
is the sign of the shuffle
{\phi}
.

Proof.

For
1\leq j\leq a+b
we denote
\mu^{\prime}_{j}=\mu_{\phi^{-1}(j)}
if
\phi^{-1}(j)\leq a
and
\mu^{\prime}_{j}=\mathrm{Id}_{p}
otherwise; similarly
\psi^{\prime}_{j}=\psi_{\phi^{-1}(j)-a}
if
\phi^{-1}(j)\geq a+1
and
\mu^{\prime}_{j}=\mathrm{Id}_{q}
otherwise.
One can check that both

(\mathrm{inv}\rho(\underline{\mu}))\circ_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i),\phi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{inv}\rho(\underline{\psi}))\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{inv}\rho(\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi})

are equal to

(\mathrm{Id}_{a+b},\mu^{\prime}_{a+b}\circ_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)}\psi^{\prime}_{a+b},\cdots,(\mu^{\prime}_{1}\cdots\mu^{\prime}_{a+b})\circ_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)}(\psi^{\prime}_{1}\cdots\psi^{\prime}_{a+b}))\penalty\ .

∎

Definition 32

(Opposite shuffle)
.

Let
\phi
be a
(a,b)
-shuffle. Its
opposite

(a,b)
-shuffle

\overline{\phi}
is given by

\overline{\phi}(j)=\begin{cases}a+b+1-\phi(a+1-j)\text{ if }j\leq a\penalty\ ;\\
a+b+1-\phi(2a+b1-j)\text{ if }j\geq a+1\penalty\ .\end{cases}

In other words, the opposite
(a,b)
-shuffle
\overline{\phi}
is given by inverting indepedently the segments
\{1,\ldots,a\}
and
\{a+1,\ldots,a+b\}
, applying
\phi
to them, and finally inverting the whole segment
\{1,\ldots,a+b\}
. By inverting, we mean applying the unique permutation that yields the maximum number of inversions. Notice that the signatures of a
(a,b)
-shuffle and its opposite are related by the following formula

(-1)^{\epsilon(\overline{\phi})}=(-1)^{ab+\epsilon({\phi})}\penalty\ .

Lemma 6
.

For every
(\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{a})\in\mathcal{E}(p)_{a},(\psi_{0},\ldots,\psi_{b})\in\mathcal{E}(q)_{b}
,
1\leq i\leq p
and every
(a,b)
-shuffle
\phi
we have

\mathrm{inv}(\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{a})\circ_{i,\phi}^{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{inv}(\psi_{0},\ldots,\psi_{b})=\mathrm{inv}((\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{a})\circ_{i,\overline{\phi}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\psi_{0},\ldots,\psi_{b}))\penalty\ .

Subsequently

\mathrm{inv}(\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{a})\circ_{i}\mathrm{inv}(\psi_{0},\ldots,\psi_{b})=(-1)^{ab}\mathrm{inv}((\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{a})\circ_{i}(\psi_{0},\ldots,\psi_{b}))\penalty\ .

Proof.

Let us denote

(\sigma_{0},\ldots,\sigma_{a+b})=\mathrm{inv}(\mathrm{inv}(\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{a})\circ_{i,\phi}^{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{inv}(\psi_{0},\ldots,\psi_{b}))\penalty\ .

By definition, one has

\sigma_{a+b-j}=\mu_{a-j_{d}}\circ_{i}\psi_{b-j_{u}}

for every
0\leq j\leq a+b
, where as above

\displaystyle j_{d}

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\mathbb N|1\leq k\leq a,\ \phi(k)\leq j\}

\displaystyle j_{u}

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\mathbb N|a+1\leq k\leq a+b,\ \phi(k)\leq j\}.

By denoting
\overline{j}=a+b-j
,

\overline{j}_{d}=a-j_{d}
and
\overline{j}_{u}=b-j_{u}
, this rewrites as

\sigma_{\overline{j}}=\mu_{\overline{j}_{d}}\circ_{i}\psi_{\overline{j}_{u}}

for every
0\leq\overline{j}\leq a+b
. Combined with the fact that

\displaystyle\overline{j}_{d}=a-j_{d}

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\mathbb N|1\leq k\leq a,\ \overline{\phi}(k)>j\}

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\mathbb N|1\leq k\leq a,\ \overline{\phi}(k)\geq j+1\}

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\mathbb N|1\leq k\leq a,\ a+b+1-\overline{\phi}(k)\leq a+b+1-(j+1)\}

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\mathbb N|1\leq k\leq a,\ a+b+1-\overline{\phi}(a+1-k)\leq\overline{j}\}

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\mathbb N|1\leq k\leq a,\ \overline{\phi}(k)\leq\overline{j}\}\penalty\ ,

and similarly

\overline{j}_{u}=\#\{k\in\mathbb N|a+1\leq k\leq a+b,\ \overline{\phi}(k)\leq\overline{j}\}\penalty\ ,

we get that

(\sigma_{0},\ldots,\sigma_{a+b})=(\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{a})\circ_{i,\overline{\phi}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\psi_{0},\ldots,\psi_{b})\penalty\ .

∎

Lemma 7
.

For every
\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb S_{p}^{a},\underline{\psi}\in\mathbb S_{q}^{b}
, we have

\rho(\underline{\mu})\circ_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)}\rho(\underline{\psi})=(-1)^{ab}\sum_{\phi\in\mathrm{Sh}(a,b)}{\epsilon({\phi})}\rho(\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi})\penalty\ ,

where
\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)=\mu_{a}^{-1}\cdots\mu_{1}^{-1}(i)
, where the sum is taken over the
(a,b)
-shuffles and where
\epsilon({\phi})
is the sign of the
(a,b)
-shuffle
{\phi}
.

Proof.

This is a combination of Lemma
6
and Lemma
5
.
∎

Comonoid structure.
As described in
[
BF04
, Section 1.1]
, the Barratt-Eccles dg operad
\mathcal{E}
has the structure of a comonoid for the Hadamard tensor product. This structure is given by the map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sigma_{0},\ldots,\sigma_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}(\sigma_{0},\ldots,\sigma_{i})\otimes(\sigma_{i},\ldots,\sigma_{n})\penalty\ .}

Lemma 8
.

For every
(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
, one has that

\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}(\rho((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})))=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}\rho((\sigma_{i},\ldots,\sigma_{k}))\otimes\rho((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{i-1}))^{\sigma_{i}\cdots\sigma_{k}}\penalty\ ,

where
\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}
is the structure of a comonoid of the Barratt-Eccles dg operad
\mathcal{E}
for the Hadamard tensor product.

Proof.

This follows from direct inspection.
∎

2.4.
Homotopy theory of operads

There is a semi-model category structure on the category of dg operads constructed by B. Fresse in
[
Fre09
]
.

Theorem 1

(
[
Fre09
, Chapter 12]
)
.

The category of dg operads admits a semi-model structure, determined by the following sets of maps:

•

the set of fibrations is given by morphisms of dg operads which are arity-wise degree-wise epimorphisms,

•

the set of weak-equivalences is given by morphisms of dg operads which are arity-wise quasi-isomorphism,

•

the set of cofibrations is given by morphisms of dg operads which have the left lifting property with respect to
maps that are both fibrations and weak-equivalences.

Let us denote by
[1]
the category
0\longrightarrow 1
with two objects and a single non-trivial arrow. A functor from this category is equivalent to the data of two objects and a morphism between them.

Lemma 9
.

Let us consider a morphism of dg operad
\mathcal{P}^{(0)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(1)}
. Suppose there exists a morphism of graded
\mathbb{N}
-modules
X^{(0)}\longrightarrow X^{(1)}
such that the following diagram of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{Operads}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathbb{N}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathsf{Operads}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathsf{Operads}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb S\otimes-}

commutes. Furthermore, suppose that

(1)

the map
X^{(0)}\longrightarrow X^{(1)}
is a degree-wise injection;

(2)

the restriction of the derivation of
\mathcal{P}^{(1)}

to the generators
X^{(1)}
factors as

X^{(1)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(0)}+X^{(1)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\penalty\ .

Then the map
\mathcal{P}^{(0)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(1)}
is a cofibration.

Proof.

Let us decompose

X^{(1)}\cong X^{(0)}\oplus Y
as graded
\mathbb{N}
-modules. The restriction to
Y
of the derivation of
\mathcal{P}^{(1)}
is
a degree
-1
map
(d_{Y},\phi):Y\longrightarrow Y\oplus\mathcal{P}^{(0)}
. The fact that the whole derivation of
\mathcal{P}^{(1)}
squares to zero amounts to the facts that

(1)

the map
d_{Y}
squares to zero and thus
Y
gets the structure of a dg module ;

(2)

the map
\psi:S^{-1}\otimes Y\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(0)}
, that sends
s\otimes y
to
\phi(y)
is a morphism of dg modules.

Thus the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}((S^{-1}\otimes Y)\otimes\mathbb S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{(0)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}((D^{0}\otimes Y)\otimes\mathbb S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{(1)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

is a pushout square of dg operads. Since the left vertical map is cofibration, so is the right vertical one.
∎

Given a small ordinal
\alpha
, one can view it as a category. Objects are indexed by the ordinal
\alpha
, and there is only one non-trivial arrow between two objects
i
and
j
in
\alpha
, if and only if
i<j
. Cocontinuous functors from
\alpha
to a category give what we call
\alpha
-indexed
ladders
. We refer to Subsection
Notations and conventions
for more details.

Proposition 9
.

Let
\alpha
be a small ordinal and let

\mathcal{P}^{(0)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(2)}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\cdots\penalty\ ,

be an
\alpha
-indexed ladder of dg operads. Suppose there exists an
\alpha
-indexed ladder

X^{(0)}\longrightarrow X^{(1)}\longrightarrow X^{(2)}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow X^{(i)}\longrightarrow\cdots\penalty\ ,

of graded
\mathbb{N}
-modules such that the following diagram of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{Operads}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathsf{Operads}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathsf{Operads}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb S\otimes-}

commutes. Furthermore, suppose that

(1)

for every
i,i+1\in\alpha
, the map
X^{(i)}\longrightarrow X^{(i+1)}
is a degree-wise injection;

(2)

for every
i\in\alpha
, the restriction of the derivation of
\mathcal{P}^{(i+1)}

to the generators
X^{(i+1)}
factors as

X^{(i+1)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(i)}+X^{(i+1)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(i+1)}.

Then the colimit dg operad

\mathcal{P}^{(\alpha)}\coloneqq\underset{i\in\alpha}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}^{(i)}

is cofibrant.

Proof.

Let
v
be the subset of
\alpha+1
of elements
i
so that
\mathcal{P}^{(i)}
is cofibrant. It satisfies the following properties:

(1)

it contains the first element
0\in\alpha+1
since
\mathcal{P}^{(0)}\cong\mathcal{I}
;

(2)

for
i,i+1\in\alpha+1
, if
i\in v
, then
i+1\in v
by Lemma
9
;

(3)

if
i\in\alpha+1
is a limit ordinal and if
v\sqcap\{j<i\}=\{j<i\}
, then
i\in v
since cofibrations are stable through transfinite composition.

Therefore
v=\alpha
, which implies that
P^{(\alpha)}
is cofibrant.
∎

2.5.
Conilpotent cooperad ladders

Definition 33

(Cooperad ladder)
.

Let
\alpha
be a small ordinal. An
\alpha
-
cooperad ladder
amounts to the data of a functor

\mathcal{C}:\alpha\longrightarrow\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}^{\mathsf{conil}}\penalty\ ,

which satisfies the following conditions:

(1)

for every limit ordinal
i\in\alpha
, the map

\underset{j<i}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}^{(j)}\to\mathcal{C}^{(i)}

is an isomorphism; equivalently, the extension of
\mathcal{C}^{(-)}
to
1+\alpha

that sends
0
to
\mathcal{I}
is cocontinuous;

(2)

for every
i,i+1\in\alpha
, the map
\mathcal{C}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{(i+1)}

is an arity-wise degree-wise injection;

(3)

the decomposition map of the underlying pdg conilpotent cooperad

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{(i+1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}\mathcal{C}^{(i+1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{T}}\mathcal{C}^{(i+1)}}

factors through
\overline{\mathbb{T}}\mathcal{C}^{(i)}
.

We denote

\mathcal{C}^{(\alpha)}\coloneqq\underset{i\in\alpha}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}^{(i)}\penalty\ ,

the colimit of the cooperad ladder.

The
associated graded
of this ladder is given by

\mathrm{gr}_{i}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}^{(i)}/\underset{j<i}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}^{(j)}\penalty\ .

In fact, we have that
\mathrm{gr}_{i}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\cong\mathcal{C}^{(i)}/\mathcal{C}^{(i-1)}
and that
\mathrm{gr}_{0}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}^{(0)}
. Furthermore,
\mathrm{gr}_{i}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}=0
when
i
is a limit ordinal.

Remark 20
.

For all
i\in\alpha
, the associated graded object
\mathrm{gr}_{i}\mathcal{C}
is a dg module. Indeed, the induced coderivation on it squares to zero because of the condition in Definition
23
.

Example 2

(Coradical ladder)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent curved cooperad. The coradical filtration of
\mathcal{C}

\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{rad}}_{0}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{rad}}_{1}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\cdots\rightarrowtail\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{rad}}_{n}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\cdots\rightarrowtail\underset{i\in\omega}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ \mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{rad}}_{i}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\cong\mathcal{C}

is a cooperad ladder called the
coradical ladder
. Notice that it is also the coradical development of the constant ladder.

2.6.
Quasi-planar cooperad ladders and quasi-planar cooperads

Definition 34

(pseudo-planar cooperad)
.

A
pseudo-planar
conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
amounts to the data
(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}},\mathcal{C},\varphi_{\mathcal{C}})
of a
graded planar conilpotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
and a conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
together with an isomorphism of graded conilpotent cooperads

\varphi_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\cong\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S.

A morphism
f:(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}},\mathcal{C},\varphi_{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}},\mathcal{D},\varphi_{\mathcal{D}})
of pseudo-planar curved conilpotent cooperads is the data of a morphism of graded planar conilpotent cooperads
f_{\mathrm{pl}}:\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\to\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}
such that the composite map

f:\mathcal{C}\cong\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S\xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S}\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S\cong\mathcal{D}

is a morphism of conilpotent curved cooperads. We denote the category of pseudo-planar conilpotent curved cooperads by
\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}^{\mathsf{conil}}_{\mathrm{pseudo-pl}}
.

Remark 21
.

Notice that the category of seudo planar conilpotent curved cooperads fits into the following pseudo-commutative square diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}^{\mathsf{conil}}_{\mathrm{pseudo-pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}^{\mathsf{conil}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}^{\mathsf{conil}}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}^{\mathsf{conil}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes\mathbb S}

which is a homotopy pullback.

Definition 35

(Quasi-planar cooperad ladder)
.

A
quasi-planar cooperad ladder
amounts to the data
of a small ordinal
\alpha
and a functor

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle\to\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}^{\mathsf{conil}}_{\mathrm{pseudo-pl}}

\displaystyle i

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i)},\mathcal{C}^{(i)},\varphi_{\mathcal{C}^{(i)}})\penalty\ ,

that satisfies the following conditions:

(1)

the functor
\mathcal{C}
is a cooperad ladder (thus
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
is also cocontinuous);

(2)

for every
i,i+1\in\alpha
, the restriction of the coderivation of
\mathcal{C}^{(i+1)}
to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}\otimes 1
factors through

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}\otimes 1\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}\otimes 1+\mathcal{C}^{(i)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{(i+1)};

in other words, the differential of
\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}=\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}/\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i)}\right)\otimes\mathbb S
has the form
d_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb S}
.

Definition 36

(Quasi-planar cooperad)
.

A
quasi-planar
conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
amounts to the data of a pseudo-planar curved conilpotent cooperad
(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}},\mathcal{C},\varphi_{\mathcal{C}})
which is the colimit of a quasi-planar cooperad ladder.

Remark 22
.

The main ideas for this definition where already in
[
GL22
, Definition 11.2]
.

Remark 23
.

In a future work, we will show describe the relation between the notion of a quasi-planar cooperad and the notion of a
higher cooperad
introduced in
[
DV21
]
.

Proposition 10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent dg cooperad. The underlying dg
\mathbb{S}
-module of
\mathcal{C}
is projective.

Proof.

Let us prove this by induction on the quasi-planar ladder that defines
\mathcal{C}
. The dg
\mathbb{S}
-module
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}
is free as a dg
\mathbb{S}
-module, therefore it is projective.

Let us write
\mathcal{C}^{(i)}
as the direct sum
\mathcal{C}^{(i-1)}\oplus Y
in the category of graded
\mathbb{S}
-modules. Then
\mathcal{C}^{(i)}
is obtained by attaching
Y
to
\mathcal{C}^{(i-1)}
via a pushout of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S^{-1}\otimes Y)\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{(i-1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(D^{0}\otimes Y)\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{(i)}\penalty\ ,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

which implies that
\mathcal{C}^{(i)}
is again cofibrant in the category of dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules endowed with the projective model structure. We conclude by the fact that the colimit of cofibrant objects is again a cofibrant.
∎

Remark 24
.

When
\mathcal{C}
is a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad, the pre-differential of its underlying pdg
\mathbb{S}
-module can still be constructed by attaching cells at each step.

Quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperads are sent to cofibrant dg operads in the semi-model category structure of
[
Fre09
]
by the operadic cobar construction of Subsection
2.1
.

Proposition 11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. Then the dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is cofibrant.

Proof.

We can notice that the composition of the following functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}^{\mathsf{conil}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{Operads}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}

satisfies the conditions of Proposition
9
, which allows us to conclude.
∎

2.7.
The bar-Barratt-Eccles construction is quasi-planar

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. Our goal is to prove that the curved conilpotent cooperad
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
has the canonical structure of a quasi-planar cooperad.

For all arities
m\geq 0
, remember that there is a canonical isomorphism of graded
\mathbb S
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}(m)\otimes\mathcal{P}(m)\cong(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}(m)\otimes\mathbb S_{m})\otimes\mathcal{P}(m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}(m)\otimes\mathcal{P}(m))\otimes\mathbb S_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(e\otimes\{\sigma\})\otimes p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle(e\otimes p^{\sigma^{-1}})\otimes\{\sigma\}\penalty\ ,}

where the right action of
\mathbb S_{m}
on
\mathcal{E}(m)\otimes\mathcal{P}(m)
is diagonal, given by
(e\otimes p)^{\sigma}=e^{\sigma}\otimes p^{\sigma}
, and where
(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}(m)\otimes\mathcal{P}(m))\otimes\mathbb S_{m}
is the free right
\mathbb S_{m}
-module on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}(m)\otimes\mathcal{P}(m)
. This isomorphism is described in Proposition
1
. Notice that it commutes with
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes d_{\mathcal{P}}
.

Let us denote
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}
the graded planar conilpotent cooperad

\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}=\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I}).

One has a canonical isomorphism of graded conilpotent cooperad

\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})=\mathbb{T}(s\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I})\cong\mathbb{T}\left((s\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I})\otimes\mathbb S\right)\cong\left(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I})\otimes\mathbb S\right)=\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S.

One can to filter the graded
\mathbb N
-module
s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P})
by the degree of the elements of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}
:

(\tilde{F}_{n}s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P}))(m)\coloneqq s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl},\leq n-1}(m)\otimes\mathcal{P}(m))\penalty\ ,\quad n\in\mathbb N\penalty\ ,

where
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl},\leq n}(m)
is the graded
\mathbb S
-module that consists in elements of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}(m)
whose degree is at most
n

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl},\leq n}(m)_{k}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}(m)_{k}\text{ if }k\leq n;\\
0\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

One can notice that

(\tilde{F}_{0}s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P}))(m)=0\penalty\ ,

Then, one filters
s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P})\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{0}(s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P})\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I})=0

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{n}(s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P})\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I})=\tilde{F}_{n}(s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P})\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I},\quad n\geq 1.

Thus, one can also filter
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}=\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P})\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I})
by

\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}(m)=\bigoplus_{t}\sum_{i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k}=n}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\penalty\ \tilde{F}_{i_{j}}(s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P})\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I})(l_{j})\penalty\ ,

where the first sum is taken over the planar trees
t
with
m
-leaves ; it has
k
nodes whose arities are
l_{1},\ldots,l_{k}
.

Finally, this induces a filtration
\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\simeq(\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}})\otimes\mathbb S
on the underlying graded conilpotent cooperad of
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
.

Remark 25
.

Let us point out that the same filtration was used in
[
Hof16
, Section 2]
in order to develop the obstruction theory of morphisms of algebras over a dg operad in positive characteristic.

Proposition 12
.

For every integer
n\geq 0
,
\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
is a conilpotent curved sub-cooperad of
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
. Moreover,
the diagrams

\tilde{F}_{0}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}\longrightarrow\tilde{F}_{1}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}\longrightarrow\cdots

\tilde{F}_{0}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\tilde{F}_{1}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\cdots

form a quasi-planar
\omega
-ladder whose colimit is given by the pseudo-planar conilpotent curved cooperad
(\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}},\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}))
.

Proof.

The only point that is not clear is the fact that the non-planar component of the pre-differential
of
\tilde{F}_{n+1}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
actually targets
\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
. This follows from the fact that this non-planar component is given by applying the non-planar part of the differential of
\mathcal{E}
.
∎

Remark 26
.

A first version of this proposition was proven in
[
GL22
, Proposition 11.31]
.

Example 3
.

Let
f:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
be a morphism of dg operads. The morphism of conilpotent curved cooperads
\mathrm{B}(f\otimes\mathcal{E}):\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{Q})
is pseudo-planar in the sense that it restricts to a map

\mathrm{B}(f\otimes\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{pl}}:\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{Q})_{\mathrm{pl}}\penalty\ ,

in a compatible way with the pseudo-planar isomorphisms.

Over a characteristic zero field, dg operads admit a model category where weak-equivalences are given by arity-wise quasi-isomorphisms and where fibrations are given by arity-wise degree-wise epimorphisms. One can then consider the transferred model category structure, along the operadic bar-cobar adjunction, on conilpotent curved cooperads constructed in
[
Gri21
]
. Every conilpotent curved cooperad is then shown to be weak-equivalent to a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad.

Proposition 13

(
[
GL22
, Lemma 11.32]
)
.

Let
\Bbbk
be a field of characteristic zero and let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent curved cooperad. There exists a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and an acyclic cofibration
\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The canonical quasi-morphism
\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\Omega\mathcal{C}
admits a section, since both dg operads are cofibrant. Therefore it suffices to consider the following composition

\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})\penalty\ ,

which is an acylic cofibration since it is the composition of two acyclic cofibrations.
∎

Remark 27
.

Proposition
13
will imply that when
\Bbbk
is a field of characteristic zero, one does recover the previously known homotopical operadic calculus of
[
LG19
]
and
[
GL22
]
.

Corollary 1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent curved cooperad. The following assertions
are equivalent

(1)

the operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is cofibrant;

(2)

the morphism of dg operads
\Omega\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}

admits a section.

Proof.

If
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is cofibrant, it is clear that the map

\Omega\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}

admits a section. Conversely, let us suppose that this map admits a section. Then the projection
\Omega\mathrm{B}((\Omega\mathcal{C})\otimes\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}
also admits a section given by

\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})\penalty\ .

Therefore
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is a retract of
\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})
. Since
\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})
is cofibrant (by Proposition
11
combined with Proposition
12
), then its retract
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is also cofibrant.
∎

Remark 28
.

In fact, for any
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad, the dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is not only
\mathcal{E}
-split, but there exists explicit canonical map
\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C}
which endows
\Omega\mathcal{C}
with a
\mathcal{E}
-comodule structure. This is done in the next Subsection
2.8
.

2.8.
The universal structure of a Barratt-Eccles comodule

In this subsection, we equip any dg operad of the form
\Omega\mathcal{C}
with a
\mathcal{E}
-comodule structure, where
\mathcal{C}
is again a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad and where
\mathcal{E}
denotes the Barratt-Eccles operad. This comonoid structure can be viewed as the positive characteristic analogue of the canonical
\mathrm{u}\mathcal{C}\mathrm{om}
-comonoid structure that exists for any dg operad. By that, we mean that it produces universal convolution (curved absolute) partition
\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
-algebra structures on the hom-graded modules between types of coalgebras and types of algebras. For more details, we refer to the forthcoming paper
[
GRiL24
]
.

Recall that the restriction of the differential on
\Omega\mathcal{C}
to the generators
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
decomposes into three maps

•

d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}:s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\longrightarrow s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S\cong s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}
, which is given by
s^{-1}d_{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}
;

•

d_{\Delta}:s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(2)}s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
, induced by the (planar) partial decompositions;

•

d_{\theta}:s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
, induced by the curvature of
\mathcal{C}
.

For
p\geq 1,q\geq 0,1\leq i\leq p
, let us denote

\Delta^{p,q}_{i}:s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(p+q-1)\longrightarrow s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(p)\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(q)

the
i
-th (planar) partial decomposition map of the cooperad
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
into arity
p
and arity
q
elements. It is the composition of the map
d_{\Delta}
at arity
p+q-1
with the projection onto planar trees
t
with two nodes

•

the root node with
p
leaves;

•

the second node with
q
leaves and that is plugged to the root node at its ith leaf.

Let
M
be a pdg
\mathbb S
-module that is
quasi-free
, that is, such that there exists a graded
\mathbb N
-module
N
and an isomorphism of graded
\mathbb S
-module
M\cong N\otimes\mathbb S
. We are interested in how the pre-differential interacts with the action of the symmetric groups. Note for any given
n\geq 0
, there is an isomorphism

M(n)\cong\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{n}}N(n)\otimes\{\sigma\}\penalty\ ,

of graded modules.

Definition 37

(
\sigma
pre-differential)
.

Let
M
be a pdg
\mathbb S
-module that is
quasi-free
, that is, such that there exists a graded
\mathbb N
-module
N
and an isomorphism of graded
\mathbb S
-module
M\cong N\otimes\mathbb S
. Let
\sigma
be in
\mathbb S_{n}
, the
\sigma

pre-differential
, denoted by
d_{\sigma}
, is the degree
-1
endomorphism of
N(n)
given by the composite

N(n)\simeq N(n)\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\}\hookrightarrow M(n)\xrightarrow{d_{M}}M(n)\twoheadrightarrow N(n)\otimes\{\sigma\}\simeq N(n)\penalty\ .

We denote by
D_{\sigma}
the
\mathbb S_{n}
-equivariant degree
-1
endomorphism of
M(n)
whose restriction to the generators
N
is the composition

N(n)\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\}\hookrightarrow M(n)\xrightarrow{d_{M}}M(n)\twoheadrightarrow N(n)\otimes\{\sigma\}\hookrightarrow M(n)\penalty\ .

For any
x
in
N(n)
, we have
D_{\sigma}(X\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\})=d_{\sigma}(x)\otimes\{\sigma\}
.

Similarly, for a sequence of permutations
\underline{\sigma}\coloneqq(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
,
we set

d_{\underline{\sigma}}\coloneqq d_{\sigma_{1}}\cdots d_{\sigma_{k}}\quad\text{and}\quad D_{\underline{\sigma}}\coloneqq D_{\sigma_{1}}\cdots D_{\sigma_{k}}\penalty\ .

Notice that, for any
x
in
N(n)
, we have

D_{\emptyset}(x\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\})=x\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\}\penalty\ ;\quad D_{(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})}(x\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\})=d_{(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})}(x)\otimes\{\sigma_{1}\cdots\sigma_{k}\}\penalty\ .

The case of interest is when
M
is the underlying pdg
\mathbb S
-module of the quasi-planar curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and
N
is the graded
\mathbb{N}
-module
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
of planar generators.

Lemma 10
.

Let
n\geq 0
and let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. For every planar generator
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)
, there exists a natural integer
k_{x}
such that for every sequence of permutations
(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
of length
k\geq k_{x}
, we have

d_{(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})}(x)=0\penalty\ .

The same result holds, mutatis mutandis, for
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}
equipped with the pre-differential
d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}=s^{-1}d_{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Proof.

Since the cooperad
\mathcal{C}
is quasi-planar, it is the colimit of a quasi-planar ladder
\{\mathcal{C}^{(i)}\}_{i\in\alpha}
. Let us prove by an ordinal induction that
\mathcal{C}^{(i)}
satisfies the property of the lemma for every
i<\alpha+1
.

The conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}
is planar, therefore
d_{\sigma}
is zero whenever the permutation
\sigma
is non-trivial. So we only need to worry about the planar pre-differential
D_{\mathrm{Id}}
. Now notice that by definition
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}
admits no non-trivial decomposition, therefore its pre-differential squares to zero. This proves the property for
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}
.

Now, let us suppose that
\mathcal{C}^{(j)}
satisfies the property for every
j<i
. If
i
is a limit ordinal,
\mathcal{C}^{(i)}
also satisfies the property. Otherwise,
i
has the form
i=l+1
. One can notice that on
\mathrm{gr}_{i}\mathcal{C}
, the maps
D_{\sigma}
is zero whenever the permutation
\sigma
is non-trivial and that
d^{2}=D_{\mathrm{Id}}^{2}=0
. So, for every
x\in\mathcal{C}^{(i)}(n)
, and for every two permutations
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
,
D_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})}(x)
is in
\mathcal{C}^{(l)}(n)
. This proves the property for
\mathcal{C}^{(i)}
, and allows us to conclude. Finally, the result for
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}
is a direct consequence of the previous one.
∎

The comodule map.
Let us consider the morphism of graded
\mathbb{N}
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}:s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}x\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\rho(\underline{\sigma})\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(s^{-1}x\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\})\penalty\ ,}

where
s^{-1}x\in s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)
is a planar generator and where the sum is taken over all sequences of permutations
\underline{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
for all
k\geq 0
. The formula is well-defined by Lemma
10
. Moreover, notice that
\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}
is the restriction of the morphism of graded operads

\Omega\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}}\mathbb{T}(\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow\mathbb{T}(\mathcal{E})\otimes\mathbb{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C}\penalty\ ,

which we also denote by
\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}
.

Theorem 2
.

The map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}:s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}x\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\rho(\underline{\sigma})\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(s^{-1}x\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\})\penalty\ ,}

where
s^{-1}x\in s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)
is a planar generator and where the sum is taken over all sequences of permutations
\underline{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
for all
k\geq 0
, induces a morphism of dg operads

\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C}

which endows
\Omega\mathcal{C}
with a left
\mathcal{E}
-comodule structure.

Proof.

By definition, it induces a morphism of graded operads. By Lemma
11
, Lemma
13
and Lemma
17
, this induced morphism commutes with the differentials. Thus, it is a morphism of dg operads. By Lemma
18
, it defines a left
\mathcal{E}
-comodule structure.
∎

The rest of this subsection is devoted to proving Theorem
2
.

Lemma 11
.

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{I}\otimes\mathbb S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\theta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\otimes d_{\theta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}}

is commutative, where
d_{\theta}
is the term of the differential in
\Omega\mathcal{C}
induced by the curvature
\theta
of
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

The diagram trivially commutes at arity
n\neq 1
. In arity
1
, the commutation is a direct consequence of the fact that
\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}(x\otimes\mathrm{Id})=(\mathrm{Id})\otimes x\otimes\{\mathrm{Id}\}
for any
x\in s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(1)
.
∎

Lemma 12
.

For every natural integers,
n\geq 2,k\geq 1
, we have an equality of degree
-1
maps from
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}(n)
to
\mathcal{E}(n)\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}(n)
:

\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}d_{\mathcal{E}}\rho(\underline{\sigma})\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)=\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\rho((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k-1}))^{\sigma_{k}}\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)+(-1)^{k}\rho((\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{k}))\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)\penalty\ .

Proof.

We have

d_{\mathcal{E}}(\rho(\underline{\sigma}))=\rho((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k-1}))^{\sigma_{k}}+\sum_{0<i<k}(-1)^{k-i}\delta_{i}(\rho(\underline{\sigma}))+(-1)^{k}\rho((\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{k}))\penalty\ ,

where

\delta_{i}(\rho(\underline{\sigma}))=\rho(\sigma_{1}\ldots,\sigma_{i}\sigma_{i+1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})\penalty\ .

This gives us

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}d_{\mathcal{E}}\rho(\overline{\sigma})\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\Big(\rho((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k-1}))^{\sigma_{k}}\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)

\displaystyle+\sum_{0<i<k}(-1)^{k-i}\sum_{\underline{\sigma}}\delta_{i}(\rho(\underline{\sigma}))\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)\Big)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{k}\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\rho((\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{k}))\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)\penalty\ .

Since the underlying graded conilpotent cooperad of
\mathcal{C}
is planar, the curvature equation tells us that
d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}^{2}
is planar and that, for every permutation
\sigma\in\mathbb S_{n}
,

\sum_{\mu\xi=\sigma}D_{\mu}D_{\xi}=\begin{cases}d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}^{2}\text{ if }\sigma=\mathrm{Id}\penalty\ ,\\
0\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Noticing that
\delta_{i}(\rho(\underline{\sigma}))
depends only on the product
\sigma_{i}\sigma_{i+1}
and not on the particular values of
\sigma_{i}
and
\sigma_{i+1}
, we have

\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\delta_{i}(\rho(\underline{\sigma}))\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)=\sum_{\underline{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k-1})\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k-1}}\sum_{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}=\mu_{i}}\rho(\underline{\mu})\otimes D_{(\mu_{1},\ldots,\sigma\sigma^{\prime},\ldots,\mu_{k-1})}(-)\penalty\ .

If
\mu_{i}=\mathrm{Id}
then
\rho(\underline{\mu})=0
and if
\mu_{i}\neq\mathrm{Id}
then
D_{(\mu_{1},\ldots,\sigma\sigma^{\prime},\ldots,\mu_{k-1})}=0
. Thus

\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\delta_{i}(\rho(\underline{\sigma}))\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)=0\penalty\ ,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 13
.

The following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}+\mathrm{Id}\otimes d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}}

is commutative.

Proof.

In arity
0
and
1
, the commutation is straightforward to check. In arity
n\geq 2
, we have
d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{n}}D_{\sigma}
. This gives the following equalities of maps on
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}(n)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}(-)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k},\penalty\ \sigma\in\mathbb S_{n}}\rho(\underline{\sigma})^{\sigma}\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}D_{\sigma}(-)\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Id}\otimes d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}})\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}(-)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k},\penalty\ \sigma\in\mathbb S_{n}}(-1)^{k}\rho(\underline{\sigma})\otimes D_{\sigma}D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)\penalty\ .

It follows from Lemma
12
that

\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}(-)=(d_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\mathrm{Id})\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}(-)+(\mathrm{Id}\otimes d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}})\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}(-)\penalty\ ,

and thus that the square also commutes in arity
n\geq 2
.
∎

Lemma 14
.

Let
p,q\geq 1
,
1\leq i\leq p
,
n=p+q-1
and let
\sigma\in\mathbb S_{n}
be a non-trivial permutation. One has the following egalites between maps from
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}})(n)
to
(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(2)}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))(n)\otimes\mathbb S_{n}
depending on the
(p,q,i)
-admissibility of
\sigma
.

(1)

If
\sigma
is
(p,q,i)
-top admissible, that is, if there exists a unique non-trivial permutation
\psi\in\mathbb S_{q}
such that
\sigma=\mathrm{Id}_{p}\circ_{i}\psi
, then

\Delta_{i}^{p,q}D_{\sigma}=-(\mathrm{Id}\otimes D_{\psi})\Delta_{i}^{p,q}\penalty\ .

(2)

If
\sigma
is
(p,q,i)
-bottom admissible, that is, if there exists a unique non-trivial permutation
\mu\in\mathbb S_{p}
such that
\sigma=\mu\circ_{\mu^{-1}(i)}\mathrm{Id}_{q}
, then

\Delta_{i}^{p,q}D_{\sigma}=-(D_{\mu}\otimes\mathrm{Id})\Delta_{\mu^{-1}(i)}^{p,q}\penalty\ .

(3)

If
\sigma
is
(p,q,i)
-non admissible, then

\Delta_{i}^{p,q}D_{\sigma}=0\penalty\ .

Proof.

Since the derivation of
\Omega\mathcal{C}
squares to zero, one has the following equation

d_{\Delta}d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}+(d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}+\mathrm{Id}\otimes d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}})d_{\Delta}=0

between maps from
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
to
(\mathbb{T}^{(2)}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}))\simeq(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(2)}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\otimes\mathbb S
. In arity
n
, we denote

\pi_{p,q,i,\sigma}
the projection

(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(2)}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))(n)\otimes\mathbb S_{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl},p,q,i}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}})\otimes\{\sigma\}\penalty\ ,

where
\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl},p,q,i}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}})
denotes the planar trees labelled by
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
that have two nodes: the root node with
p
input and another node with
q
inputs, where the top node is attached to the
i^{th}
input of the root node.
One has

\pi_{p,q,i,\sigma}d_{\Delta}d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}=\Delta_{i}^{p,q}D_{\sigma}.

Moreover,

\pi_{p,q,i,\sigma}(d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}\otimes\mathrm{Id})d_{\Delta}=\begin{cases}(D_{\mu}\otimes\mathrm{Id})\Delta_{\mu^{-1}(i)}^{p,q}\text{ if $\sigma$ is bottom admissible;}\\
0\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

and

\pi_{p,q,i,\sigma}(\mathrm{Id}\otimes d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}})d_{\Delta}=\begin{cases}(\mathrm{Id}\otimes D_{\psi})\Delta_{i}^{p,q}\text{ if $\sigma$ is top admissible;}\\
0\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Then, the three equations of the proposition are just the three possible cases (in terms of
(p,q,i)
-admissibility of
\sigma
) of the equation

\pi_{p,q,i,\sigma}(d_{\Delta}d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}+(d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}+\mathrm{Id}\otimes d_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}})d_{\Delta})=0\penalty\ .

∎

Lemma 15
.

Let us denote by
u
the generator element of
u\mathcal{A}ss(0)=\mathcal{E}(0)
.
Let
p\geq 1
, let
\sigma\in\mathbb S_{p}
be a non-trivial permutation and let
1\leq i\leq p+1
. Then,

\sum_{\mu\penalty\ \circ_{\mu^{-1}(i)}u=\sigma}(D_{\mu}\otimes\mathrm{Id})\Delta_{\mu^{-1}(i)}^{p,0}=-\Delta_{i}^{p,0}D_{\sigma}\penalty\ ,

where the sum ranges over all
\mu
in
\mathbb S_{p+1}
such that
\mu\penalty\ \circ_{\mu^{-1}(i)}u=\sigma
.

Proof.

It follows from the same arguments as those used in the proof of Lemma
14
.
∎

Lemma 16
.

Let
k\geq 0
,
p\geq 1
,
q\geq 0
,
1\leq i\leq p
,
n=p+q-1
and let
\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}
be a sequence of non-trivial permutations. Then

\Delta_{i}^{p,q}D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)=\sum_{\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi}=\underline{\sigma}}(-1)^{k}\epsilon(\phi)(D_{\underline{\mu}}\otimes D_{\underline{\psi}})\Delta_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)}^{p,q}(-)\penalty\ ,

where the sum ranges over all
\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb S_{p}^{a}
and all
\underline{\psi}\in\mathbb S_{q}^{b}
, with
a\geq 1
,
b\geq 1
and
a+b=k
, such that there exists a
(a,b)
-shuffle
\phi
such that
\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi}=\underline{\sigma}
, and where
\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)=\mu_{a}^{-1}\cdots\mu_{1}^{-1}(i)
.

Proof.

First, in the case where
q\geq 1
, a straightforward induction using Lemma
14
shows that:

(1)

if
\underline{\sigma}
is
(p,q,i)
-admissible, that is, if there exist an unique shuffle permutation
\phi\in\mathbb S_{k}
(which is an
(a,b)
-shuffle) and an unique pair of sequences of permutations
\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb S_{p}^{a},\underline{\psi}\in\mathbb S_{q}^{b}
such that

\underline{\sigma}=\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi}\penalty\ ,

then

\Delta_{i}^{p,q}D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)=(-1)^{k}\epsilon(\phi)(D_{\underline{\mu}}\otimes D_{\underline{\psi}})\Delta_{j}^{p,q}(-)\penalty\ ,

where
j=\mu_{a}^{-1}\cdots\mu_{1}^{-1}(i)
.

(2)

If
\underline{\sigma}
is not
(p,q,i)
-admissible, then

\Delta_{i}^{p,q}D_{\underline{\sigma}}(-)=0\penalty\ .

In the case where
q=0
, the result follows from a similar induction using Lemma
15
.
∎

Lemma 17
.

The following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}})\otimes\mathbb S.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\otimes d_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

is commutative, where
\gamma
is the composition within the operad
\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

First let
p\geq 1,q\geq 0
,
1\leq i\leq p
and
n=p+q-1
. As in the proof of Lemma
14
, we denote
\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl},p,q,i}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}})
the planar trees labelled by
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
that have two nodes: the root node with
p
inputs and another node with
q
inputs, which is attached to the
i^{th}
input of the root node.
Let us prove that, in arity
n
, the composition of the two maps

s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)\rightrightarrows\mathcal{E}(n)\otimes\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})(n)\otimes\mathbb S_{n}

with the projection

\mathrm{Id}\otimes\pi_{p,q,i}\otimes\mathrm{Id}:\mathcal{E}(n)\otimes\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})(n)\otimes\mathbb S_{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{E}(n)\otimes\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl},p,q,i}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}})\otimes\mathbb S_{n}

are equal. This amounts to show that

\sum_{\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb S_{p}^{a},\underline{\psi}\in\mathbb S_{q}^{b}}(-1)^{|\mu||\psi|}(\rho(\underline{(}\mu))\circ_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)}\rho(\underline{(}\psi)))\otimes((D_{\underline{\mu}}\otimes D_{\underline{\psi}})\Delta^{p,q}_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)})=(\mathrm{Id}\otimes\Delta^{p,q}_{i})\left(\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\rho(\underline{\sigma})\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}}\right)\penalty\ ,

which follows from the sequence of equalities

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb S_{p}^{a},\underline{\psi}\in\mathbb S_{q}^{b}}(-1)^{|\mu||\psi|}(\rho(\underline{(}\mu))\circ_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)}\rho(\underline{(}\psi)))\otimes((D_{\underline{\mu}}\otimes D_{\underline{\psi}})\Delta^{p,q}_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb S_{p}^{a},\underline{\psi}\in\mathbb S_{q}^{b},\phi\in\mathrm{Sh}(a,b)}(-1)^{\epsilon(\phi)}\rho(\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi})\otimes((D_{\underline{\mu}}\otimes D_{\underline{\psi}})\Delta^{p,q}_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)})\quad\text{ by Lemma \ref{lemma:inversecompinbe}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\sum_{\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi}=\underline{\sigma}}(-1)^{\epsilon(\phi)}\rho(\underline{\sigma})\otimes((D_{\underline{\mu}}\otimes D_{\underline{\psi}})\Delta^{p,q}_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\rho(\underline{\sigma})\otimes\left(\sum_{\underline{\mu}\circ_{i,\phi}\underline{\psi}=\underline{\sigma}}(-1)^{\epsilon(\phi)}((D_{\underline{\mu}}\otimes D_{\underline{\psi}})\Delta^{p,q}_{\underline{\mu}^{-1}(i)})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{\sigma}\in\mathbb S_{n}^{k}}\rho(\underline{\sigma})\otimes\left((-1)^{|\sigma|}(\Delta^{p,q}_{i}D_{\underline{\sigma}})\right)\quad\text{ by Lemma \ref{propositionbeecomposition}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Id}\otimes\Delta^{p,q}_{i})(\sum_{\underline{\sigma}}\rho(\underline{\sigma})\otimes D_{\underline{\sigma}})\penalty\ .

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 18
.

The following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}}

Proof.

This is direct consequence of Lemma
8

∎

Corollary 2
.

The canonical morphism of conilpotent curved cooperads

\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})

that is adjoint to the morphism of dg operads
\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}:\Omega\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C}
is pseudo-planar in the sense that it restricts to a morphism of graded planar cooperads

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\to\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})_{\mathrm{pl}}\penalty\ ,

which is compatible with the pseudo-planar isomorphisms.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the fact that the restriction of the map

\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}}:\Omega\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C}

to
s^{-1}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
factors through
(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})_{\mathrm{pl}}=\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C}
.
∎

2.9.
The comodule structure on a bar-Barratt-Eccles construction

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. The goal of this subsection is to compute the map

\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}:s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\penalty\ .

The structure of an Hadamard comonoid on the Barratt-Eccles operad
\mathcal{E}

yields a map of graded
\mathbb S
-modules

s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{s(\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\mathrm{Id})}s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\cong\mathcal{E}\otimes s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})

Together with the map

s^{2}\mathcal{I}\cong\mathcal{I}\otimes s^{2}\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{2}\mathcal{I}

it yields a map

s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}).

Then, we get a composite map:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathbb{T}}(s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}(s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{E}\otimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}(s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}).}

One can notice that the restriction of this map to
s^{-1}\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}))
factors through
s^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}(s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}))
since the restriction of
\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathcal{E}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}
to
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}
factors through
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{E}
and since
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}
is stable through the operadic compososition
of
\mathcal{E}
.

Proposition 14
.

The map
\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}
is equal to the above composition.

Proof.

Let us denote
f_{1}=\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}
and let
f_{2}
be the above composition. One can check that
f_{2}
makes the diagram of Lemma
21
commute as well as
f_{1}
(by Lemma
17
). Moreover the restriction of
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are equal on
s^{-1}s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I})
by Lemma
20
. We conclude by Lemma
21
.
∎

Lemma 19
.

Let
p\in\mathcal{P}(m)_{k}
, let
(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n})\in\mathbb S_{m}^{n}
and let
(\mu_{i},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb S_{m}^{n-i+1}
be non trivial permutations. Then
the element

D_{(\mu_{i},\ldots,\mu_{n})}\left(\rho((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}))\otimes p\right)\in(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})(m)_{k-n-1+i}\penalty\ ,

is equal to

\begin{cases}\left(\rho((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{i-1}))\otimes p^{(\sigma_{i}\cdots\sigma_{n})^{-1}}\right)^{\sigma_{i}\cdots\sigma_{n}}\text{ if }(\mu_{i},\ldots,\mu_{n})=(\sigma_{i},\ldots,\sigma_{n})\penalty\ ,\\
0\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

This follows from a direct inspection.
∎

Lemma 20
.

The following square diagram of graded
\mathbb S
-modules commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P})\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes s^{-1}s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{E},B(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}(\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\mathrm{Id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Proof.

The two maps from
s^{-1}s(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P})\otimes\mathbb S
to
\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
are morphisms of graded
\mathbb S
-modules that send
and element
s^{-1}s\rho((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}))\otimes p
to

\sum_{i}\rho((\sigma_{i},\ldots,\sigma_{n}))\otimes s^{-1}s\rho((\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{i-1}))^{\sigma_{i}\cdots\sigma_{n}}\otimes p.

∎

Lemma 21
.

Let us consider two maps

f_{1},f_{2}:s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\penalty\ .

Let us suppose that the following diagram commutes for
i\in\{1,2\}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}})\otimes\mathbb S.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\otimes d_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(f_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

Then the two assertions are equivalent

(1)

f_{1}=f_{2}
;

(2)

the restriction to
s^{-1}s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}
of
f_{1}
and

f_{2}
are equal.

Proof.

It is clear that (2) is a consequence of (1). Let us assume (2).
Noticing that the vertical map

\mathrm{Id}\otimes d_{\Delta}:\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathbb{T}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}})\otimes\mathbb S

is injective, the assertion (1) follows from a straightforward induction on the coradical filtration of
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
, that is, the number of nodes of the trees that make up
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
.
∎

2.10.
The quasi-planar filtration of quasi-planar cooperads

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad that is the colimit of a quasi-planar cooperad ladder
(\mathcal{C}^{(i)})_{i\in\alpha}
. We built in the previous subsection
2.8
a morphism of dg operads

\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C}

which induces a morphism of conilpotent curved cooperads

\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})

that is pseudo-planar in the sense that its to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
factors through
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{pl}}
, see Corollary
2
.

Lemma 22
.

Let us consider a cospan diagram in the category of pseudo-planar curved conilpotent cooperads

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{D}\xleftarrow{g}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}

such that both maps
f,g
are arity-wise degree-wise injective.
Then this diagram has a pullback
(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}},\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime})
. Moreover the
commutative square diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

is a pullback in the category of graded conilpotent cooperads and in the category of graded
\mathbb N
-modules and the commutative square diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

is a pullback both in the category of conilpotent curved cooperads and in the category of pdg
\mathbb S
-modules.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{pl}}
be the pullback intersection in the category of graded
\mathbb N
-modules of the cospan
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{pl}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{pl}}
.
As a direct consequence of the fact that the tensor product of graded
\Bbbk
-modules preserves intersections,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{pl}}
has the canonical structure of a graded conilpotent cooperads that makes it the pullback in the category of graded conilpotent cooperads of the same cospan.

Now, let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}
be the pullback in the category of conilpotent pdg cooperads of the cospan
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}
.
Since both functors

\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{\mathrm{conil}}\xrightarrow{-\otimes\mathbb S}\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}^{\mathrm{conil}}\leftarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathsf{Cooperads}^{\mathrm{conil}}

preserves limits, one has a canonical isomorphism of graded conilpotent cooperads
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}\simeq\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S
. In particular, the map
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is degree-wise injective. Subsequently
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}
is curved as
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is and thus it is a pullback in the category of curved conilpotent cooperads. The pair
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{pl}},\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime\prime})
is the expected pullback in the category of pseudo-planar curved conilpotent cooperads.
∎

Definition 38

(Quasi-planar filtration)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. For every integer
n\geq 0
, we define the
n
-th quasi-planar filtration
as the pair
(F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}},F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C})
given by the following pullback in the category of pseudo-planar curved conilpotent cooperads

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})\penalty\ .}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

This gives a diagram of pseudo-planar conilpotent curved cooperads

\mathcal{I}=F_{0}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow F_{1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\cdots

called the
quasi-planar filtration
of
\mathcal{C}
.

As described in Lemma
22
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

is a pullback both in the category of graded planar conilpotent cooperads and in the category of graded
\mathbb N
-modules and the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

is a pullback both in the category of conilpotent curved cooperads and in the category of conilpotent pdg cooperads and in the category of pdg
\mathbb N
-modules. In particular, the map
F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
is degree-wise injective as well as the map
F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
.

Proposition 15
.

The quasi-planar filtration of the quasi-planar curved conilpotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}
is a quasi-planar
\omega
-ladder whose colimit is
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

The fact that the diagram forms a quasi-planar ladder follows from the fact that the diagram

\tilde{F}_{0}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\tilde{F}_{1}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\cdots

is a quasi-planar
\omega
-ladder. The fact that its colimit is
\mathcal{C}
is a consequence of the fact that the colimt of the diagram just above is
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
and of the fact that pullbacks in graded
\mathbb N
-modules commute with directed colimits.
∎

Proposition 16
.

For every dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, the quasi-planar filtration cooperad ladder on
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
is canonically isomorphic to the quasi-planar
\omega
-ladder
\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
.

Proof.

Knowing that the map

\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}:s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})

proceeds from the structure of an Hadamard comonoid on the operad
\mathcal{E}
(see Proposition
14
), one can notice that the composite injective map

\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega B(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}))

factors through
\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}))
. We thus get the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\penalty\ ,}

where the bottom square is induced by the map

\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\penalty\ .

The fact that the map
\Delta_{\mathcal{E},\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}
proceeds from the structure of an Hadamard comonoid on the operad
\mathcal{E}
implies that the composite top-down endomorphism of
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
at the right of the square is the identity. Thus, the composite endomorphism on
the left of
\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
is also the identity.

To conclude, the map
F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\tilde{F}_{n}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
is both degree-wise surjective and injective and therefore it is a natural isomorphism.
∎

2.11.
The cofibrant resolution

Finally, for any dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, there is always a cofibrant replacement of the form
\Omega\mathcal{C}
, where
\mathcal{C}
is a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. This coincides a previous result for augmented dg operads, see
[
DV21
, Theorem 2, Proposition 17]
. A general version of this theorem for any
\mathbb{S}
-projective operad
\mathcal{P}
was also proven in
[
BM06
, Theorem 8.5.4]
.

Proposition 17
.

For every operad
\mathcal{P}
,
\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
is cofibrant and the canonical morphism

\psi:\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{P}

is an arity-wise quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

The fact that
\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
is cofibrant follows from the fact that
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
is quasi-planar and that
\Omega
sends quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperads to cofibrant dg operads.

Let us us show that the map
\psi:\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
is an arity-wise quasi-isomorphism. It suffices to show that the counit morphism
\epsilon:\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
is an arity-wise quasi-isomorphism. It admits a canonical section
s
in the category of dg
\mathbb S
-modules, given by the following composition:

\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\cong s^{-1}s(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow s^{-1}\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\penalty\ .

Let us denote
\pi_{\mathcal{P}}=s\circ\epsilon
. Since
\epsilon\circ s=\mathrm{Id}
, the counit
\epsilon
is an arity-wise quasi-isomorphism if and only if
\pi_{\mathcal{P}}
