is arity-wise quasi-isomorphism. So let us prove that
\pi_{\mathcal{P}}
is an arity-wise quasi-isomorphism.

Let us first assume that the unit map
\eta:\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
has a left inverse in the category of graded
\mathbb S
-modules. Let us denote
\mathcal{Q}
the graded
\mathbb S
-module defined as follows

\mathcal{Q}\coloneqq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathcal{P}\penalty\ .

The map
\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
also has a left inverse. We denote
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}
the kernel of this section.
Let us denote
\mathcal{C}
the following planar graded conilpotent cooperad

\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s\mathcal{Q}\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I})\cong\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s\overline{\mathcal{Q}}\oplus s\mathcal{I}\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I})\penalty\ .

We have a canonical isomorphism of graded operads

\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\simeq\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})\otimes\mathbb S\penalty\ .

Let
h
be the degree
1
endomorphism of
\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
given as follows.

(1)

On the trivial tree (with no node):

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow s^{-1}s^{2}\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\mapsto s^{-1}s^{2}1\penalty\ .

(2)

On planar trees with one node, it is zero.

(3)

On planar trees
t
with two nodes or more it is

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t(\overline{\mathcal{C}})\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}(n_{1})\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}(n_{2})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}F_{1}^{\mathrm{rad}}(\overline{\mathcal{C}})(n_{1})\otimes s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}(n_{2})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-2}F_{1}^{\mathrm{rad}}(\overline{\mathcal{C}})(n_{1})\otimes\overline{\mathcal{C}}(n_{2})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}(n_{1})\otimes\overline{\mathcal{C}}(n_{2})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}(n_{1}+n_{2}-1)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the third and the fourth map are given by

s^{-1}x\otimes s^{-1}y\otimes\cdots\otimes\{\sigma\}\mapsto-(-1)^{|x|}s^{-2}x\otimes y\otimes\cdots\otimes\{\sigma\}\mapsto-(-1)^{|x|}s^{-1}x\otimes y\otimes\cdots\otimes\{\sigma\}\penalty\ .

Let show that
\partial(h)+\pi_{\mathcal{P}}
is an isomorphism. We filter
\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
using the tree filtration relative to the quasi-planar filtration of
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
, with the difference that we define the first part not to be the trivial tree but
0
. This gives

\displaystyle F_{0}\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})

\displaystyle=0\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle F_{1}\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}\oplus s^{-1}(s(\mathcal{E}_{\leq 0}\otimes\mathcal{P})\oplus s^{2}\mathcal{I})\penalty\ .

This filtration is preserved by
h
,
\pi_{\mathcal{P}}
and the derivation. Moreover, the map on the associated graded object
\mathrm{gr}(\partial(h)+\pi_{\mathcal{P}})
is an isomorphism; so is
\partial(h)+\pi_{\mathcal{P}}
. This means that
\pi_{\mathcal{P}}
is an arity-wise quasi-isomorphism as it is chain homotopic to an arity-wise quasi-isomorphism.

Finally, if the unit
\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
does not admit a left inverse in the category of graded
\mathbb S
-modules, then
\mathcal{P}=0
and the result holds trivially.
∎

3.
Algebras, coalgebras, and Bar-Cobar adjunctions

In this section, we recall the various notions of (co)algebras over a (co)operads, as well as the bar-cobar adjunctions that interrelate them. Along the way, we develop some of their main categorical and algebraic properties. Finally, we review the different methods that can give a homotopical meaning to the categories of dg (co)algebras over an dg operad.

3.1.
From
\mathbb S
-modules to functors

The category of dg modules is enriched and tensored in dg
\mathbb S
-modules as follows.

(1)

For every dg modules
X,Y
, the mapping dg
\mathbb S
-module is
\mathrm{Mult}(X,Y)

given by

\mathrm{Mult}(X,Y)(n)\coloneqq[X^{\otimes n},Y].

In the case where
Y=X
, we set
\mathrm{End}(X)\coloneqq\mathrm{Mult}(X,X)
.

(2)

For every dg module
X
and every dg
\mathbb S
-module
M
, the tensorisation of
X
by
M
is just given by
M\circ X
as dg module, where
X
is considered as dg
\mathbb S
-modules concentrated in arity zero. Thus for every additional dg
\mathbb S
-module
N
, the structural map

(N\circ M)\circ X\longrightarrow N\circ(M\circ X)

is an isomorphism.

It is also enriched and cotensored in dg
\mathbb S
-modules as follows.

(1)

For every dg modules
X,Y
, the mapping dg
\mathbb S
-module is
\mathrm{coMult}(X,Y)

given by

\mathrm{coMult}(X,Y)(n)\coloneqq[X,Y^{\otimes n}].

In the case where
Y=X
, we set
\mathrm{coEnd}(X)\coloneqq\mathrm{coMult}(X,X)
.

(2)

For every dg module
X
and every dg
\mathbb S
-module
M
, the cotensorisation of
X
by
M
is
X^{M}
given by

X^{M}=\prod_{n\geq 0}\left[M(n),X^{\otimes n}\right]^{\mathbb S_{n}}\penalty\ .

For every additional dg
\mathbb S
-module
N
, the structural map

\varphi_{M,N}:\left(X^{M}\right)^{N}\longrightarrow X^{N\circ M}

is a degree-wise injection.

Similarly, dg modules are on the one hand enriched and tensored over dg
\mathbb N
-module through the bifunctors

X,Y\mapsto\mathrm{Mult}_{\mathrm{pl}}(X,Y)\coloneqq[X^{\otimes-},Y];\quad M,X\mapsto M\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}X

and they are on the other hand enriched and cotensored over dg
\mathbb N
-module through the bifunctors

X,Y\mapsto\mathrm{coMult}_{\mathrm{pl}}(X,Y)\coloneqq[X,Y^{\otimes-}];\quad X,M\mapsto X^{M}\prod_{n}[M(n),X^{\otimes n}]\penalty\ .

There are canonical isomorphisms of dg
\mathbb N
-modules
\mathrm{Mult}_{\mathrm{pl}}(-,-)\cong U_{\mathbb S}\mathrm{Mult}(-,-)
and
\mathrm{coMult}_{\mathrm{pl}}(-,-)\cong U_{\mathbb S}\mathrm{coMult}(-,-)
. We obtain by transposing along the adjunction two canonical natural isomorphisms

(M\otimes\mathbb S)\circ X\simeq M\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}X\penalty\ ,\quad X^{M\otimes\mathbb S}\simeq X^{M}\penalty\ ,

for every dg module
X
and every dg
\mathbb N
-module
M
.

Let us state some categorical properties that the constructions perfomed so far satisfy.

Lemma 23
.

Let
M
be a dg
\mathbb S
-module. The endofunctor
M\circ-
commutes with sifted colimits and the endofunctor
(-)^{M}
commutes with
\beta
-filtered colimits for a small regular cardinal
\beta
.

Proof.

The assertion about
M\circ-
can be deduced from the following facts

(1)

the tensor product
X\mapsto X^{\otimes n}
commutes with sifted colimits;

(2)

the functor
Y\mapsto M(n)\otimes_{\mathbb S}Y

commutes with colimits for every natural integer
n
;

(3)

coproducts commute with colimits.

Now, let
\beta
be a regular cardinal such that
\beta>\omega
and such that every
M(n)
, for every
n
in
\mathbb N
, is
\beta
-small. Then the endofunctor
(-)^{M}
commutes with
\beta
-filtered colimits since

(1)

the tensor product
X\mapsto X^{\otimes n}
commutes with sifted colimits, in particular
\beta
-filtered colimits;

(2)

the construction
Y\mapsto[M(n),Y]
commutes with

\beta
-filtered colimits;

(3)

the construction
Z\mapsto Z^{\mathbb S_{n}}
commutes with filtered colimits;

(4)

countable products commute with
\beta
-filtered colimits since
\beta>\omega
.

∎

Lemma 24
.

Let
M
be a dg
\mathbb S
-module. The endofunctor
(-)^{M}
commutes with
finite
cosifted limits.

Proof.

This can be deduced from the following facts:

(1)

the tensor product
X\mapsto X^{\otimes n}
commutes with finite cosifted limits,

(2)

the functor
Y\mapsto[M(n),Y]^{\mathbb S_{n}}
commutes, limits for every natural integer
n
;

(3)

products commute with limits.

∎

One can perform the same constructions with graded modules or pdg modules instead of dg modules. Moreover, these constructions commute strictly with the forgetful functors
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}\longrightarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}\longrightarrow\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}
.

Finally, let us notice that when a dg
\mathbb S
-module
M
is planar as a graded
\mathbb S
-module, one has extra properties on these endofunctors.

Lemma 25
.

Let
M
be a dg
\mathbb S
-module. Let us suppose that the underlying graded
\mathbb S
-module of
M
has the form
M_{\mathrm{pl}}\otimes\mathbb S
. Equivalently,
M(n)
is a quasi-free dg
\Bbbk[\mathbb S_{n}]
-module for all
n\geq 0
. Then

(1)

the endofunctor
(-)^{M}
commutes with
finite
sifted colimits;

(2)

the endofunctor
M\circ(-)
commutes with
finite
cosifted limits.

Proof.

(1)

For the endofunctor
(-)^{M}
, it is a consequence of the fact that the tensor product, the functors
[M_{\mathrm{pl}}(n),-]
and products commute with finite sifted colimits.

(2)

For the endofunctir
M\circ(-)
, it is a consequence of the fact that the tensor product, the functors
M_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)\otimes-
and coproducts commute with finite cosifted limits.

∎

Notation
.

Let
f:X\longrightarrow Y
be a map of degree
0
and
g:X\longrightarrow Y
be a map of degree
p
between graded modules
X,Y
. Let us consider

\shuffle_{n}(f,g)\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{n}f^{\otimes i-1}\otimes g\otimes f^{\otimes n-i}:X^{\otimes n}\longrightarrow Y^{\otimes n}\penalty\ ,

which is an
\mathbb{S}_{n}
-equivariant map of degree
p
. Let
M
be an graded
\mathbb{S}
-module.

(1)

It induces a map of degree
p

\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Id}_{M(n)}\otimes_{\mathbb S_{n}}\shuffle_{n}(f,g):\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}M(n)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{n}}X^{\otimes n}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}M(n)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{n}}Y^{\otimes n}\penalty\ .

By a slight abuse of notation, we denote this morphism by
M\circ\shuffle(f,g)
.

(2)

It induces a map of degree
p

\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathrm{Id}_{M(n)},\shuffle_{n}(f,g)]^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}:\prod_{n\geq 0}[M(n),X^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\longrightarrow\prod_{n\geq 0}[M(n),Y^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}

By a slight abuse of notation, this map will be denoted by
\shuffle(f,g)^{M}
.

3.2.
Algebras and coalgebras over (co)operads

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad and let
\mathcal{C}
be a dg cooperad. We recall the definitions of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra and dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra, as well as the definitions of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra and of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. See
[
GL22
, Section 3]
.

Definition 39

(dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra)
.

A dg
\mathcal{P}
-
algebra

A
amounts to the data of a dg module
(A,d_{A})
equipped with a morphism of dg operads
\Gamma_{A}:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}(A)
.

This data is equivalent to a structural map
\gamma_{A}:\mathcal{P}\circ A\longrightarrow A
that satisfies unitality and associativity conditions. In other words, it is equivalent to the data of an algebra over the monad
\mathcal{P}\circ-
.

Remark 29
.

More precisely, the data of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure on
(A,d_{A})
amounts to a collection of maps

\gamma_{A}^{n}:\mathcal{P}\otimes_{\mathbb S_{n}}A^{\otimes n}\longrightarrow A\penalty\ ,

for all
n\geq 0
, which satisfy compatibility conditions. Since we consider on the left-hand side
coinvariants
with respect to the action of
\mathbb S_{n}
, no divided power operations appear. The definition of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra encodes classical types of algebraic structures over a positive characteristic field. For instance, dg Lie-algebras, dg commutative algebras, dg Poisson algebras, etc.

Definition 40

(dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra)
.

A dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
V
amounts to the data of a dg module
(V,d_{V})
equipped with a morphism of dg operads
\delta_{V}:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathrm{coEnd}(V)
.

This data is equivalent to a structural map
\Delta_{V}:V\longrightarrow\displaystyle V^{\mathcal{P}}
such that the following diagrams commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V^{\mathcal{P}})^{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{P}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathcal{P}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Delta_{V})^{\mathrm{Id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id})^{\gamma}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id})^{\eta}}

where
\gamma:\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
and
\eta:\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
are, respectively, the composition morphism and the unit of the dg operad
\mathcal{P}
.

Remark 30
.

The data of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra structure on
(V,d_{V})
also amounts to a collection of maps

\Delta_{V}^{n}:V\longrightarrow[\mathcal{P}(n),V^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb S_{n}}\penalty\ ,

for all
n\geq 0
, which satisfy compatibility conditions. Since we consider on the right-hand side
invariants
with respect to the action of
\mathbb S_{n}
, no divided power operations appear. The definition of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra encodes classical types of coalgebraic structures,
without any conilpotency condition
, over a positive characteristic field. For instance, dg Lie-coalgebras, dg cocommutative coalgebras, dg Poisson coalgebras, etc.

Even though
(-)^{\mathcal{P}}
fails to be a comonad, the forgetful functor from dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras to dg modules is comonadic.

Theorem 3

(
[
Ane14
, Theorem 2.7.11]
)
.

The forgetful functor from dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras to dg modules is comonadic. The related comonad
L^{\mathcal{P}}(-)
is given, for a dg module
X
, by the following pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\mathcal{P}}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\mathcal{P}})^{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathcal{P}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathcal{P}}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id})^{\mathcal{P}}}

in the category of dg modules, where
\gamma:\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
is the composition map of
\mathcal{P}
.

The structure of a comonad on
L^{\mathcal{P}}
is given by the decomposition map
L^{\mathcal{P}}X\longrightarrow(X^{\mathcal{P}})^{\mathcal{P}}
, which factors through

L^{\mathcal{P}}L^{\mathcal{P}}X
, and by the following counit map
L^{\mathcal{P}}X\longrightarrow X^{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow X
.

Definition 41

(dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra)
.

A dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
W
amounts to the data of a dg module
(W,d_{W})
equipped with structural map
\Delta_{W}:W\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}\circ W
such that the following diagrams commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{C}\circ W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \Delta_{W}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}}

where
\Delta:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{C}
and
\epsilon:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
are, respectively, the decompositon map and the counit map of the cooperad
\mathcal{C}
. In other words, it is a coalgebra over the comonad
\mathcal{C}\circ-
.

Remark 31
.

The structural map of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
W

\Delta_{W}:W\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{C}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{n}}W^{\otimes n}\penalty\ ,

lands on the direct sum. Therefore any element
w
in
W
can only be decomposed into a
finite sum
. This implies that a dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras always satisfies some type
conilpotency
condition. Furthermore, the fact that
\Delta_{W}
lands on the
coinvariants
on the right-hand side implies that
divided power operations
will appear for this type of structures. Thus the definition of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra encodes divided powers conilpotent types of coalgebraic structures.

Definition 42

(dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra)
.

A dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra

\Lambda
amounts to the data of a dg module
(\Lambda,d_{\Lambda})
equipped with a structural morphism
\gamma_{\Lambda}:\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow\Lambda
such that the following diagrams commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}})^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{{\mathcal{C}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Id})^{\gamma_{\Lambda}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Id})^{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{B}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Id})^{\epsilon}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\Lambda}}

where
\Delta:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{C}
and
\epsilon:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
are, respectively, the decompositon morphism and the counit morphism of the cooperad
\mathcal{C}
. In other words, it is an algebra over the monad
(-)^{\mathcal{C}}
.

Remark 32
.

The structural map of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
\Lambda

\gamma_{\Lambda}:\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),\Lambda^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb S_{n}}\longrightarrow\Lambda\penalty\ ,

comes from an infinite product. Therefore any formal infinite sum of operations admits a well-defined image in
\Lambda

by definition
, without presupposing any topology. We refer to these phenomena as
absolute
algebraic structures. They generalize the notion of a
contramodule
, see
[
Pos21
]
for a precise definition of them. Furthermore, the presence of
invariants
on the left-hand side implies that these algebraic structures are also endowed with
divided powers
operations. Therefore the definition of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-dalgebra encodes divided powers absolute types of algebraic structures.

Remark 33

(Planar definitions)
.

One can also define the dg (co)algebras over a planar dg operad
\mathcal{P}
as the (co)algebras over the dg operad
\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathbb S
. And the dg (co)algebras over a dg planar cooperad
\mathcal{C}
as the (co)algebras over the dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathbb S
. In this case, no
divided powers
operations appear in dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras nor in dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proposition 18
.

The category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras is
\omega
-presentable.
The categories of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras, dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras and dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras are presentable.

Proof.

The monad
\mathcal{P}\circ-
and the comonad
\mathcal{C}\circ-
are
\omega
-accessible. The monad
(-)^{\mathcal{C}}
and the comonad
L^{\mathcal{P}}
are also accessible but for a larger small cardinal (
\aleph_{1}
). We conclude by the fact that for a regular small cardinal
\alpha
, the category of algebras over an
\alpha
-accessible monad in an
\alpha
-presentable category is
\alpha
-presentable. See
[
AR94
]
for more details. Moreover, the category of coalgebras over an accessible comonad in a presentable category is presentable, see
[
CR14
]
.
∎

One can consider the same definitions and perform the same constructions with graded modules or pdg modules instead of dg module. These constructions commute strictly with the forgetful functors
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}\longrightarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}\longrightarrow\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}
.

Curved algebras and coalgebras.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a curved cooperad. It is in particular a pdg cooperad, and one can define the categories of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras and of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Among these, the full subcategories of
curved
objects will be of particular interest.

Definition 43

(Curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a curved cooperad. A pdg coalgebra
(W,\Delta_{W},d_{W})
is
curved
if the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-d_{W}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}}

where
\theta
denotes the curvature of the cooperad
\mathcal{C}
.

The category
\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}
of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is the full subcategory of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras spanned by those which are curved.

Remark 34
.

If the curvature of
\mathcal{C}
is zero, then the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras in pdg modules is isomorphic to the category of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Definition 44

(Curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a curved cooperad. A pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
(\Lambda,\gamma_{\Lambda},d_{\Lambda})
is
curved
if the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\theta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\Lambda}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\Lambda}}

The category
\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}
of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is the full subcategory of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras spanned by those which are curved.

Remark 35
.

If the curvature of
\mathcal{C}
is zero, then the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras in pdg modules is isomorphic to the category of dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

3.3.
Categorical properties of the category of (co)algebras over a operad

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. Let us consider the following commutative diagram of forgetful functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}.}

All these categories are presentable. Moreover, all the functors are right adjoints because they preserve limits and filtered colimits. They are conservative and they also preserve sifted colimits. Therefore, all these forgetful functors are monadic.

Let us consider the following commutative diagram of forgetful functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}

All these categories are presentable. Moreover, all the functors are left adjoints since they preserve colimits. They are also conservative and preserve finite cosifted limits. Therefore, all the functors are comonadic.

3.4.
Categorical properties of coalgebras over a conilpotent curved cooperad

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent curved cooperad whose underlying graded conilpotent cooperad is the image through
-\otimes\mathbb S
of a graded planar conilpotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
.

Proposition 19
.

The forgetful functor from curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras to pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras admits a right adjoint denoted by
\mathrm{Curv}
. This gives an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Curv}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Hence the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras forms a coreflexive full subcategory of the category of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. In other words, they are coalgebras over an idempotent comonad in the category of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Proof.

Let
(W,\Delta_{W},d_{W})
be a pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra, we consider the following two maps of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{2}W\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\theta\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id})\Delta_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-d_{W}^{2}}

where
\theta:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{\mathcal{I}}
is the curvature of the cooperad
\mathcal{C}
. Let
Z_{W}
be the cofree pdg
\Bbbk
-module obtained from the graded
\Bbbk
module
s^{2}W
. Since a pdg
\Bbbk
-module is a graded
\Bbbk
-module together with a degree
-1
endomorphism,
Z_{W}
is given by
s^{2}W\otimes\Bbbk[\mathrm{u}]
, where
\mathrm{u}
is a generator in degree
-1
. From the above two maps, one gets a natural coreflexive pair of maps of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\circ Z_{W})\times W}

where the rightward maps are obtained from the previous diagram of graded
\Bbbk
-modules, using the forgetful-cofree adjunction between graded
\Bbbk
-modules and pdg
\Bbbk
-modules, and where the leftward map is just the projection onto
W
.

This pair has an equaliser
(H,\Delta_{H},d_{H})
in pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, which can be computed in graded
\Bbbk
-modules. In particular, the morphism of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras
H\longrightarrow W
is a degree-wise monomorphism. Furthermore, the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{2}H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{2}W\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\theta\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id})\Delta_{H}+d_{H}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\theta\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id})\Delta_{W}+d_{W}^{2}}

The map from
H
to
s^{2}W
is zero by the universal property of
H
. Since the bottom map is a monomorphism, the left map is also zero. So
H
is curved. We set
\mathrm{Curv}(W)\coloneqq H
, and it is straightforward to show that it defines a right adjoint to the forgetful functor.
∎

Corollary 3
.

The category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is the category of coalgebra over an idempotent comonad over pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras which is accessible and preserves coreflexive equalisers. Hence, the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is presentable.

Proof.

Let
W
be a pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra. Its image by the functor
\mathrm{Curv}
is also given by the coreflexive equaliser of a the pair of maps of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ((\mathcal{C}\circ W)\oplus Z_{W})\penalty\ .}

This coreflexive equaliser is constructed from the one that defines the functor
\mathrm{Curv}
and from the one that gives
W
as a pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra. The comonad that defines curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras preserves finite cosifted limits since its composition with the the forgetful functor to graded
\Bbbk
-modules does. Indeed, we have that:

(1)

the forgetful functor preserves and creates finite cosifted limits;

(2)

the constructions
X\mapsto\mathcal{C}\circ X
and
W\mapsto\mathcal{C}\circ((\mathcal{C}\circ W)\oplus Z_{W})
preserves finite cosifted limits;

(3)

then the construction
W\mapsto\lim(W\rightrightarrows\mathcal{C}\circ W\oplus Z_{W})
preserves finite cosifted limits.

To prove that this comonad preserves filtered colimits, it suffices to prove that its composition with the forgetful functor towards pdg
\Bbbk
-modules preserves filtered colimits. This follows from:

(1)

the forgetful functor preserves and creates filtered colimits and coreflexive equalisers;

(2)

the constructions
W\mapsto\mathcal{C}\circ W

and
W\mapsto\mathcal{C}\circ((\mathcal{C}\circ W)\oplus Z_{W})
preserve filtered colimits;

(3)

filtered colimits and coreflexive equalisers commute in graded
\Bbbk
-modules.

∎

Let us consider the following commutative diagram of forgetful functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}\penalty\ .}

All the categories are presentable and all the functors are left adjoint (since they preserve colimits), conservative and they also preserve coreflexive equalisers. Therefore all the functors are comonadic.

3.5.
Curved algebras over a conilpotent curved cooperad

Again, let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent curved cooperad whose underlying graded conilpotent cooperad is the image through
-\otimes\mathbb S
of a graded planar conilpotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
.

Proposition 20
.

The forgetful functor from curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras to pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras admits a left adjoint denoted by
\mathrm{Curv}
. This gives an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Curv}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Hence the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras forms a reflexive full subcategory of the category of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. In other words, they are algebras over an idempotent monad in the category of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proof.

Let
(\Lambda,\gamma_{\Lambda},d_{\Lambda})
be a pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. We consider the two maps of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-2}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\Lambda}\penalty\ \Lambda^{\theta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{2}_{\Lambda}}

where
\theta:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{\mathcal{I}}
is the curvature of the cooperad
\mathcal{C}
. Let
Z_{\Lambda}
be the free pdg
\Bbbk
-module obtained from the graded
\Bbbk
module
s^{-2}\Lambda
. Since a pdg
\Bbbk
-module is a graded
\Bbbk
-module together with a degree
-1
endomorphism,
Z_{\Lambda}
is given by
s^{-2}\Lambda\otimes\Bbbk[\mathrm{u}]
, where
\mathrm{u}
is a generator in degree
-1
. From the above two maps, one gets a natural reflexive pair of maps of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda\sqcup(Z_{\Lambda})^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}

where the leftward maps are obtained from the previous diagram of graded
\Bbbk
-modules, using the free-forgetful adjunction between graded
\Bbbk
-modules and pdg
\Bbbk
-modules, and where the rightward map is just the inclusion of
\Lambda
.

This pair has a coequaliser
(\Xi,\gamma_{\Xi},d_{\Xi})
in the category of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras, which can be computed in graded
\Bbbk
-modules. In particular, the morphism of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras
\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow\Xi
is a degree-wise epimorphism. Furthermore, the following square commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-2}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\Xi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\Lambda}\penalty\ \Lambda^{\theta}-d^{2}_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\Xi}\penalty\ \Xi^{\theta}-d^{2}_{\Xi}}

The map from
s^{-2}\Lambda
to
\Xi
is zero by the universal property of
\Xi
. Since the bottom map is an epimorphism, the left map is also zero. So
\Xi
is curved. We set
\mathrm{Curv}(W)\coloneqq\Xi
, and it is straightforward to show that it defines a left adjoint to the forgetful functor.
∎

Corollary 4
.

The category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is the category of algebras over idempotent monad over pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras which is accessible and preserves reflexive coequalisers. Hence, the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is presentable.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda
be a pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. Its image by the functor
\mathrm{Curv}
is also given by the reflexive coequaliser of a the pair of maps of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Z_{\Lambda}\oplus\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}})^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}.}

This reflexive coequaliser is constructed from the one that defines the functor
\mathrm{Curv}
and from the one that gives
\Lambda
as a pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. This monad preserves finite sifted colimits since its composition with the forgetful functor towards graded
\Bbbk
-modules does. Indeed, we have that:

(1)

this forgetful functor preserves and creates finite sifted colimits;

(2)

the constuction
X\mapsto X^{\mathcal{C}}
preserves finite sifted colimits;

(3)

the construction
\Lambda\mapsto Z_{\Lambda}
preserves finite sifted colimits;

(4)

then the construction
\Lambda\mapsto\varinjlim((Z_{\Lambda}\oplus\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}})^{\mathcal{C}}\rightrightarrows\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}})
preserves finite sifted colimits.

Let us prove that this monad is accessible. Let
\beta
be a small regular cardinal so that the monad
(-)^{\mathcal{C}}
is
\beta
-accessible. To prove that the idempotent "curvature" monad preserves
\beta
-filtered colimits, it suffices to prove that its composition with the forgetful functor towards pdg
\Bbbk
-modules perserves these colimits. This follows from the following facts:

(1)

the forgetful functor preserves and creates reflexive coequalisers and
\beta
-filtered colimits;

(2)

the constructions
\Lambda\mapsto\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}
and
\Lambda\mapsto\mathcal{C}\circ((\mathcal{C}\circ\Lambda)\oplus Z_{\Lambda})
preserve filtered colimits;

(3)

reflexive coequalisers and
\beta
-filtered colimits commute in pdg
\Bbbk
-modules.

∎

Let us consider the following commutative diagram of forgetful functors:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}\penalty\ .}

All the categories are presentable. Moreover all the functors are right adjoint (they are accessible and preserve limits), conservative and they also preserve finite sifted colimits. Therefore they are monadic.

3.6.
Complete algebras over the canonical ladder of a quasi-planar cooperad

This section is the mirror of
[
GL22
, Section 4.4]
in any characteristic for a quasi-planar cooperad ladder equipped with its canonical quasi-planar filtration. For the rest of this section, we fix a quasi-planar curved conilpotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}
.

Lemma 26
.

Let
n
be a natural integer and let
X
be a pdg
\Bbbk
module. Let us consider the inclusion
i_{n}:F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{C}
. The natural map

(\mathrm{Id})^{i_{n}}:X^{\mathcal{C}}\twoheadrightarrow X^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}

is a degree-wise epimorphism. Subsequently, the forgetful functor from pdg
F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}
-algebras to pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is fully faithful.

Proof.

In the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules, this map may be rewritten as

X^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\twoheadrightarrow X^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}

Moreover, the map of graded
\mathbb N
-module
F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
has a left inverse. Therefore the above map has a section. It implies that it is a degree-wise epimorphism. We thus get a morphism of monads that is object-wise an epimorphism. Therefore the related right adjoint forgetful functor is fully faithful.
∎

For every natural integer
n
, we denote by

F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}:\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\longrightarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}

be the functor that is left adjoint to the forgetful functor. It sends every pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
(B,\gamma_{B},d_{B})
to the coequaliser of the following pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}})^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\gamma_{\Lambda})^{\mathrm{Id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Id})^{\Delta}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ (\mathrm{Id})^{i_{n}}}

This coequalizer is also the pushout of the span

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Id})^{i_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\Lambda}}

Both of these colimits can be computed in the category of pdg
F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}
-algebras or in the underlying category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules.

This gives a natural diagram

\Lambda\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\longrightarrow F^{n-1}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\longrightarrow\cdots F^{1}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\longrightarrow F^{0}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\penalty\ ,

where we set
F^{-1}_{\mathrm{qp}}(\Lambda)=0
. For every natural integer
n
in
\mathbb{N}
, the
associated graded
is given by

\mathrm{gr}^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\coloneqq\mathrm{Ker}\left(F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}(\Lambda)\longrightarrow F^{n-1}_{\mathrm{qp}}(\Lambda)\right)\penalty\ .

Definition 45

(qp-completion functor)
.

The
qp-completion functor
, denoted by
\widehat{(-)}
, is the endofunctor of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras that sends an algebra
\Lambda
to the limit

\widehat{\Lambda}\coloneqq\lim_{n\in\omega^{\mathrm{op}}}F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}(\Lambda)\penalty\ .

Definition 46

(qp-complete pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra)
.

A pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
\Lambda
is called
qp-complete
if the canonical morphism
\Lambda\longrightarrow\widehat{\Lambda}
is an isomorphism.

We denote
\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}
the full subcategory of that of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras spanned by the qp-complete ones.

Lemma 27

(After
[
GL22
, Lemma 4.22]
)
.

Let
f:\Lambda\longrightarrow\Gamma
be a morphism of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Let us suppose that for every natural integer
n
, the induced map

\mathrm{gr}^{n}(f):\mathrm{gr}^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{gr}^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Gamma

is a degree-wise epimorphism.

(1)

For every
n
, the map

F^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\times_{F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Gamma}F^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Gamma

is a degree-wise epimorphism.

(2)

The map
\widehat{f}:\widehat{\Lambda}\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{\Gamma}
is a degree-wise epimorphism.

Proof.

Let us prove the first assertion. Let us consider the square diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{n}}

Given an element
(y,z)\in F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\times_{F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Gamma}F^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Gamma

one can find
x^{\prime}
so that
p_{n}(x^{\prime})=y
. Then
q_{n}(z-F^{n+1}_{rad}(f)(x^{\prime}))=0
. In other words

z-F^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}(f)(x^{\prime})\in\mathrm{gr}^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Gamma\penalty\ .

Let
x^{\prime\prime}
be one of its antecedent in
\mathrm{gr}^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda
. Then
x=x^{\prime}+x^{\prime\prime}
is an antecedent of
(y,z)
. This proves the first point.

Let us prove the second point. Let
z
be a degree
k
element of
\widehat{\Lambda}
. It is a sequence

(z_{n})_{n\in\mathbb N}\in\prod_{n}F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}
, where each
z_{n}
is the image of
z_{n+1}
via the projection
F^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}(\widehat{\Lambda})\twoheadrightarrow F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}(\widehat{\Lambda})
.
Using

(1)

the fact that the map
\mathrm{gr}^{0}(f)

is a degree-wise epimorphism;

(2)

the first assertion of the lemma;

(3)

the axiom of choice;

one can build a sequence
x=(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb N}\in\prod_{n}F_{\mathrm{qp}}^{n}\Lambda_{k}
, where each
x_{n}
is the image of
x_{n+1}
via the projection
F^{n+1}_{\mathrm{qp}}(\Lambda)\twoheadrightarrow F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}(\Lambda)
and such that
F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}(f)(x_{n})=z_{n}
. Thus
x
is an antecedent of
z
in
\widehat{\Lambda}
.
∎

Lemma 28

(After
[
GL22
, Proposition 4.23]
)
.

The completion endofunctor preserves degree-wise epimorphisms.

Proof.

Let
f:\Lambda\longrightarrow\Gamma
be a degree-wise epimorphism.
In order to show that
\widehat{f}
is a degree-wise epimorphism, it suffices using Lemma
27
to prove that
\mathrm{gr}^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}(f)
is a degree-wise epimorphism for every natural integer
n
.

Infinite products in graded
\Bbbk
-modules are exact, the contravariant functor
[-,X]
for every
X
is also exact, therefore the sequence

0\longrightarrow\Lambda^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}/F_{n-1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{F_{n-1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longrightarrow 0

is exact. Thus, the following commutative diagram of graded
\Bbbk
-modules is a pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}/F_{n-1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{F_{n-1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{n-1}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda.}

Therefore the kernel
\mathrm{gr}^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda
is the image in
F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda
of the map
\Lambda^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}/F_{n-1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda
. Let us consider the following commutative diagram of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}/F_{n-1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}/F_{n-1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Gamma.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}/F_{n-1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}(f)}

Since the top map and the right map are degree-wise epimorphisms, so is bottom map, which proves the result.
∎

Lemma 29

(After
[
GL22
, Proposition 4.21 and 4.24]
)
.

(1)

The
X
be a pdg module
X
, the free pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
X^{\mathcal{C}}
is qp-complete.

(2)

The natural map

\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{\Lambda}

is an degree-wise epimorphism for every dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
\Lambda
.

Proof.

For every free pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
X^{\mathcal{C}}
on a pdg module
X
, one has

F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}X^{\mathcal{C}}=X^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}
and that it is complete:

\widehat{X^{\mathcal{C}}}=\lim_{n\in\omega^{\mathrm{op}}}X^{F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}\cong X^{\mathcal{C}}.

Now, let
\Lambda
be any pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. We consider the following commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Lambda}.}

The top horizontal map is an isomorphism and the right vertical map is a degree-wise epimorphism by Lemma
28
. The map from
\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}
to
\Lambda
is also epimorphism. Therefore so is the map
\Lambda\longrightarrow\widehat{\Lambda}
.
∎

Lemma 30
.

The canonical map

\widehat{\Lambda}\longrightarrow\widehat{\widehat{(\Lambda)}}

is an isomorphism. Therefore the completion functor equipped with the natural map
\Lambda\longrightarrow\widehat{\Lambda}
is an idempotent monad in the category of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proof.

For every natural integer
n
, both maps

\Lambda\longrightarrow\widehat{\Lambda}\longrightarrow F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda
are degree-wise epimorphisms. Applying the left adjoint functor
F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}
which preserves degree-wise epimorphisms, one gets a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}

The first map is both a degree-wise epimorphism and a degree-wise monomorphism. Hence it is an isomorphism and so is the second map. Taking the limit over
n\in\omega^{\mathrm{op}}
, one gets the required isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 21
.

The category of qp-complete pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra is presentable.

Proof.

It is a reflexive full subcategory of the category of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras which is presentable. The reflector is the completion
\Lambda\mapsto\widehat{\Lambda}
. One can find a small regular cardinal
\beta
so that every functor
F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}
is
\beta
-accessible and so that
\beta
-filtered colimits commute with limits of diagrams indexed by
\omega^{op}
. Then, the completion functor is
\beta
-accessible. Hence the category of qp-complete pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is presentable.
∎

Remark 36
.

The category of qp-complete pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra is the homotopy limit of the (pseudo) diagram of categories

\cdots\longrightarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\longrightarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ F_{n-1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ F_{1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\longrightarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ F_{0}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}.

It is presentable as a small limit of presentable categories and left adjoint functors.

Remark 37
.

The analogue definitions and results are valid for dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras or graded
\mathcal{C}
-algebras as well.

3.7.
Categorical properties of qp-complete curved algebras over a quasi-planar cooperad

Proposition 22
.

The qp-completion idempotent monad on pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras preserves curved algebras. Moreover, the restricted monad (to curved algebras) is accessible. Hence, the category of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is presentable and the forgetful functor towards curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is accessible.

Proof.

For every curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
\Lambda
,
\widehat{\Lambda}
is also curved since the map
\Lambda\longrightarrow\widehat{\Lambda}
is a degree-wise epimorphism. Therefore, the qp-completion monad restricts to curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Let
\beta
be regular small cardinal such that

(1)

the forgetful functor
\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\longrightarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}
is a
\beta
-accessible functor between
\beta
-accessible categories;

(2)

the completion monad on
\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}
is
\beta
-accessible.

Then, the restriction of this monad to curved algebras is also
\beta
-accessible.
∎

Remark 38
.

Let us consider the following
commutative diagram of functors.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}

The vertical arrows are monadic and accessible but they do not preserve reflexive coequalisers in general; otherwise all
\mathcal{C}
-algebras would be qp-complete.

3.8.
Naturality of (co)algebras of a dg operad.

The definitions of algebras and coalgebras are compatible with morphisms of operads. This will mean that any such morphism induces an adjunction between the appropriate categories. Let
f:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
be a morphism of dg operads.

Naturality of algebras.
The morphism
f
induces a morphism of monads
\mathcal{P}\circ-\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}\circ-
, which yields an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

since the category of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebras is cocomplete, by the adjoint lifting theorem. See Appendix
A.1
for more details. Since the monad
\mathcal{Q}\circ-
commutes with sifted colimits, the left adjoint functor
f_{!}
sends a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
to the reflexive coequaliser of the pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}\circ(\mathcal{P}\circ A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}\circ A\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \gamma_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}\penalty\ (f\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id})}

between the structural map
\gamma_{A}
of
A
and the map induced by the morphism
f
composed with the monad structure
\gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}\circ-
of
\mathcal{Q}\circ-
. This is a coequalizer both in dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebras and in graded
\Bbbk
-modules.

Naturality of coalgebras.
The morphism
f
also induces a morphism of comonads
L^{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow L^{\mathcal{Q}}
, which yields an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

since the category of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebras is complete, again using the adjoint lifting theorem. Since the comonad
L^{\mathcal{Q}}
commutes with
finite
cosifted limits, the functor
f_{!}
sends a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
V
to the coreflexive equaliser of the pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\mathcal{Q}}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\mathcal{Q}}L^{\mathcal{P}}V\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{f}(\mathrm{Id})\penalty\ \omega_{\mathcal{Q}}(V)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}\penalty\ (f\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id})}

between the structure map
\Delta_{V}
of
V
and the map given by the comonad structure
\omega_{\mathcal{Q}}
of
L^{\mathcal{Q}}
composed with the morphism induced by
f
. This is an equalizer both in dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebra and in graded
\Bbbk
-modules.

3.9.
Naturality of (co)algebras over a cooperad.

The definitions of algebras and coalgebras are compatible with morphisms of of cooperads. This will mean that any such morphism induces an adjunction between the appropriate categories. Let
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}
be two conilpotent curved cooperads whose underlying graded conilpotent cooperads are the image through
-\otimes\mathbb S
of a graded planar conilpotent cooperads respectively
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}
. Let
g:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a morphisms of curved cooperads.

Naturality of coalgebras.
The map
g
induces a morphism of comonads on the category of pdg
\Bbbk
-modules
\mathcal{C}\circ-\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}\circ-
, which yields an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

by the adjoint lifting theorem. Since the comonad
\mathcal{D}\circ-
preserves finite sifted limits, the functor
g^{!}
sends a pdg
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
W
to the coreflexive equaliser of the pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{D}\circ W\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \Delta_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id})\penalty\ \Delta_{\mathcal{C}}(W)}

between the structure map
\Delta_{W}
of
W
and the map given by the comonad structure
\Delta_{\mathcal{C}}(-)
of
\mathcal{C}\circ-
composed with the morphism induced by
g
. The existence of this adjunction and the fact that this equaliser can be computed in graded
\Bbbk
-modules follows from Lemma
25
.

Proposition 23
.

The adjunction
g_{*}\dashv g^{!}
restrict to an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

relating curved
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebras and curved
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebras.

Proof.

The fact that
g_{*}
sends curved objects to curved objects is clear. Now, given a curved
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
W
, the squared coderivation
d^{2}
on
\mathcal{C}\circ W
is

\displaystyle d^{2}

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\circ\mathrm{Id}_{W}+\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\shuffle(\mathrm{Id}_{W},d_{W}^{2})

\displaystyle=-((\theta_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}})\Delta_{\mathcal{C}})\circ\mathrm{Id}_{W}+((\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\shuffle(\epsilon_{\mathcal{C}},\theta_{\mathcal{C}}))\Delta_{\mathcal{C}})\circ\mathrm{Id}_{W}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\shuffle(\mathrm{Id}_{W},(\theta_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\mathrm{Id}_{W})\Delta_{W})\penalty\ ,

where
\upsilon_{\mathcal{C}}
denotes the counit of the cooperad
\mathcal{C}
. If we denote
j
the inclusion
g^{!}W\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}\circ W
, then
the pre-composition of
j
with the sum

((\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\shuffle(\epsilon_{\mathcal{C}},\theta_{\mathcal{C}}))\Delta_{\mathcal{C}})\circ\mathrm{Id}_{W}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\shuffle(\mathrm{Id}_{W},(\theta_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\mathrm{Id}_{W})\Delta_{W})

is zero. Thus,

jd^{2}=d^{2}j=-((\theta_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}})\Delta_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\mathrm{Id}_{W})j=-j((\theta_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\mathrm{Id}_{g^{!}_{W}})\Delta_{g^{!}_{W}})\penalty\ .

Since
j
is injective,
g^{!}_{W}
is curved.
∎

Remark 39
.

Actually, when dealing with coalgebras, we do not need
\mathcal{C}
to proceed from a planar cooperad. We will need it in the context of algebras, in order to be able to talk about complete
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Naturality of algebras.
The morphism
g
induces a morphism of monads on pdg
\Bbbk
-modules
(-)^{\mathcal{D}}\longrightarrow(-)^{\mathcal{C}}
, which yields an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

by the adjoint lifting theorem. The functor
g^{!}
sends a curved
\mathcal{D}
-algebra
\Lambda
to the reflexive coequaliser of the pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Lambda^{\mathcal{D}})^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\gamma_{\Lambda})^{(\mathrm{Id})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Lambda)^{\Delta_{\mathcal{C}}}\penalty\ (\Lambda)^{g}}

between the structure map
\gamma_{\Lambda}
of
\Lambda
and the map induced by the morphism
g
composed with the monad structure
(-)^{\Delta_{\mathcal{C}}}
of
(-)^{\mathcal{C}}
. The existence of this adjunction and the fact that this coequaliser can be computed in graded
\Bbbk
-modules follows from Lemma
25
.

Proposition 24
.

The adjunction
g^{!}\dashv g^{*}
restrict to an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

relating curved
\mathcal{D}
-algebras and curved
\mathcal{D}
-algebras.

Proof.

This follows from dual arguments as those used to prove Proposition
24
. The fact that
g_{*}
sends curved algebras to curved algebras is clear. Now, given a curved
\mathcal{D}
-algebra
\Lambda
, the squared coderivation
d^{2}
on
\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}\simeq\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
is

\displaystyle d^{2}

\displaystyle=-\Lambda^{d_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}}+\shuffle(\mathrm{Id}_{\Lambda},d_{\Lambda}^{2})^{\mathcal{C}}\penalty\ .

It is thus the sum of the three maps

\displaystyle\delta^{(1)}:\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{\theta_{\mathcal{C}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathrm{Id}}}\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}};

\displaystyle\delta^{(2)}:\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{-\Lambda^{\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\shuffle(\upsilon_{\mathcal{C}},\theta_{\mathcal{C}})}}\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}};

\displaystyle\delta^{(3)}:\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\shuffle(\Lambda^{\upsilon_{\mathcal{D}}},\Lambda^{\theta_{\mathcal{C}}})^{\mathcal{C}}}(\Lambda^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}})^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}}}\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}},

where
\upsilon_{\mathcal{C}},\upsilon_{\mathcal{D}}
are the counits of the cooperads
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}
. Since the curvature on cooperads in zero on arities different from
1
and since the morphism of cooperads
g
commutes with the curvatures and the counits, the map

\Lambda^{\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\shuffle(\upsilon_{\mathcal{C}},\theta_{\mathcal{C}})}:\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}

is equal to the composition

\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\xrightarrow{\shuffle(\Lambda^{\upsilon},\Lambda^{\theta})^{\mathcal{C}}}(\Lambda^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}})^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\xrightarrow{(\Lambda^{g})^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}(\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}})^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\hookrightarrow\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}.

As a consequence, if we denote
p
the projection
p:\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\twoheadrightarrow g^{!}(\Lambda)
, then

p(\delta^{(2)}+\delta^{(3)})=0\penalty\ .

The map

\Lambda^{\theta_{\mathcal{C}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathrm{Id}}:\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}

is equal to the composition

\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\xrightarrow{(\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}})^{\theta_{\mathcal{C}}}}(\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}})^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\hookrightarrow\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\penalty\ .

Thus
\delta^{(1)}=\gamma_{\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}(\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}})^{\theta_{\mathcal{C}}}

and therefore

d^{2}p=pd^{2}=p\delta^{(1)}=p\gamma_{\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}(\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}})^{\theta_{\mathcal{C}}}=\gamma_{g^{!}(\Lambda)}(g^{!}(\Lambda))^{\theta_{\mathcal{C}}}p\penalty\ .

Since
p
is surjective, this entails that

d^{2}=\gamma_{g^{!}(\Lambda)}(g^{!}(\Lambda))^{\theta_{\mathcal{C}}}\penalty\ ,

which proves the result.
∎

Proposition 25
.

Let
g:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a quasi-planar morphism of quasi-planar curved conilpotent cooperads. Then the functor
g_{*}
sends qp-complete
\mathcal{C}
-algebras to qp-complete
\mathcal{D}
-algebras.

Proof.

Let us consider the following commutative square diagram of quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperads

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

for every
n\geq 0
. Let
\Lambda
be a qp-complete pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. The

\mathcal{D}
-algebra
g_{*}(F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda)
rewrites as

g_{*}(F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda)=g_{*}i^{*}i_{!}(\Lambda)\cong j^{*}(F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}g)^{*}i_{!}(\Lambda)\penalty\ .

Thus
g_{*}(F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda)
is qp-complete as it is the image of a pdg
F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{D}
-algebra. Since
g_{*}
preserves limits and since
\Lambda
is complete, the map

g_{*}(\Lambda)\longrightarrow\lim_{n\in\omega^{\mathrm{op}}}g_{*}(F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda)

is an isomorphism. So
g_{*}(\Lambda)
is complete as it is the limit of a diagram of complete algebras.
∎

Proposition 26
.

Let
g:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a quasi-planar morphism of curved cooperads. There is an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{g}^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras and qp-complete curved
\mathcal{D}
-algebras, where the left adjoint
\widehat{g}^{!}
is given by
g^{!}
post-composed with the completion functor of
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
24
, using the fact that the completion functor of
\mathcal{C}
-algebras preserves curved objects.
∎

Embedding of cooperads.
Let us assume that
g:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
is a arity-wise degree-wise injection. Since the underlying graded
\mathbb S
-module of
\mathcal{C}
is cofree and thus injective, we get a section of graded
\mathbb S
-modules
p:\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
that is left inverse to
g
.

As a consequence, the morphism of comonads on pdg
\Bbbk
-modules
\mathcal{C}\circ-\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}\circ-
is object-wise a monomorphism and the morphism of monads on pdg
\Bbbk
-modules
(-)^{\mathcal{D}}\longrightarrow(-)^{\mathcal{C}}
is object-wise an epimorphism. This implies that the functors

\displaystyle g_{*}:

\displaystyle\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\longrightarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}

\displaystyle g_{*}:

\displaystyle\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\longrightarrow\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}

are fully faithful. Therefore the first
g_{*}
is the forgetful functor related to an idempotent comonad and the second
g_{*}
is the forgetful functor related to an idempotent monad. In this case, their respective adjoint functors admit an easy description in terms of pullbacks and pushouts.

Lemma 31
.

For every pdg
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
(W,\Delta_{W},d_{W})
,
g^{!}(W)
is given by the following pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{!}(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}\circ W.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{W}}

in the category of pdg
\Bbbk
-modules.

Proof.

Follows directly from the dual of Proposition
71
.
∎

Lemma 32
.

For every pdg
\mathcal{D}
-algebra
(\Lambda,\gamma_{\Lambda},d_{\Lambda})
,
g^{!}(\Lambda)
is given by the following pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{!}(\Lambda).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{g}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

in the category of pdg
\Bbbk
-modules.

Proof.

Follows directly from Proposition
71
.
∎

3.10.
The Bar-Cobar adjunction

Koszul duality between dg operads and conilpotent curved cooperads is encoded by the notion of a curved twisting morphism, see
[
Gri21
]
or
[
RiL22b
]
. Any curved twisting morphism
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
between a conilpotent curved cooperad and a dg operad induces a bar-cobar adjunction between the categories of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

The bar construction relative to
\alpha
.
Given a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
(A,\gamma_{A},d_{A})
, one can construct a curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
\mathrm{B}_{\alpha}A
called the bar construction. The underlying graded
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra of
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
is given by
\mathcal{C}\circ A
. It is equipped with the unique coderivation whose projection onto the generators is the sum of the following maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}:\mathcal{C}\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{A}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}:\mathcal{C}\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{A}}

One can check that the pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
\mathrm{B}_{\alpha}A
is in fact a curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra.

The cobar construction relative to
\alpha
.
Given a curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
(W,\Delta_{W},d_{W})
, one can construct a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
\Omega_{\alpha}W
called de cobar construction. The underlying graded
\mathcal{P}
-algebra is given by
\mathcal{P}\circ W
. It is equipped with the unique derivation whose restriction to the generators is the sum of the maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}:W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\circ W\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{W}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}:W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\circ W\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1).(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}}

One can check that the pdg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
\Omega_{\alpha}W
is in fact a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra.

Definition 47

(Twisting morphism)
.

Let
(W,\Delta_{W},d_{W})
be a curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra and let
(A,\gamma_{A},d_{A})
be a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. A
twisting morphism relative to
\alpha
is the data of a graded morphism
\lambda:W\longrightarrow A
which satisfies the following equation

\gamma_{A}\penalty\ (\alpha\circ\lambda)\penalty\ \Delta_{W}+d_{A}\penalty\ \lambda-\lambda\penalty\ d_{W}=0\penalty\ .

We denote the set of twisting morphisms between
W
and
A
by
\mathrm{Tw}^{\alpha}(W,A)
.

Proposition 27
.

Let
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
be a curved twisting morphism between a conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
. There are natural isomorphisms

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}(\Omega_{\alpha}W,A)\cong\mathrm{Tw}^{\alpha}(W,A)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}(W,\mathrm{B}_{\alpha}A)\penalty\ ,

for any curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
W
and any dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
. This gives the bar-cobar adjunction relative to
\alpha

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between the categories of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. Furthermore,

Proof.

These constructions can be found in
[
LG19
, Section 4.3]
. The case where
\mathcal{C}
is a dg cooperad can be found in
[
LV12
, Section 11]
.
∎

3.11.
The complete Bar-Cobar adjunction

Any curved twisting morphism
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
between a conilpotent curved cooperad and a dg operad also induces a complete bar-cobar adjunction between the categories of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras and of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

The complete cobar construction.
Given a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
(V,\Delta_{V},d_{V})
, one can construct a curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
\widehat{\Omega}_{\alpha}V
called the complete cobar construction. The underlying graded
\mathcal{C}
-algebra is given by
V^{\mathcal{C}}
. It is equipped with the unique derivation whose restriction to the generators is the sum of the maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}:V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{C}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{V}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}:V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{C}}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1).(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Id})^{\alpha}}

One can check that the pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
\widehat{\Omega}_{\alpha}V
is in fact a qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra.

The complete bar construction.
Given a curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
(\Lambda,\gamma_{\Lambda},d_{\Lambda})
, one can construct a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}\Lambda
called the complete bar construction. The underlying graded
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra is given by
L^{\mathcal{P}}\Lambda
. It is equipped with the unique coderivation whose projection onto the generators is the sum of the maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}:L^{\mathcal{P}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\Lambda}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}:L^{\mathcal{P}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Id})^{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{B}}

Remark 40
.

The cofree
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra functor
L^{\mathcal{P}}
behaves well with respect to coderivations, which can be induced simply by a graded map onto the cogenerators. We refer to
[
GL22
, Section 6.5]
for these type of results.

Definition 48

(Twisting morphism)
.

Let
(\Lambda,\gamma_{\Lambda},d_{\Lambda})
be a curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra and let
(C,\Delta_{C},d_{C})
be a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra. A
twisting morphism relative to
\alpha
is the data of a graded morphism
\lambda:C\longrightarrow\Lambda
which satisfies the following equation

\gamma_{\Lambda}\penalty\ (\lambda)^{\alpha}\penalty\ \Delta_{C}+d_{\Lambda}\penalty\ \lambda-\lambda\penalty\ d_{C}=0\penalty\ .

We denote the set of twisting morphisms between
C
and
\Lambda
by
\mathrm{Tw}^{\alpha}(C,\Lambda)
.

Proposition 28
.

Let
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
be a curved twisting morphism between a conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
. There are natural isomorphisms

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}}\left(C,\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}\Lambda\right)\cong\mathrm{Tw}^{\alpha}(C,\Lambda)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}\left(\widehat{\Omega}_{\alpha}C,\Lambda\right)\penalty\ ,

for any curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
\Lambda
and any dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
C
. This gives the complete bar-cobar adjunction relative to
\alpha

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between the categories of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras and the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proof.

These constructions can be found in
[
GL22
, Section 8]
.
∎

Remark 41
.

It is also an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

since
\widehat{\Omega}_{\alpha}
naturally lands in qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

3.12.
Compatibility with the morphisms of (co)operads.

Let
f:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
be a morphims of dg operads, let
g:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a morphism of conilpotent curved cooperads, and let
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
and
\beta:\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
be two curved twisting morphisms, such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

Then the bar-cobar adjunctions are compatible with the natural adjunctions induced by the morphisms, meaning that the following square of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is commutative. The complete bar-cobar adjunctions are also compatible with the natural adjunctions induced by the morphisms, meaning that the following square of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is commutative.

Remark 42
.

Under the extra assumption that the morphism
g:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
is quasi-planar, one can consider qp-complete algebras in the right hand side of the last commutative square of adjunctions, where
g^{!}
is replaced by
\widehat{g}^{!}
.

3.13.
(Co)admissible operads

In this subsection, we review various constructions that allow one the give a meaning to the homotopy theory of dg
\mathcal{P}
-(co)algebras, where
\mathcal{P}
is a dg operad.

Definition 49

(Admissible dg operad)
.

A dg operad
\mathcal{P}
is called
admissible
if the category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras admits a combinatorial model structure on right-transferred along the free-forgetful adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ -}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

determined by the following sets of maps

(1)

the set of weak-equivalences is given by quasi-isomorphisms;

(2)

the set of fibrations is given by degree-wise epimorphisms;

(3)

the generating cofibrations are the maps
\mathcal{P}\circ S^{n}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\circ D^{n+1}
for
n\in\mathbb Z
;

(4)

the generating acyclic cofibrations are the maps
\mathcal{P}\circ 0\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\circ D^{n+1}
for
n\in\mathbb Z
.

Definition 50

(Coadmissible dg operad)
.

A dg operad
\mathcal{P}
is called
coadmissible
if the category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras admits a combinatorial model structure left-transferred along the forgetful-cofree adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

determined by the following sets of maps

(1)

the set of weak-equivalences is given by quasi-isomorphisms,

(2)

the set of cofibrations is given by degree-wise injections,

(3)

the generating (acyclic) cofibrations are the maps between small objects so whose image through the forgetful functor is an (acyclic) cofibration.

Remark 43
.

Let
\Bbbk
be a characteristic zero field. Then every dg operad is admissible. Even in characteristic zero, not every dg operad is coadmissible. For instance, the operad
\mathrm{u}\mathcal{C}\mathrm{om}
is not coadmissible, see
[
GL22
, Section 8.3]
.

For a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
to be admissible (resp. coadmissible) it suffices to provide a natural path object (resp. a natural cylinder object).

Proposition 29

(
[
BM03
]
)
.

A dg operad
\mathcal{P}
that is
\mathcal{E}
-split is admissible and coadmissible. In particular, a cofibrant operad is admissible and coadmissible.

Proof.

As described in
[
BF04
]
, the cellular model of the interval
I
has the canonical structure of a
\mathcal{E}
-coalgebra.

For every dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
