, the dg module
[I,A]
inherits the structure of a dg
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
-algebra and therefore the structure of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. This provides a natural path object, and proves the existence of the transferred model structure on dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras.

For every dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
V
, the dg module
V\otimes I
inherits the structure of a dg
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra and therefore the structure of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra. This provides a natural cylinder object, and proves the existence of the transferred model structure on dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras.
∎

Example 4
.

For any dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, the dg operad
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
is
\mathcal{E}
-split. Indeed, the Barratt-Eccles operad is a dg Hopf operad: in particular, there exists a morphism of dg operad
\mathcal{E}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}
, which induces an
\mathcal{E}
-splitting on
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
. Thus,
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
is always admissible and coadmissible.

Proposition 30
.

Let
f:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
be a morphism of dg operads.

(1)

If
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
are both admissible, the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a Quillen adjunction.

(2)

If
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
are both coadmissible, the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a Quillen adjunction.

Proof.

This is a consequence of the fact that these model structures are transferred from dg modules. It is immediate to see that
f^{*}
preserves quasi-isomorphisms and fibrations of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebras, and that
f^{*}
preserves quasi-isomorphisms and cofibrations of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebras.
∎

Lemma 33

(After
[
GL22
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad and let
K
be an arity-wise acylic dg
\mathbb N
-module. We denote
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}\left(K\otimes\mathbb S\right)
,
i
the canonical inclusion of
\mathcal{P}
into
\mathcal{Q}
and
p
the canonical projection from
\mathcal{Q}
onto
\mathcal{P}
. This gives the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where the inclusion
i
is an acyclic cofibration and
p
is an acyclic fibration. If
\mathcal{P}
is
\mathcal{E}
-split, so is
\mathcal{Q}
. Furthemore, the induced Quillen adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

are Quillen equivalences.

Proof.

The fact that the inclusion
\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
is an acyclic cofibration and that its left inverse
\mathcal{Q}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
is a acyclic fibration is clear.

Let
e:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
be the
\mathcal{E}
-splitting of the dg operad
\mathcal{P}
. An
\mathcal{E}
-splitting of the dg operad
\mathcal{Q}
is given by the morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\penalty\ \otimes\penalty\ i}

together with the choice of a section
K\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\otimes K
of the projection
\mathcal{E}\otimes K\longrightarrow K
in the category of dg
\mathbb N
-module.

Let us prove that the Quillen adjunctions relating dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebras to dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras are Quillen equivalences. This amounts to prove that the two right Quillen functors

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ho}(p^{\ast}):\mathrm{Ho}(\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ho}(\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{alg})

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ho}(i^{\ast}):\mathrm{Ho}(\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{alg})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ho}(\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg})

are equivalences. Let us show that these are inverse equivalences. On the one hand, we have a canonical isomorphism
i^{\ast}p^{\ast}\simeq\mathrm{Id}
, thus we get an isomorphism
\mathrm{Ho}(i^{\ast})\mathrm{Ho}(p^{\ast})\simeq\mathrm{Id}
.

Notice that the data of a dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra structure amounts to the data of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure and of a morphism of dg module
K\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A\longrightarrow A
. We define an endofunctor
\tilde{(-)}
of the category of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebras as follows. Let
A
be a dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra. Its image
\tilde{A}
is the dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra whose underlying dg module is the path object
[I,A]=I^{\ast}\otimes A
(where
I
is the cellular model of the interval). Its dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure is given by the following maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}(n)\otimes(I^{\ast}\otimes A)^{\otimes n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}(n)\otimes\mathcal{P}(n)\otimes(I^{\ast}\otimes A)^{\otimes n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}(n)\otimes(I^{\ast})^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{P}(n)\otimes A^{\otimes n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\ast}\otimes A,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e(n)\penalty\ \otimes\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{I}^{n}\penalty\ \otimes\penalty\ \gamma_{A}^{n}|_{\mathcal{P}}}

where
\gamma_{A}^{n}|_{\mathcal{P}}
is given by the dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure on
A
and where
\gamma_{I}^{n}
is given by the dg
\mathcal{E}
-algebra structure on
I
constructed in
[
BF04
]
. The action of
K
is given as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(n)\otimes(I^{\ast}\otimes A)^{\otimes n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(n)\otimes A^{\otimes n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\ast}\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{A}^{n}|_{K}}

where the first map results from the inclusion of the first point into the interval
\Bbbk\longrightarrow I
which gives a map
I^{\ast}\longrightarrow\Bbbk
, and where
\gamma_{A}^{n}|_{K}
is the action of
K
on
A
given by its dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra structure.

We have canonical natural transformations

p^{\ast}i^{\ast}\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\tilde{(-)}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathrm{Id}

that are object-wise quasi-isomorphisms. Hence we get an isomorphism
\mathrm{Ho}(p^{\ast})\mathrm{Ho}(i^{\ast})\simeq\mathrm{Id}
.
Therefore,
\mathrm{Ho}(i^{\ast})
and
\mathrm{Ho}(p^{\ast})
are inverse to each other and their related Quillen adjunctions are Quillen equivalences.

Finally, let us prove that the Quillen adjunctions relating dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebras to dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras are also Quillen equivalences. Again, this amounts to prove that the two right adjoint functors

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ho}(p^{\ast}):\mathrm{Ho}(\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ho}(\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{cog})

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ho}(i^{\ast}):\mathrm{Ho}(\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{cog})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ho}(\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog})

are equivalences. This follows from similar arguments as those used in the algebra case. There is a canonical isomorphism
i^{\ast}p^{\ast}\simeq\mathrm{Id}
.

Again, a dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebra structure is completely determined by a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra and a morphism of dg modules
V\longrightarrow V^{K\otimes\mathbb{S}}
. We define an endofunctor
\tilde{(-)}
category of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebras as follows. Let
V
be a dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebra. The underlying dg module of
\tilde{V}
is the cylinder object
I\otimes V
. Its dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\otimes V\otimes\mathcal{P}(n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\otimes V\otimes\mathcal{E}(n)\otimes\mathcal{P}(n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}(n)\otimes I\otimes V\otimes\mathcal{P}(n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\otimes n}\otimes V^{\otimes n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(I\otimes V)^{\otimes n}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\penalty\ \otimes\penalty\ e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{I}^{n}\penalty\ \otimes\penalty\ \Delta_{V}^{n}|_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where
\Delta_{V}^{n}|_{\mathcal{P}}
is the given by the dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra structure on
A
and where
\Delta_{I}^{n}
is the dg
\mathcal{E}
-algebra on
I
constructed in
[
BF04
]
. The coaction of
K
is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\otimes V\otimes K(n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes K(n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\otimes n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(I\otimes V)^{\otimes n}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{V}^{n}|_{K}}

where
\Delta_{V}^{n}|_{K}
is the coaction of
K
on
V
and where the last map proceed from the inclusion of the first point into the interval
\Bbbk\longrightarrow I
.
We have canonical natural transformations

p^{\ast}i^{\ast}\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\tilde{(-)}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathrm{Id}

that are object-wise quasi-isomorphisms and yield an isomorphism
\mathrm{Ho}(p^{\ast})\mathrm{Ho}(i^{\ast})\simeq\mathrm{Id}
.
Therefore,
\mathrm{Ho}(i^{\ast})
and
\mathrm{Ho}(p^{\ast})
are inverse equivalences and their related Quillen adjunctions are Quillen equivalences.
∎

Proposition 31

(
[
BM03
,
GL22
]
)
.

Let
f:\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{Q}
be a quasi-isomorphism of cofibrant dg operads. The induced Quillen adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

are both Quillen equivalences.

Proof.

Let us denote
QE
the (large) set of morphisms of cofibrant operads
f:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
whose induced Quillen adjunctions relating their algebras and relating their coalgebras are Quillen equivalence. One can notice that
QE
is stable through composition and retracts and satisfies the 2-out-of-3 law. By Lemma
33
is contains morphisms of the form
\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}(K\otimes\mathbb S)
, where
K
is an acyclic dg
\mathbb N
-module. Since every acyclic cofibration of cofibrant dg operads is a retract of a map having this from,
QE
contains all the acyclic cofibration of cofibrant dg operads. Now, let
f:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
be an weak-equivalence of cofibrant dg operad. We can factorise the map
\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{Q}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
by a cofibration followed by an acyclic cofibration

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

The maps
\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{Q}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}
belong to
QE
since they are acyclic cofibrations. Then, the map
\mathcal{R}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
belongs to
QE
by the 2-out-of-3 law. So
f
belongs to
QE
as composition of two maps in
QE
.
∎

Semi-model category structures and reduction the admissible case.
Under weaker assumptions, M. Spitzweck constructed in
[
Spi01
]
a
semi-model category structure
on the category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras. Nevertheless, up to a convenient replacement of the initial dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, one can always assume the existence of a model category structure without losing any homotopical information.

Theorem 4

(
[
Spi01
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a
\mathbb S
-projective dg operad. The category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras admits a semi-model category structure, determined by the following sets of maps

(1)

the set of weak-equivalences is given by quasi-isomorphisms,

(2)

the set of fibrations is given by degree-wise epimorphisms,

(3)

the set of cofibrations is given by morphisms with a left-lifting property against acyclic fibrations.

These semi-model category structures are stable under quasi-isomorphisms of dg operads.

Theorem 5

(
[
Fre09
]
)
.

Let
f:\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{Q}
be a quasi-isomorphism of
\mathbb S
-projective dg operads. The induced adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is an equivalence of semi-model categories.

In particular, for any
\mathbb S
-projective dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, the canonical quasi-isomorphism
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{P}
always induces an equivalence of semi-model categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

where on the left-hand side there is a model category structure on the category of dg
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
-algebras. Recall that
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
is admissible since it is
\mathcal{E}
-split. Therefore, by replacing
\mathcal{P}
with
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
we can always "rectify" the semi-model structure into a model structure, and both present the same underlying homotopy category.

3.14.
Quasi-planar Bar-Cobar adjunctions

In the subsequent sections, we will manly work with dg operads of the form
\Omega\mathcal{C}
, where
\mathcal{C}
is a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. Nevertheless, if one starts with a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, there is a canonical quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad given by
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
. One can then construct
quasi-planar
bar-cobar adjunctions between their respective (co)algebras categories. It will follow from the results in the subsequent sections that these adjunctions are Quillen adjunctions when
\mathcal{P}
is (co)admissible and induce Quillen equivalences when
\mathcal{P}
is cofibrant.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. Let
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{P}
be the canonical quasi-isomorphism of dg operads, where
\mathcal{E}
is the Barratt-Eccles operad.

Quasi-planar bar-cobar adjunction.
The morphism
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}
induces an adjunction
(\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}\dashv\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
between dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and dg
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
-algebras. We define the
quasi-planar bar-cobar adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

as the following composition of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Quasi-planar complete bar-cobar adjunction.
The morphism
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}
induces an adjunction
(\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}\dashv\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
between dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras and dg
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras. We define the
quasi-planar complete bar-cobar adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

as the following composition of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

4.
Model structure on coalgebras over a cooperad

The goal of this section is to study the homotopical properties of the bar-cobar adjunction between dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras and curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, in the case where
\mathcal{C}
is a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad.

The dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is cofibrant, and therefore admissible by Proposition
29
. This means that the category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras admits a model category structure where weak-equivalences are given by quasi-isomorphisms and fibrations by degree-wise epimorphisms. Let us consider the bar-cobar adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

relative to
\iota:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}
, which will be denoted by
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}},\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}
from now on, since we will not consider any other curved twisting morphism. Our first goal is going to be to transfer this model category structure along the bar-cobar adjunction to the category of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Theorem 6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. The exists a combinatorial model category structure on the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras given by the following sets of maps:

(1)

the set of weak-equivalences is given by morphisms
f
such that
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(f)
is a quasi-isomorphism,

(2)

the set of cofibrations is given by morphisms
f
such that
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(f)
is a cofibration; they correspond to degree-wise injections,

(3)

the set of fibrations is given by morphisms with the right lifting property with respect to acyclic cofibrations.

Remark 44
.

Using the standard transfer theorem for model category structures only gives that cofibrations are morphism which are sent by
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}
to cofibrations. The theorem contains an additional characterization of cofibrations of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras as degree-wise injective maps.

4.1.
Outline of the transfer of model structures

Let us first start by defining the sets of morphisms of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras that we intend to study.

Definition 51

(Cofibrations)
.

A morphism
f
of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is a
cofibration
if
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(f)
is a cofibration of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Definition 52

(Weak-equivalences)
.

A morphism
f
of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is a
weak-equivalence
if
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(f)
is a quasi-isomorphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Definition 53

(Fibrations)
.

A morphism of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is a
fibration
if it has the right-lifting property against all acyclic cofibrations.

Both categories
\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}
and
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}
are presentable, therefore by Appendix
A.2
it suffices to exhibit a natural cofibrant resolution and a natural cylinder for coalgebras to prove the existence of the transferred model structure. We will show that cofibrations of Definition
51
are given by degree-wise injective maps in Proposition
36
and we will construct a natural cylinder in Proposition
39
.

For the rest of this section, let us fix a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
whose quasi-planar ladder is indexed by some small ordinal
\alpha
.

4.2.
Elementary cofibrations

Elementary cofibrations are a particularly well-behaved set of cofibrations of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. For any such elementary cofibration, its cokernel is a dg
\Bbbk
-module.

Definition 54

(Elementary cofibrations)
.

A morphism
f:W^{\prime}\rightarrowtail W
of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is an
elementary cofibration
if it is degree-wise injective and if the map
\overline{\Delta}_{W}:W\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ W
factors through the sub-object
\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ W^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ W
, that is, if there exists a dashed map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ W^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{C}}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ f}

that makes the diagram commute. Here
\Delta_{W}
denotes the structural map of
W
.

Remark 45
.

The induced map
\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ W^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ W
is also monomorphism since it is has a left inverse in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules.

Lemma 34
.

Let
f:W^{\prime}\rightarrowtail W
be an elementary cofibration curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. Then
\mathrm{Coker}(f)
is a dg module.

Proof.

Let us denote
p_{f}:W\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Coker}(f)
the canonical projection. We have

d^{2}p_{f}=p_{f}d^{2}=-p_{f}(\theta\circ\mathrm{Id}_{W})\overline{\Delta}_{W}=-(\theta\circ p_{f})\overline{\Delta}_{W}=-(\theta\circ\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{Coker}(f)})(\mathcal{C}\circ p_{f})\overline{\Delta}_{W}.

The definition of an elementary cofibration tells us that
(\mathcal{C}\circ p_{f})\overline{\Delta}_{W}=0
. We conclude by noticing that
p_{f}
is an epimorphism.
∎

Proposition 32
.

Let
f:W^{\prime}\rightarrowtail W
be an elementary cofibration. Then
f
is a cofibration.

Proof.

Identifying
W^{\prime}
with its image in
W
, we can decompose the underlying graded
\Bbbk
-module of
W
as the a direct
W\cong W^{\prime}\oplus U
. The pre-differential of
W
decomposes as the pre-differential
d_{W^{\prime}}
on
V
, the differential
d_{U}
on
U
and a degree
-1
map
\zeta:U\longrightarrow W^{\prime}
. The map of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

g:U\hookrightarrow W\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}\circ W

induces a morphism of dg modules

D^{0}\otimes U\longrightarrow\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W\penalty\ ,

whose restriction to
S^{0}\otimes U
is
g
and whose restriction to
S^{-1}\otimes U
is
-\mathrm{Id}\otimes\partial(g)
. One can notice that this restriction to
S^{-1}\otimes U
factors through the sub-dg module
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W^{\prime}\subset\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W
. We thus get a commutative diagram of dg modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{-1}\otimes U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{0}\otimes U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

which gives a commutative diagram of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ(S^{-1}\otimes U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ(D^{0}\otimes U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W\penalty\ .}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

This diagram is a pushout square since the underlying diagram of graded
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras is a pushout square. Since the left vertical map is a cofibration, so is the right vertical map
f
.
∎

Lemma 35
.

Let us consider a commutative diagram of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

where

(1)

i
and
j
are elementary cofibrations,

(2)

the map of dg modules induced by
g

\bar{g}:W^{\prime}/U\longrightarrow W/U

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Then
g
is a weak-equivalence.

Proof.

Let us decompose the underlying graded
\Bbbk
-module of
W
as a direct sum
W\cong U\oplus Y
. We decompose the underlying graded
\Bbbk
-module of
W^{\prime}
as a direct sum
W^{\prime}\cong U\oplus X
in such a way so that the restriction of
g
to
X
lands in
Y
. The diagrams built in the proof of Proposition
32
fit in the following commutative cube

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ(S^{-1}\otimes X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ(S^{-1}\otimes Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ(D^{0}\otimes X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ(D^{0}\otimes Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W\penalty\ .}

The left and the right face are pushouts squares which are also homotopy pushouts. The two back horizontal maps and the top front horizontal map are quasi-isomorphisms. Thus the homotopy pushout map
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}g
is also a quasi-isomorphism, which implies that
g
is a weak-equivalence.
∎

Proposition 33
.

Let us consider a commutative diagram of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

such that

(1)

f
is a weak-equivalence,

(2)

i
and
j
are elementary cofibrations,

(3)

the map of dg modules induced by
g

\bar{g}:U^{\prime}/U\longrightarrow W^{\prime}/W

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Then
g
is a weak-equivalence.

Proof.

Let us consider the following pushout square in the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

It yields a pushout square of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}U^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

which is also an homotopy pushout. Thus, the map

\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}U^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}Z

is a quasi-isomorphism. To conclude, the map
Z\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}W^{\prime}
is a weak-equivalence by Lemma
35
.
∎

4.3.
Ladders and filtered quasi-isomorphisms

A sub-set of weak-equivalences of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is given by filtered quasi-isomorphisms of
\beta
-indexed ladders, for any small ordinal
\beta
. The key example of such ladders is the quasi-planar ladder induced by the canonical quasi-planar filtration of
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 55

(
\beta
-ladder of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras)
.

Let
\beta
be a small ordinal. A
\beta
-
ladder of curved

\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras
is a functor

W:\beta\longrightarrow\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ ,

that sends every limit ordinal
k\in\beta
to

W(k)=\underset{i<k}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ W(i)\penalty\ ,

with
W(-1)=0
, and such that every map

W(i-1)\rightarrowtail W(i)\penalty\ ,i\in\beta\penalty\ ,

is an
elementary cofibration
.

Notation
.

We denote by

W(\beta)\coloneqq\underset{i\in\beta}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ W(i)

the value of the colimit of this
\beta
-ladder.

Remark 46
.

The first property about limit ordinal is equivalent to the fact that the functor

\displaystyle(1+\beta)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}

\displaystyle i

\displaystyle\mapsto W(i+1)

is cocontinuous.

Definition 56

(Associated graded of a ladder)
.

Given a
\beta
-ladder
W
, we define its
associated graded
as

\mathrm{gr}_{i}W\coloneqq W(i)/\penalty\ \underset{j<i}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ W(j)\penalty\ .

The coderivation squares to zero on this quotient
\mathrm{gr}_{i}W
, therefore it is a dg module.

The following general proposition will allow us to construct
\beta
-ladders of coalgebras in a general setting.

Proposition 34
.

Let
\beta
be a small ordinal, and let

\mathcal{D}^{(0)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(1)}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\cdots

be a
\beta
-indexed cooperad ladder, where we denote
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\mathcal{D}^{(\beta)}
. For every
i\in\beta
, let
F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}(-)
be the idempotent comonad on curved
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebras related to coreflexive full subcategory of curved
\mathcal{D}^{(i)}
-coalgebras.

(1)

For every curved
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
W
the diagram

F_{0}^{\mathcal{D}}W\longrightarrow F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}W\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}W\longrightarrow\cdots

is a
\beta
-ladder of curved
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebras.

(2)

The canonical map

\underset{i\in\beta}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}W\longrightarrow W

is an isomorphism, therefore the colimit of the ladder is
W
.

Proof.

For every
i\in\beta
and for every pdg
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
W
,
F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}W
is given as the following pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{(i)}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}\circ W\penalty\ ,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules. Combined with the fact that directed colimits commute with pullbacks in graded
\Bbbk
-modules, we get the fact that the map

\underset{i<k}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}W\longrightarrow F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}W

is an isomorphism for every limit ordinal
k\in\beta+1
.

It remains to show that for every
i\in\beta
, the map
F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}W\longrightarrow F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W
is an elementary cofibration. It fits in the following pullback diagram of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{(i)}\circ F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{(i+1)}\circ F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W\penalty\ .}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Since degree-wise injections are preserved by the tensor product and by pullbacks, the map
F_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}W\longrightarrow F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W
is a degree-wise injection. It remains to show that for every
p,q,j
(with
p\geq 1
and
1\leq j\leq p
) the decomposition map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes j-1}\otimes\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(q)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes q}\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes p-j}}

factors through the sub-object

\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes j-1}\otimes\overline{\mathcal{D}}^{(i)}(q)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes q}\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes p-j}\penalty\ .

Using coassociativity, one can rewrite the map as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p+q-1)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes p+q-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p)\otimes\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(q)\right)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes p+q-1}\penalty\ .}

Since the sequence
(\mathcal{D}^{(i)})_{i\in\beta}
is a cooperad ladder, the map

\Delta_{j}:\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p+q-1)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p)\otimes\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(q)

factors through
\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i)}(p)\otimes\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i)}(q)
, which proves the result.
∎

Corollary 5

(Coradical ladder)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. For every
n\in\omega
, let
F_{n}^{\mathrm{rad}}(-)
be the idempotent comonad of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras that coreflects onto the full subcategory of curved
F_{n}^{\mathrm{rad}}\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, where
F_{n}^{\mathrm{rad}}\mathcal{C}
is the
n
-stage of the coradical ladder.

Let
W
be a curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra. The diagram

F_{0}^{\mathrm{rad}}W\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow F_{i}^{\mathrm{rad}}W\hookrightarrow\cdots

is an
\omega
-ladder of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, called the coradical ladder. The colimit of this diagram is again
W
.

Proof.

Follows directly from Proposition
34
.
∎

Corollary 6

(Quasi-planar ladder)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. Recall from Subsection
2.10
that since
\mathcal{C}
is quasi-planar, it admits a canonical quasi-planar
\omega
-ladder

F_{0}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow F_{1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\cdots

whose colimit is
\mathcal{C}
. For every
i\in\omega
, let
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}(-)
be the idempotent comonad of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras that coreflects onto the full subcategory of curved
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Let
W
be a curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra. The diagram

F_{0}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow F_{1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\cdots

is an
\omega
-ladder of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, called the quasi-planar ladder. The colimit of this diagram is again
W
.

Proof.

Follows directly from Proposition
34
.
∎

Using ladders, we can define the notion of a filtered quasi-isomorphism of ladders.

Definition 57

(Filtered quasi-isomorphism of ladders)
.

A morphism of
\beta
-ladders of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras
f:W\longrightarrow W^{\prime}
is a
filtered quasi-isomorphism
if

\mathrm{gr}_{i}(f):\mathrm{gr}_{i}W\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathrm{gr}_{i}W^{\prime}

is a quasi-isomorphism for all
i\in\beta
.

Proposition 35
.

Let
f:A\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}A^{\prime}
be a quasi-morphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. The morphism of quasi-planar ladders

F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(f):F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime}

is a filtered quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

For every
i\in\omega
, the map

\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A)\longrightarrow\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime})

can be rewritten as the morphism of dg modules

\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A\longrightarrow\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A^{\prime}

which is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

4.4.
Cofibrations

We characterize cofibrations as degree-wise injections and subsequently prove that any filtered quasi-isomorphism is a weak-equivalence.

Lemma 36
.

A cofibration of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras is in particular a degree-wise injection.

Proof.

Let us consider the acyclic dg module
D^{1}
equipped with
its canonical structure of a unital associative commutative algebra.
Let
f:A\longrightarrow A^{\prime}
be a cofibration of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Its lifting property and the fact that the map
D^{1}\otimes A\longrightarrow 0
is an acyclic fibration imply that the inclusion
A\rightarrowtail D^{1}\otimes A
which is a degree-wise injection factors through
f
. Therefore
f
is also a degree-wise injection.
∎

Proposition 36
.

A morphism of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is a cofibration if and only if it is a degree-wise injection.

Proof.

Let
f:W\longrightarrow W^{\prime}
be a morphism of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. On the one hand, let us suppose that
f
is a degree-wise injection. Then it can be recovered from the transfinite composition of the sequence

W\longrightarrow W+F_{1}^{\mathrm{rad}}W^{\prime}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow W+F_{n}^{\mathrm{rad}}W^{\prime}\longrightarrow\cdots\penalty\ .

Every morphism in this sequence is an cofibration since it is an elementary cofibration. Cofibrations are stable by transfinite compositions, therefore
f
is also a cofibration.

On the other hand, let us suppose that
f
is a cofibration. We consider the following diagram of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ W^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(f)}

The top horizontal maps are clearly degree-wise injections. The map
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(f)
is by definition a cofibration of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras, therefore it is in particular a degree-wise injection by Lemma
36
. This implies that
f
is also a degree-wise injection.
∎

Proposition 37
.

Let
f:W\longrightarrow W^{\prime}
be a filtered quasi-isomorphism of
\beta
-ladders of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. The map
f(\beta):W(\beta)\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}W^{\prime}(\beta)
is a weak-equivalence.

Proof.

Notice that the following holds.

(1)

The map
f(0)
is a weak-equivalence, since it is the identity of the zero object
0
.

(2)

If
i\in\beta+1
is a limit ordinal so that
f(j)
is an equivalence
for every
j<i
, then the colimits

\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W(i)=\underset{j<i}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ \Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W(j),\quad\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W^{\prime}(i)=\underset{j<i}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ \Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W^{\prime}(j)

are homotopy colimits, and therefore the map
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}f(i)
is a quasi-isomorphism. Thus
f(i)
is a weak equivalence.

(3)

By Proposition
33
,
f(i+1)
is a weak-equivalence whenever
f(i)
is a weak-equivalence.

We conclude by an ordinal induction.
∎

4.5.
The cylinder object

Let
W
be a curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra. Let
A
be a cylinder object of
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W
in the category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras, that is, a dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra together with a factorisation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(W\oplus W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where
i
is a cofibration and
p
is an acyclic fibration of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Let
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
be the following pullback in in the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

\mathrm{Cyl}(W)\coloneqq\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\times_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(W)}W.

Our goal is to show that
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
is a natural cylinder object for
W
.

Notice that
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
fits in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(W\oplus W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\oplus W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cyl}(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(i)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(p)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\dagger}\times\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{W\oplus W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{proj}_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{W}}

of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. The morphism
i^{\dagger}:W\oplus W\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
is the transpose of
i
and the morphism
\nabla:W\oplus W\longrightarrow W
is the universal codiagonal morphism. The morphism
\eta
is the unit of the bar-cobar adjunction
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\dashv\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}
, which is a degree-wise injection, and thus a cofibration by Proposition
36
. Notice that
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(p)
is a filtered quasi-isomorphism of quasi-planar ladders by Proposition
35
.

Let us choose a particular summand
W
in the direct sum
W\oplus W
. We choose one of the two sections
s:W\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
of the map
\mathrm{proj}_{W}:\mathrm{Cyl}(W)\longrightarrow W
and one of the two sections
d:\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W\longrightarrow A
of the map
p:A\longrightarrow\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W
, in such a way that the following diagram of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cyl}(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(d)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(p)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{proj}_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

commutes. Moreover, the dg module
A
decomposes into
A=\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W\oplus K
where
K
is the kernel of the map
p:A\longrightarrow\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W
. The dg module
K
is acyclic, since
p
is a quasi-isomorphism. Let us take a contracting homotopy
h
of
K
, that is, a degree
1
endomorphism of
K
such that

\partial(h)=d_{K}h+hd_{K}=\mathrm{Id}_{K}.

We can extend
h
to
A
by zero on
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W
. Then
\partial(h)=\pi_{K}
. Now let
H
be the degree
1

endormophism of the graded complex
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A=\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A
defined as follows

H\coloneqq\sum_{k=0}^{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)}\otimes(\pi_{\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}^{\otimes k}\otimes h\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{A}^{\otimes n-k-1})\quad\text{ on }\quad\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)\otimes A^{\otimes n}\quad\text{for}\quad n\geq 1,

and

H=0\quad\text{on}\quad\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(0)\penalty\ .

In particular, for every
0\leq k\leq n-1
, the restriction of
H
to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)\otimes((\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W)^{\otimes k}\otimes K\otimes A^{\otimes n-k-1})
is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)\otimes((\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W)^{\otimes k}\otimes K\otimes A^{\otimes n-k-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)\otimes((\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W)^{\otimes k}\otimes K\otimes A^{\otimes n-k-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}^{\otimes k}\otimes\ h\ \otimes\mathrm{Id}_{A}^{\otimes n-k-1}}

One can extend
H
to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A=\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A
through the same formula

H\coloneqq\begin{cases}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)}\otimes(\pi_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}^{\otimes k}\otimes H\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{A}^{\otimes n-k-1})\text{ on }\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)\otimes(\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A)^{\otimes n}\quad\text{for}\quad n\geq 1;\\
0\quad\text{on}\quad\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(0)\penalty\ .\end{cases}

The same formula mutatis mutandis allows us to extend
H

to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A=\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A
. One can notice then that
H
commutes with the maps

\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\rightrightarrows\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A.

Lemma 37
.

The subobject
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)\rightarrowtail\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
is stable by
H
.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)^{\prime}
be the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cyl}(W)^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W;}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

in the category of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. By universal property, one has a morphism of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cyl}(W)^{\prime}
. Let us show that
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)^{\prime}
is curved and therefore isomorphic to
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
.

Let
X
be the pullback of the span
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W\longleftarrow W
in the category of pdg
\Bbbk
-modules and let
Y
be the pullback of the span
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W\longleftarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}W
in the category of pdg
\Bbbk
-modules. The pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)^{\prime}
can be computed as the following equalizer

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cyl}(W)^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ Y\penalty\ ,}

both in the category pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras and in the category of pdg
\Bbbk
-modules. This follows from the results of Subsection
3.4
, as the forgetful functor preserves finite cosifted limits. The following square of pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cyl}(W)^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A;}

commutes. The bottom horizontal and the left vertical maps are monomorphisms, which implies that the map
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
is also a monomorphism. Therefore
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)^{\prime}
is also curved and the canonical morphism
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cyl}(W)^{\prime}
is an isomorphism of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

It is clear that the sub-objects
\mathcal{C}\circ X\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
and
\mathcal{C}\circ Y\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
are stable through
H
. Hence, so is the sub-object
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)\subset\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
.
∎

Lemma 38
.

The subobjects
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
and
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
are stable by
H
for every
i\in\alpha
.

Proof.

It is clear by definition of
H
that
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A=F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\circ A
is stable by it. Recall that
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
is given by the following pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{Cyl}(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cyl}(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules. We already know that
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
and
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
are stable by
H
. Therefore
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
is also stable by
H
.
∎

Lemma 39
.

For every
i+1\in\omega
, the endomorphism
\partial(H)
of
\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
is equal to
the identity minus the projection onto
\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W
.

Proof.

For every natural integer
n
and every
0\leq k\leq n-1
, the summands

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)\otimes(\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W)^{\otimes n}\subset\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A\cong\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)\otimes(\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W)^{\otimes k}\otimes K\otimes A^{n-k-1}\subset\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A\cong\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\penalty\ ,

are both stable by the differential and by
H
. Then, a direct inspection shows that
\partial(H)
is zero on the first summand and the the identity on the second one, which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 38
.

For every
i+1\in\alpha
, the endomorphism
\partial(H)
of
\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
is equal to the identity minus the projection onto
\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}
. Therefore the maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{Cyl}(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(s)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathrm{proj}_{W})}

are quasi-isomorphisms.

Proof.

Let us consider the following commutative diagram of dg modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathrm{Cyl}(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(d))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(p))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(s)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\eta_{C})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathrm{proj}_{W})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(j)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\eta_{W})}

Let us denote by
\pi_{W}
the composition
\penalty\ \mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(s)\penalty\ \mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathrm{proj}_{W})\penalty\
and by
\pi_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
the composition
\penalty\ \mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(d))\penalty\ \mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(p))\penalty\
.
By Lemma
39
, we know that:

\partial(h)=(\mathrm{Id}-\pi_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W})\penalty\ .

Since
\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(j)
commutes with the differential
d
and
H
, we have that
\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(j)\partial(h)=\partial(h)\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(j)
and we compute that

\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(j)\partial(h)=\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(j)(\mathrm{Id}-\pi_{W})\penalty\ .

Since
\mathrm{gr}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{qp}}(j)
is a monomorphism, it implies that
\partial(h)=\mathrm{Id}-\pi_{W}
.
∎

Proposition 39
.

The factorisation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\oplus W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cyl}(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\dagger}\times\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{proj}_{W}}

makes
\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
a good cylinder object of
W
, in the sense that

(1)

the map
i^{\dagger}\times\nabla:W\oplus W\rightarrowtail\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
is a cofibration,

(2)

the map
\mathrm{proj}_{W}:\mathrm{Cyl}(W)\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}W
is a weak-equivalence.

Proof.

The morphism
i^{\dagger}\times\nabla:W\oplus W\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cyl}(W)
is a degree-wise monomorphism since both
\eta_{W}:W\oplus W\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(W\oplus W)
and
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(i):\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(W\oplus W)\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
are. Thus, it is a cofibration. To conclude, Proposition
38
tells us that the map
\mathrm{proj}_{W}:\mathrm{Cyl}(W)\longrightarrow W
is a filtered quasi-isomorphism. Thus it is a weak-equivalence.
∎

Remark 47
.

Actually, the map
\mathrm{proj}_{W}:\mathrm{Cyl}(W)\twoheadrightarrow W
is also a fibration as the pullback of a fibration.

5.
A Quillen equivalence,
\infty
-morphisms and homotopy transfer theorems for algebras

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar curved conilpotent cooperads. We show that the bar-cobar adjunction relating
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras to
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is a Quillen equivalence. This allows us to give another presentation of the homotopy category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras in terms of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras together with their transferred model category structure. We introduce
\infty
-morphisms of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras and show that they are (up to homotopy) invertible. We prove a homotopy transfer theorem for dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Finally, we show how another model categories structures on the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras can be obtained by a left Bousfield localization.

5.1.
The Quillen equivalence

Our goal is to prove that the bar-cobar adjunction can be promoted into a Quillen equivalence. Similar theorems in characteristic zero were proven in
[
Hin01
,
LH03
,
Val20
,
LG19
]
. For that, we leverage the quasi-planar context to build a contracting homotopy that works in positive characteristic, as we explain in Subsection
5.2
.

Theorem 7
.

The adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a Quillen equivalence, when one considers the transferred model category structure on the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Proof.

The theorem follows directly from Lemma
40
.
∎

Before going into the proofs, let us spell out a direct consequence of Theorem
7
.

Corollary 7
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be an cofibrant dg operad. The quasi-planar bar-cobar adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\text{-}\mathsf{cog},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a Quillen equivalence, when curved
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
-coalgebras are endowed with the transferred structure from dg
\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
-algebras.

Proof.

Let us denote
\psi_{\mathcal{P}}=\Omega\mathrm{B}(\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}):\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{P}
the canonical quasi-isomorphism, and
\epsilon:\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P}
the quasi-isomorphism given by the counit map.

First notice that the adjunction
(\psi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}\dashv\psi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
is a Quillen equivalence since both
\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
and
\mathcal{P}
are cofibrant dg operads. The adjunctions
(\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}\dashv\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
and
(\epsilon)_{!}\dashv\epsilon^{*}
are in general just Quillen adjunctions.

Let
A
be a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. Our goal is to show that the counit

\epsilon_{A}:\Omega^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}A\longrightarrow A

is a quasi-isomorphism. A direct computation shows that

\Omega^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}\cong(\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}\Omega_{\pi}\mathrm{B}_{\pi}A\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}\cong(\psi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}(\epsilon)_{!}\Omega_{\pi}\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\epsilon^{*}\psi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}\cong(\psi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}\Omega_{\iota}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}\psi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}\penalty\ .

Therefore since the counit of
(\psi_{\mathcal{P}})_{!}\dashv\psi_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
and of
\Omega_{\iota}\dashv\mathrm{B}_{\iota}
are quasi-isomorphisms, so is
\epsilon_{A}
. Showing that for a curved
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
-coalgebra, the unit of adjunction is a weak-equivalence is completely analogous.
∎

Remark 48
.

If the dg operad
\mathcal{P}
is just
\mathbb{S}
-projective, the above result can be adapted, and homotopy category of curved
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})
-coalgebras given by localizing at transferred weak-equivalences is still equivalent to the homotopy category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras given by localizing at quasi-isomorphisms.

Lemma 40
.

For every dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra
A
, the counit map
\epsilon_{A}:\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}A
is a quasi-isomorphism.

In the following paragraphs, our strategy to prove Lemma
40
is the following. First, we construct a section
\zeta_{A}
of the counit map
\epsilon_{A}:\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\longrightarrow A
in the category of dg modules. The goal is then to show that
\pi_{A}=\zeta_{A}\epsilon_{A}
is homotopic to an isomorphism on
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
. This is done by constructing an explicit degree
1
endomorphism
H
and by showing in Proposition
40
that
\partial(H)+\pi_{A}
is an isomorphism. Therefore
\epsilon_{A}
has a left inverse,
\zeta
, and a right inverse,
\zeta_{A}(\partial(H)+\pi_{A})^{-1}
, in the homotopy category of dg modules. This implies that
\epsilon_{A}
is a isomorphism in the homotopy category, and therefore a quasi-isomorphism.

5.2.
The contracting homotopy on trees

The key ingredient to showing Lemma
40
is to construct a degree
1
endomorphism of
\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{C}
that will yield contracting homotopy for algebras. The same construction will also work for coalgebras, and is used in the proof of Lemma
52
.

We abbreviate the dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
by
\mathcal{P}
. Let us denote:

(1)

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}=\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
,

(2)

\overline{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}=\overline{\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}}s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
.

The map h.
The graded
\mathbb N
-module
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}=(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
is made of planar trees such that
some
top nodes are labelled by
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
and the other nodes are labelled by
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
. Let
h
be its degree
1
endomorphism that consists in
taking the leftest top node of a labelled planar tree and

(1)

if this node is labelled by an element of
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
,
h
suspends this element;

(2)

otherwise
h
sends this labelled tree to zero.

Pictorially
h
it is given as follows
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(3)
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(2)
\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(1)
h
\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(3)
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(2)
\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(1)

In more precise terms,
h
is defined as follows by induction on the height of the planar trees that make
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}
:

(1)

On
\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
,
h
is zero.

(2)

On
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}
,
h
is given by

\displaystyle s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}\twoheadrightarrow s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{I}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}

\displaystyle s^{-1}x

\displaystyle\mapsto x\penalty\ .

(3)

Then on

(\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\mathrm{pl},\leq n+1}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\simeq s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}({\mathbb{T}}_{\mathrm{pl},\leq n}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}

h
is defined inductively by the sum of the map

\displaystyle(\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\mathrm{pl},\leq n+1}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\twoheadrightarrow s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{I}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}

\displaystyle s^{-1}x

\displaystyle\mapsto x

with the map

\mathrm{Id}_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(n)}\otimes\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\pi_{\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{I}}^{\otimes k}\otimes h\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}^{\otimes n-k-1}\right).

The qp filtration on trees.
We consider the filtration on
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}=(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
induced by the qp filtration of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
. The
n
-th term
F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})
is the subobject of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
made of planar trees labelled by elements of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
and
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
such that the sum of the qp weights of these elements does not exceed
n
. Remember that the non top nodes are only labelled by
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
. More precisely

\displaystyle F_{0}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{I}\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}(F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})\penalty\ \oplus

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}\sum_{j_{0}+j_{1}+\cdots+j_{k}=n,j_{0}>0}(s^{-1}F_{j_{0}}^{\mathrm{qp}}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(k))\otimes\left(F_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})\otimes\cdots\otimes F_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})\right)\penalty\ .

This induces a filtration of the graded
\mathbb S
-module
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{C}\cong(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})\otimes\mathbb S
:

F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{C}=F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})\otimes\mathbb S.

Equipped with the contracting homotopy and the qp-filtration, let us prove Lemma
40
.

Notation
.

Remember that we abbreviate the dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
by
\mathcal{P}
and that
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}=\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
. Let us denote:

(1)

QA=\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A
, the underlying graded
\Bbbk
-module of
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
.

(2)

RA=\overline{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A\oplus\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A\subset QA
, where we have a a canonical isomorphism of graded
\Bbbk
-modules
QA\cong A\oplus RA
.

(3)

p_{A}:QA\twoheadrightarrow A
the projection onto
A
with respect to
RA
. It results from the projection
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{I}\circ\mathcal{I}\simeq\mathcal{I}
.

The section.
The counit map
\epsilon_{A}:QA\longrightarrow A
has a canonical section in the category of dg modules
\zeta_{A}:A\longrightarrow QA
whose underlying graded map is the inclusion

A\cong\Bbbk\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\Bbbk\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A\rightarrowtail\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A.

There is a canonical isomorphism of dg modules

\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\cong A\oplus K

where
K
is the kernel of the counit. Our goal is to prove that
\pi_{A}=\zeta_{A}\epsilon_{A}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

The contracting homotopy on algebras.

Let
H
be the degree
1
endomorphism of the underlying graded
\Bbbk
-module
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A
of
QA
defined as

H=h\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A.

The differential of
QA
.

The differential on the graded
\Bbbk
-module
QA=\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A
may be
decomposed into several terms.

(1)

The pre-differential
d_{\mathcal{C}}
that comes from the pre-differential of
\mathcal{C}
which is non-zero on elements in
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}
and in
\mathcal{C}
.

(2)

The differential
d_{A}
on
A
.

(3)

The pre-differential
d_{CP}
induced the operadic curved twisting morphism
\iota:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
, which sends elements in
\mathcal{C}
to elements in
\mathcal{P}
.

(4)

The pre-differential
d_{\Delta}
induced by the decomposition morphism
\Delta
of the cooperad
\mathcal{C}
which is non-zero on elements in
\mathcal{P}
.

(5)

The pre-differential
d_{\theta}
induced by the curvature of
\mathcal{C}
which is non-zero on elements in
\mathcal{P}
.

(6)

The pre-differential
d_{CA}
induced by the dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra structure of
A
, which sends elements in
\mathcal{C}\circ A
to elements in
A
.

The qp filtration on algebras.
First we consider the filtration on
QA
induced by the qp filtration on
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
:

F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)=(F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A,\quad n\in\mathbb N.

In other words, we have

\displaystyle F_{0}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)

\displaystyle\coloneqq A\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)

\displaystyle\coloneqq(F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A)\oplus\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}\sum_{j_{0}+j_{1}+\cdots+j_{k}=n,j_{0}>0}(s^{-1}F_{j_{0}}^{\mathrm{qp}}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(k))\otimes\left(F_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{qp}}QA\otimes\cdots\otimes F_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{qp}}QA\right)\penalty\ ,

where the sum symbol
\sum
stands for the union within
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A
. This filtration is exhaustive, that is,

QA\cong\underset{n\in\omega}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}QA\penalty\ .

Moreover, the qp filtration of
QA
is stable by the pre-differentials
d_{A},d_{CA},d_{C},d_{\theta},d_{CP},d_{\Delta}
and by
H
. It is also stable through
\pi_{A}=\zeta_{A}\epsilon_{A}
. Furthermore, one has a canonical isomorphism of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QP)=\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}A

One can notice that
d_{\theta}
and
d_{CA}
vanish on
\mathrm{gr}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)
.

Proposition 40
.

For every
n\geq 0
, the map
\partial(H)+\pi_{A}
on
\mathrm{gr}^{\mathrm{qp}}_{n}(QA)
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

On
\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)
,
\pi_{A}
is the identity while
\partial(H)
is zero. So
\partial(H)+\pi_{A}
is the identity.

Let
n>0
. On
\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)
,
\pi_{A}
is zero. It remains to show that
\partial(H)
is an isomorphism. One can notice that on
\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)
,
d_{\mathcal{C}}
is reduced to its planar part in the sense that it is given by applying to every labels in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
the planar part of the coderivation of
\mathcal{C}
. As a consequence it commutes with
H
. So, again on
\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)
, one has

\partial H=d_{CP}H+Hd_{CP}+d_{\Delta}H+Hd_{\Delta}.

The labelled trees that form
\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})
have at most
n
nodes. One can filter it by the opposite of the number of nodes. The induced filtration on

\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)
is preserved by
d_{CP},d_{\Delta}
and
H
. Moreover, on the associated graded object
\mathrm{gr}\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)
,
d_{\Delta}
is zero and
\partial H
is

\partial H=d_{CP}H+Hd_{CP}=\mathrm{Id}.

Subsequently,
\partial H
is an isomorphism of
\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)
.

Thus for every natural integer
n
,
\partial(H)+\pi_{A}
induces an isomorphism on
\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(QA)
. So it is an automorphism of
QA
, which concludes the proof.
∎

5.3.
Fibrant objects

Fibrant objects in the transferred model structure on curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras admit a comparatively much simpler description than cofibrant objects in the model structure of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. They are given by quasi-cofree
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, that is, curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras whose underlying graded
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra is cofree.

Lemma 41
.

The functor
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}
commutes with sifted colimits.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that sifted colimits in dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras and in curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras are computed in graded
\Bbbk
-modules and that the endofunctor of graded
\Bbbk
-modules
\mathcal{C}\circ(-)
preserves sifted colimits.
∎

Remark 49
.

The functor
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}
is also conservative and thus it is monadic. Moreover,
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}
preserves finite cosifted limits and is conservative, thus it is comonadic. Hence, the adjunction
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\dashv\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}
is bimonadic; see for
[
Gri23
]
for more details.

Proposition 41
.

Let
W
be a curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra. The following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

W
is fibrant;

(2)

W
is in the essential image of the functor
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}
;

(3)

W
is a quasi-cofree
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra, that is, its underlying graded
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra is cofree.

Proof.

Let
W
be a quasi-cofree
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra, that is, a curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra whose underlying graded
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra is of the form
W\cong\mathcal{C}\circ A
. A straightfoward check shows that the degree
0
map

s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ A\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ A\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\circ A\xrightarrow{d_{V}}{\mathcal{C}}\circ A\twoheadrightarrow A

defines the structure of a dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra on
A
. Therefore,
W\cong\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
. Conversely, every image of a dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra
A
through
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}
is quasi-cofree by definition.

Now, let
W
be a fibrant object in curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. Let us prove that it is quasi-cofree. The unit of adjunction
\eta_{C}:W\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W
admits a retraction
r
, since it is an acyclic cofibration and since
W
is fibrant. Let
A
be the colimit of the reflexive pair of maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(r)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}}

in the category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras, where
\epsilon_{\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}:\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W\longrightarrow\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W
is the counit of adjunction. By Lemma
41
, the colimit of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(r)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(\epsilon_{\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}W})}

of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
, since
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}
preserves sifted colimits. It is also clear that this colimit is
W
. So there exists a canonical isomorphism
W\cong\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
.
∎

5.4.
Infinity morphisms

The notion of
\infty
-morphism extends the usual notion of morphisms of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Their main advantage is that
\infty
-quasi-isomorphisms are invertible (up to homotopy), and therefore one can replace a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras with two inverse
\infty
-quasi-isomorphisms. This provides a powerful tool to describe the homotopy category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Recall that for every dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra, the counit map
\epsilon_{A}:\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\longrightarrow A
has a canonical section
\zeta_{A}:A\longrightarrow\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
in the category of dg modules. Thus, if
K
is the kernel of this counit map, one has a canonical decomposition of the dg module
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
as

\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\cong K\oplus A.

Definition 58

(
\infty
-morphisms)
.

Let
A,A^{\prime}
be two dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. An
\infty
-
morphism
from
f:A\rightsquigarrow A^{\prime}
amounts to the data of, equivalently:

(1)

a morphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras
f:\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\longrightarrow A^{\prime}
,

(2)

a morphism of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras
f^{\dagger}:\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime}
.

Linear part.
Let
f:A\rightsquigarrow A^{\prime}
be an
\infty
-morphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Its
linear part

f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is the morphism of dg modules given by the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{dg}}:A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Let us denote
\epsilon:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
and
\mu:\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
the counit and the coaugmentation of the quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
, respectively. The linear part of
f:A\rightsquigarrow A^{\prime}
is equivalently given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{dg}}:A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}}

Homotopy part.
Let
f:A\rightsquigarrow A^{\prime}
be an
\infty
-morphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Its
homotopy part

f_{\mathrm{h}}
is the morphism of dg modules given by the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{h}}:K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
K
is the kernel of the counit map
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\longrightarrow A
. Equivalently, it is given by the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{h}}:M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}}

where
M
is the kernel of the map
\epsilon\circ\mathrm{Id}:\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\twoheadrightarrow A
induced by the counit of
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 59

(
\infty
-quasi-isomorphism)
.

An
\infty
-morphism
f:A\rightsquigarrow A^{\prime}
is called an
\infty
-
quasi-isomorphism
if its linear part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a quasi-isomorphism of dg modules.

Definition 60

(
\infty
-isotopy)
.

An
\infty
-morphism
f:A\rightsquigarrow A
is called an
\infty
-
isotopy
if its linear part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is an identity map of
A
.

Lemma 42
.

Let
f:A\rightsquigarrow A^{\prime}
be an
\infty
-morphism. If
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a fibration, then
f^{\dagger}
is the composition of an
\infty
-isotopy followed by a strict fibration of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, that is, a fibration of the form
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(p)
, where
p
is a fibration of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proof.

Let
s:A^{\prime}\longrightarrow A
be a section of
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
