in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules that is, a map such that
f_{\mathrm{dg}}s=\mathrm{Id}
. Let us consider the degree
0
map
\tau
from
\mathcal{C}\circ A
to
A
defined by
\tau_{\mathrm{dg}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Id}_{A}
and by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\mathrm{h}}\coloneqq:\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{h}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

It induces a graded endomorphism
t
of the graded
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
\mathcal{C}\circ A
given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t:\mathcal{C}\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{C}\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ A\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \tau}

that is an isomorphism since
\mathrm{gr}^{\mathrm{rad}}(t)=\mathrm{Id}
. Let us denote
D
the coderivation of
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
and let

\tilde{D}\coloneqq tDt^{-1}.

This is again a coderivation of the graded
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras
\mathcal{C}\circ A
such that
\tilde{D}^{2}=(\theta\circ\mathrm{Id})\Delta
. Therefore it defines a dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra structure on
A
, and
t:A\rightsquigarrow A
becomes an
\infty
-isotopy from
(\mathcal{C}\circ A,D)
to
(\mathcal{C}\circ A,\tilde{D})
. Moreover, we have

(\mathcal{C}\circ f_{\mathrm{dg}})t=f^{\dagger}\penalty\ .

∎

Proposition 42
.

Let
f:A\rightsquigarrow A^{\prime}
be an
\infty
-morphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. The morphism of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras
f^{\dagger}:\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime}
is

(1)

a weak-equivalence if and only if the linear part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a quasi-isomorphism;

(2)

an isomorphism if and only if the dg part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is an isomorphism;

(3)

a fibration if and only if the dg part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a degree-wise epimorphisms;

(4)

a cofibration if and only if the dg part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a degree-wise injection.

Proof.

(1)

Let us consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{dg}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(f^{\dagger})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in the category of dg modules. The two horizontal maps,
\zeta_{A}
and
\zeta_{A^{\prime}}
, are quasi-isomorphisms. Thus, the left vertical map is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if the right vertical map is a quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

If
f^{\dagger}
is an isomorphism, then
f_{\mathrm{dg}}=\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{\mathrm{rad}}(f^{\dagger})
is an isomorphism. Conversely, if
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is an isomorphism, then

\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{rad}}(f^{\dagger})=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{gr}_{n}(\mathcal{C})}\circ f_{\mathrm{dg}}

is an isomorphism. Hence,
f^{\dagger}
is an isomorphism.

(3)

Since
\mathcal{C}
is coaugmented, one has a functor from dg modules to curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras that sends a dg module
X
to
X
endowed with the trivial curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra structure. This structure is given by the zero structural map
0:X\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ X
.

This functor sends acyclic cofibrations of dg modules to filtered quasi-isomorphisms that are also degree-wise inclusions. They are in particular acyclic cofibrations of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. If
f^{\dagger}
is a fibration, then it has the right lifting property with respect to all acyclic cofibration, and in particular with respect to such acyclic cofibrations of dg modules. Subsequently,
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
has the right lifting property with respect to every acyclic cofibration of dg modules. So it is a fibration of dg modules, hence a degree-wise epimorphism. Conversely, if
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a fibration, then
f^{\dagger}
is a fibration as a direct consequence of Lemma
42
.

(4)

Let us consider the same commutative square diagram shown in point (1). If
f^{\dagger}
is a cofibration, then the right vertical map
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(f^{\dagger})
and the top horizontal map of this square are degree-wise injection. Thus the left vertical map
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is also a degree-wise injection. Conversely, let us suppose that
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a degree-wise injection. Then, it has a left inverse
g
in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules. Let us consider the endomorphism
h
of graded
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ A\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ g}

Its linear part is the identity of
A
. The same arguments as those used to prove point (2) show that
h
is a graded isomorphism. In particular, it is a degree-wise injection. So
f^{\dagger}
is a degree-wise injection, hence a cofibration.

∎

Proposition 43
.

Let
A
and
A^{\prime}
be two dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. There exists a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras

A\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\cdot\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\cdot\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\cdots\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\cdot\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}A^{\prime}\penalty\ ,

if and only if there exist
\infty
-quasi-isomorphisms
A\rightsquigarrow A^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow A
which are inverse to each other in homology.

Proof.

Given any zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms between
A
and
A^{\prime}
we get a zig-zag of weak-equivalences between
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
and
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime}
. All the objects in the zig-zag are fibrant and cofibrant, therefore they admit homotopy inverses, and we have two homotopy inverse arrows between
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A
and
\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime}
. Conversely, if there is a
\infty
-quasi-isomorphism
f^{\dagger}:\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime}
, we get the following zig-zag

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}(f^{\dagger})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

∎

5.5.
Homotopy transfer theorem for algebras

The goal of this subsection is to prove a homotopy transfer theorem for dg algebras over a cofibrant dg operad. First, we show that given a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
and dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
together with a decomposition
A\cong K\oplus X
, where
K
is acyclic, we can "perturb" the dg
\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure on
A
so that this new structure "restricts" to
X
. Then, we give an homotopical meaning to this construction in cases of interest.

5.5.1.
Technicalities

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad.

Notation
.

Let us denote
M
the graded
\mathbb S
-module
\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}
:

M=\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}.

We have a canonical isomorphism of graded conilpotent cooperads
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\cong\mathbb{T}(sM)
, since
sM
is precisely the graded
\mathbb{S}
-module of generators of the operadic bar construction of
\mathcal{P}
.

Let us also consider a sum of dg modules

A=X\oplus K

where
K
is acyclic. Let us choose a contracting homotopy
h
of
K
; we can extend
h
to
A
, by
0
on
X
. Let us denote
\pi_{X},\pi_{K}
the projections endomorphisms of
A
onto respectively
X
and
K
. All these arrows satisfy the following equations:

\left\{\hbox to109.3pt{\vbox to74pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 54.6484pt\lower-37.00002pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-54.6484pt}{-31.84029pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right.

Example 5
.

Let
X
be a dg module. Since we are working over a field
\Bbbk
, there exists a splitting
X\cong\mathrm{H}(X)\oplus K
, where
\mathrm{H}(X)
is the homology of
X
and
K
is an acyclic dg module.

Let us suppose that the dg module
(A,d_{A})
is endowed a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure

\gamma_{A}:\mathcal{P}\circ A\longrightarrow A\penalty\ .

Notice that it induces a dg
\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure on
A
by pulling back along the map
\Omega B\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
.

Definition 61
.

Let
D_{\beta}
be the degree
-1
coderivation on the graded
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A
that proceeds from the structure of a
\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebra on the dg module
A
, in the sense that

(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A,D_{\beta})=\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}A.

Moreover, let
\beta
be the composition of
D_{\beta}
with the projection onto
A
. One can notice that the restriction of
\beta
to
A\cong\mathcal{I}\circ A
is the differential
d_{A}
and that its restriction to
\overline{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\circ A
is

\overline{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\circ A\twoheadrightarrow sM\circ A\twoheadrightarrow s\mathcal{P}\circ A\xrightarrow{s\gamma}sA\longrightarrow A.

where the last map just sends
sa
to
a
.

The degree
0
map

sM\circ A\xrightarrow{\beta}A\xrightarrow{-h}A

yields a morphism of graded
\mathbb S
-modules
sM\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}(A)
. Therefore there is a morphism of graded operads
\mathbb{T}(sM)\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}(A)
,
which in turn yields a degree
0
map of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

\phi:\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A=\mathbb{T}(sM)\circ A\longrightarrow A.

Let
f
be the related morphism of graded
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras

f:\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\phi}\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A.

Definition 62
.

Let
\chi
be the degree
-1
morphism from
\mathbb{T}(sM)\circ A
to
A
whose restriction to
A
is zero and whose restriction to
\overline{\mathbb{T}}\circ A
is

\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)\circ A\cong sM\circ\mathbb{T}(sM)\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\phi}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{\beta}A.

Notice that
\phi
is given by the sum of
-h\penalty\ \chi
and of the projection onto
A
.

Recall that the conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
is given by the conilpotent graded cooperad
\mathbb{T}(sM)
endowed with the following pre-differentials:

(1)

the pre-differential
d_{\gamma}
which is induced by the operad structure of
\mathcal{P}
;

(2)

the pre-differential
d_{\mathcal{P}}
which is induced by the differential of
\mathcal{P}
;

(3)

the pre-differential
d_{u}
, which maps
s^{2}\mathcal{I}
to the unit of
\mathcal{P}
.

We will denote the later two pre-differentials by
sd_{M}
, as they are defined on the generators of
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
. We refer to Subsection
2.1
for more details.

Lemma 43
.

The following diagram in graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq 2}(sM)\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sM\circ\mathbb{T}_{\leq 1}(sM)\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sM\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq 2}(sM)\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\gamma}\circ A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}{}\circ\shuffle(\phi,\chi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\phi}

commutes.

Proof.

This follows from a straightforward checking.
∎

Lemma 44
.

The map
\beta\penalty\ f:\mathbb{T}(sM)\circ A\longrightarrow A

is equal to
d_{A}\penalty\ \phi+\chi
.

Proof.

This follows from a straightforward computation.
∎

Definition 63
.

Let
\mu
be the degree
-1
map from
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A
to
A

whose restriction to
A\cong\mathcal{I}\circ A
is
d_{A}
and whose restriction to

\overline{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\circ A
is the sum of the two maps

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\circ A\xrightarrow{\chi}A\xrightarrow{\pi_{X}}A

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\circ A\xrightarrow{\theta\circ A}A\xrightarrow{-h}A.

Moreover, let
D_{\mu}
be the unique degree
-1
coderivation on
the graded
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A
whose projection onto
A
is
\mu
.

Lemma 45
.

The degree
-1
composite map
\beta f-\mu:\mathbb{T}(sM)\circ A\longrightarrow A

is equal to the sum of the two maps

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)\circ A\cong sM\circ\mathbb{T}(sM)\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\phi}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{sd_{M}\circ A}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{\phi}A

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)\circ A\cong sM\circ\mathbb{T}(sM)\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\phi}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{A})}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{\phi}A.

Proof.

Let us denote
g=d_{A}\penalty\ \phi+\chi-\mu
and
g^{\prime}
the sum of the two maps described in the lemma. We want to show that
g=g^{\prime}
. Since
g
and
g^{\prime}
are respectively equal to the compositions

\displaystyle sM\circ\mathbb{T}(sM)\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\phi}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{g}A

\displaystyle sM\circ\mathbb{T}(sM)\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\phi}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{g^{\prime}}A

it suffices to prove the result on
sM\circ A
. We can notice that, on
sM\circ A
we have

g=d_{A}\penalty\ \phi+\chi-\mu=-d_{A}h\beta+\beta-\pi_{X}\penalty\ \beta+h(\theta\circ\mathrm{Id})=hd_{A}\beta+h(\theta\circ\mathrm{Id})\penalty\ .

Still on
sM\circ A
, this gives

d_{A}\beta=\beta D_{\beta}-\beta(d_{sM}\circ A)-\beta(sM\circ\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{A}))=-\theta\circ\mathrm{Id}_{A}-\beta(d_{sM}\circ A)-\beta(sM\circ\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{A})).

Therefore

g=-h\beta(d_{sM}\circ A)-h\beta(sM\circ\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{A}))=g^{\prime}\penalty\ .

∎

Proposition 44
.

The following equality between degree
-1
maps from
B\mathcal{P}\circ A
to
A

\phi D_{\mu}=\beta f

holds. Thus
fD_{\mu}=D_{\beta}f
.

Proof.

Let us prove the result on the height of the trees that make
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}=\mathbb{T}(sM)
.
First, on
A\cong\mathcal{I}\circ A
, one has

\phi D_{\mu}=d_{A}=\beta f.

Let us assume that the result the restrictions of
\phi D_{\mu}
and
\beta f
are equal on
\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)\circ A
for some natural integer
n
.
On larger trees
\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n+1}(sM)\circ A\simeq sM\circ\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)\circ A
,

\phi D_{\mu}
is the sum of the maps

(1)

\displaystyle sM\circ\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)\circ A\xrightarrow{\mu}A\penalty\ ,

(2)

\displaystyle sM\circ\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)\circ X\xrightarrow{sd_{M}\circ\mathrm{Id}}sM\circ\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)\circ X\xrightarrow{\psi}A\penalty\ ,

(3)

\displaystyle sM\circ\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)\circ X\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\shuffle(\psi,\psi D_{\mu})}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{\phi}A\penalty\ ,

together with the contribution (4) to the coderivation of
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
given by the composition of
\mathcal{P}
at the root level

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n+1}(sM)\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sM\circ(A\oplus sM\circ\mathbb{T}_{\leq n-1}(sM)\circ A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sM\circ(A\oplus sM\circ A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq 2}(sM)\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq 2}(sM)\circ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\circ(\mathrm{Id}\oplus sM\circ\phi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\gamma}\circ\mathrm{Id}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}

As a consequence of Lemma
43
, this last map (4) is equal to

sM\circ\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)\circ X\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\shuffle(\psi,-\chi)}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{\phi}A\penalty\ .

By the induction hypothesis (
\beta f=\phi D_{\mu}
on
\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)\circ A
) and by Lemma
44
,
the sum of the contributions (3) and (4) is

sM\circ\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\shuffle(\phi,d_{A}\penalty\ \phi)}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{\phi}A\penalty\ ,

which rewrites as

sM\circ\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\phi}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{A})}sM\circ A\xrightarrow{\phi}A\penalty\ .

We conclude by Lemma
45
.
∎

Proposition 45
.

The coderivation
D_{\mu}
endows
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A
with a curved
B\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra structure.

Proof.

Since
fD_{\mu}=D_{\beta}f
and since
f
is an isomorphism (by a standard filtration argument):

D_{\mu}=f^{-1}D_{\beta}f.

Thus, the coderivation
D_{\mu}
makes
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A
a curved
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra because so does the coderivation
D_{\beta}
.
∎

Proposition 46
.

The sub-graded
B\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ X
of
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A
is stable through
D_{\mu}
.

Proof.

The sub-graded
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ X
is actually the quotient/kernel of the idempotent endomorphism
(\mathrm{Id}\circ\pi_{X})
of
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A
. One can notice that the projection onto
A
of
(\mathrm{Id}\circ\pi_{X})\penalty\ D_{\mu}\penalty\ (\mathrm{Id}\circ\pi_{X})
and
D_{\mu}\penalty\ (\mathrm{Id}\circ\pi_{X})
are equal:

(\mathrm{Id}\circ\pi_{X})\penalty\ \mu\penalty\ \pi_{X}=(\mathrm{Id}\circ\pi_{X})\penalty\ \mu.

Thus

(\mathrm{Id}\circ\pi_{X})\penalty\ D_{\mu}\penalty\ (\mathrm{Id}\circ\pi_{X})=D_{\mu}\penalty\ (\mathrm{Id}\circ\pi_{X})

which proves the result.
∎

To conclude, we have a composition of morphisms of curved
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras

(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ X,D_{\mu})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A,D_{\mu})\xrightarrow{f}(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ A,D_{\beta}).

5.5.2.
The cooperad version of the homotopy transfer theorem for algebras

Theorem 8
.

Let
i:X\longrightarrow A
be an acyclic cofibration of dg modules and let
\gamma_{A}:\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ A\longrightarrow A
be a dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra structure on
A
. There exists another dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra structure

\mu_{A}:\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ A\longrightarrow A

which restricts on
X
, together with an
\infty
-isotopy

(A,\mu_{A})\rightsquigarrow(A,\gamma_{A})

of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proof.

Since
i
is an acylic cofibration of dg modules, it has a left inverse
p
and one can decompose
A
as
X\oplus K
where
K
is the kernel of
p
. The paragraph just above gives us a diagram of curved
\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

(\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ X,D_{\mu})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ A,D_{\mu})\xrightarrow{f}(\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C},D_{\beta})\penalty\ .

Applying the right adjoint functor from curved
B\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras to curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra that results from the unit map
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}
, we get diagram of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

(\mathcal{C}\circ X,\tilde{D}_{\mu})\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{C}\circ A,\tilde{D}_{\mu})\xrightarrow{\tilde{f}}(\mathcal{C}\circ A,\tilde{D}_{\beta})=\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(A,\gamma_{A})\penalty\ .

In that context,
D_{\mu}
is the coderivation on
\mathcal{C}\circ A
that induces the expected dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra
\mu_{A}
structure on
A
and
\tilde{f}
is the expected
\infty
-isotopy.
∎

5.5.3.
The homotopy transfer theorem for algebras

Theorem 9
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be a cofibrant dg operad, let
i:X\longrightarrow A
be an acyclic cofibration of dg modules and let
\gamma_{A}:\mathcal{Q}\circ A\longrightarrow A
be a dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra structure on
A
. There exists a dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra structure
\mu_{X}
on
X
, together with a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms

(A,\gamma_{A})\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\cdots\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}(X,\mu_{X})

of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebras. Furthermore, the maps in this zig-zag are homotopic to
i
in the model category of dg modules.

Proof.

Taking
\mathcal{C}
to be the quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{E})
, Theorem
8
yields the dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebra structure
\mu_{X}
on
X
together with a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras

(X,\mu_{X})\xrightarrow{i}(A,\mu_{A})\leftarrow\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}(A,\mu_{A})\xrightarrow{\tilde{f}}(A,\gamma_{A})\penalty\ .

Moreover, the acyclic fibration of dg operads
\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
has a section since
\mathcal{Q}
is cofibrant. Applying the induced right adjoint functor from dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras to dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebras yields the expected zig-zag in the category of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebras.
∎

Remark 50
.

This last result also follows from model-categorical arguments, as developed in
[
Fre10
]
.

5.6.
Further localisations and divided powers operations

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be an admissible dg operad and let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. Let us consider a morphism of dg operads
f:\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
. We have two Quillen adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Let us denote
\Omega_{f}
the composite left adjoint and
\mathrm{B}_{f}
the composite right adjoint.

Proposition 47
.

There exists a combinatorial model structure on curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras called the
f
-model structure, transferred from that of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-algebras, determined by the following sets of morphisms

(1)

the set of
f
-cofibrations is given by morphisms
g
such that
\Omega_{f}(g)
is a cofibration,

(2)

the set of
f
-weak-equivalences is given by morphisms
g
such that
\Omega_{f}(g)
is a weak equivalence.

(3)

the set of
f
-fibrations is determined by right-lifting property against all acyclic cofibrations.

Moreover, this is a left Bousfield localisation of the canonical model structure transferred from dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Meaning that the identity functor of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra, where at the source they are endowed with the canonical model structure, and at the target with the
f
-model structure, is a left Quillen functor.

Proof.

The
f
-cofibrations and the
f
-weak equivalences respectively contain cofibrations and weak-equivalences of the canonical model structure, transfer along the bar-cobar adjunction. Hence, every object is cofibrant and a natural cylinder object is provided by Proposition
39
. This proves the existence of the transferred
f
-model structure (see Appendix
A.2
). To prove that this is a left Bousfield localisation of that transferred from dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras, it suffices to show that
f
-cofibrations are in particular degree-wise injections which results from the same arguments as those used to prove Propostion
36
.
∎

Localizing at quasi-isomorphisms.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent
differential graded
cooperad. The cobar construction
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is augmented since
\mathcal{C}
has zero curvature. Let us denote
\nu:\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
the canonical morphism of dg operads given by the augmentation. We have the following adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \text{-}\mathsf{mod}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Indec}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Triv}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

where the adjunction
\nu_{!}\dashv\nu^{*}
is in fact given by the indecomposables functor
\mathrm{Indec}
(which is
\nu_{!}
) and by the trivial structure functor
\mathrm{Triv}
(which is
\nu^{*}
). Notice that since
\mathcal{C}
has zero curvature, curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras in pdg modules are precisely given by dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Proposition 48
.

The set of
\nu
-weak-equivalences is precisely the set of quasi-isomorphisms of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Proof.

The composition
\mathrm{Indec}\penalty\ \Omega_{\mathcal{C}}
is isomorphic to the forgetful functor from dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras to dg modules.
∎

Corollary 8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent dg cooperad. The set of weak-equivalences in the canonical model structure on dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is contained in the set of quasi-isomorphims.

Proof.

It suffices to apply Proposition
47
to the morphism of dg operads
\nu:\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
, combining it with Proposition
48
.
∎

Divided power operations in the homotopical setting.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent dg cooperad. By Proposition
47
, the category of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras admits a model category structure where

(1)

the set of cofibrations is given by degree-wise injections;

(2)

the set of weak-equivalences is given by quasi-isomorphisms;

(3)

the set of fibrations is given by maps with right lifting property with respect to acyclic cofibrations.

Let
(W,\Delta_{W},d_{W})
be a dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra. The structural map

\Delta_{W}:W\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{C}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{n}}W^{\otimes n}\penalty\ ,

lands on the coinvariants on the right-hand side, therefore divided power operations should appear. Nevertheless, since
\mathcal{C}
is quasi-planar, there is a natural isomorphism

\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{C}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{n}}W^{\otimes n}\cong\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\left(\mathcal{C}(n)\otimes W^{\otimes n}\right)^{\mathbb S_{n}}\penalty\ ,

of dg modules induced by the norm map (Proposition
2
). Therefore no divided power operations appear at the algebraic level.

These divided power operations do not disappear at the
\infty
-categorical level. Indeed,
\mathcal{C}(n)
is a quasi-free dg
\Bbbk[\mathbb{S}_{n}]
-module which is furthermore
projective
by Proposition
10
. Therefore we have equivalences

\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{C}(n)\otimes_{h\mathbb S_{n}}W^{\otimes n}\simeq\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{C}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{n}}W^{\otimes n}\cong\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\left(\mathcal{C}(n)\otimes W^{\otimes n}\right)^{\mathbb S_{n}}\not\simeq\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\left(\mathcal{C}(n)\otimes W^{\otimes n}\right)^{h\mathbb S_{n}}\penalty\ ,

where on the upmost left-hand side we consider
homotopy coinvariants
and on the upmost right-hand side we consider
homotopy invariants
. This means that the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms behaves like an
\infty
-category of
divided power conilpotent coalgebras
.

6.
Model structure on complete algebras over a cooperad

The goal of this section is to study the homotopical properties of the complete bar-cobar adjunction between dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras and complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras, in the case where
\mathcal{C}
is a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad.

The dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is cofibrant, and therefore also coadmissible by Proposition
29
. This means that the category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras admits a model category structure where weak-equivalences are given by quasi-isomorphisms and cofibrations by degree-wise injections. Let us
consider the complete bar-cobar adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

relative to
\iota:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}
, which will be denoted by
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}},\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}
from now on. Our first goal is going to be to transfer the model structure on dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras along this adjunction to the category of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Theorem 10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar curved conilpotent cooperad. The category of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras has the structure of a combinatorial model category given by the following sets of maps:

(1)

the set of weak-equivalences is given by morphisms
f
such that
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}
is a quasi-isomorphism,

(2)

the set of fibrations is given by morphisms
f
such that
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}
is a fibration; these are degree-wise epimorphisms,

(3)

the set of cofibrations is given by morphisms with the left-lifting property with respect to acyclic fibrations.

Remark 51
.

Using the standard transfer theorem for model category structures only gives that fibrations are morphism which are sent by
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}
to fibrations. The theorem contains an additional characterization of fibrations of complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras as degree-wise surjective maps.

6.1.
Outline of the transfer of model structures

The proof is somewhat dual to the proof of Theorem
6
, except we deal with not all curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras but those that are qp-complete. However, we extend our definitions of fibrations and weak equivalences to morphisms between any pair of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Definition 64

(Fibrations)
.

A morphism
f
of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is a
fibration
if
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(f)
is a fibration of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Definition 65

(Weak-equivalences)
.

A morphism
f
of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is a
weak-equivalence
if
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(f)
is a quasi-isomorphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Definition 66

(Cofibrations)
.

A morphism of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is a
cofibration
if it has the left-lifting property against all acyclic fibrations between qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Both categories
\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}
and
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}
are presentable, therefore by Appendix
A.2
it suffices to exhibit a natural fibrant resolution and a natural path object for qp-complete curved algebras to prove the existence of the transferred model structure. We will show that fibrations of Definition
64
are given by degree-wise surjective maps in Proposition
52
and we will construct a natural path object in Proposition
56
.

For the rest of this section, let us fix a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
whose quasi-planar ladder is indexed by some small ordinal
\alpha
.

6.2.
Elementary fibrations

Elementary fibrations are a particularly well-behaved set of fibrations of (qp-complete) curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras, such that the kernel of any such elementary fibration is a dg
\Bbbk
-module.

Definition 67

(Elementary fibrations)
.

A morphism
f:\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is an
elementary fibration
if it is degree-wise surjective and if the map
\overline{\gamma}_{\Lambda}:\Lambda^{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}\longrightarrow\Lambda
factors through
(\Lambda^{\prime})^{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}
, that is, if there exists a dotted arrow

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Lambda^{\prime})^{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f)^{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\Lambda}}

such that the diagram commutes, where
\gamma_{\Lambda}
denotes the structural map of
\Lambda
.

Remark 52
.

The map
\Lambda^{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}\longrightarrow(\Lambda^{\prime})^{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}
is a degree-wise epimorphism since it is has a retract in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules.

Lemma 46
.

Let
f:\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
be an elementary fibration of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Then
\mathrm{Ker}(f)
is a dg module.

Proof.

Let
i_{f}:\mathrm{Ker}(f)\longrightarrow\Lambda
denote the canonical inclusion. We have

i_{f}d^{2}=d^{2}i_{f}=\overline{\gamma}_{\Lambda}\penalty\ (\mathrm{id})^{\theta}i_{f}=\overline{\gamma}_{\Lambda}\penalty\ (i_{f})^{\mathrm{Id}}\penalty\ (\mathrm{id})^{\theta}\penalty\ ,

and
\overline{\gamma}_{\Lambda}\penalty\ (i_{f})^{\mathrm{Id}}=0
since
f
is an elementary fibration. We conclude using that
i_{f}
is a monomorphism.
∎

Proposition 49
.

Let
f:\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
be an elementary fibration of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. It is in particular a fibration.

Proof.

This amounts to prove that
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(f)
is a fibration. Let
U
be the kernel of
f
. Let us decompose the underlying graded
\Bbbk
-module of
A
as
\Lambda\cong\Lambda^{\prime}\oplus U
. The pre-differential of
\Lambda
rewrites as the sum of the differential
d_{U}
on
U
, the pre-differential
d_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
on
\Lambda^{\prime}
, and a degree
-1
map
\xi:\Lambda^{\prime}\longrightarrow U
. Let us consider the morphism of graded
\Bbbk
-modules
\pi_{U}:\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda\longrightarrow\Lambda\longrightarrow U
. It induces the morphism of dg modules

p:D^{0}\otimes\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda\longrightarrow U

whose restriction to
S^{0}\otimes\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda
is
\mathrm{Id}\otimes\pi_{U}
and whose restriction to
S^{-1}\otimes\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda
is
-\mathrm{Id}\otimes\partial(\pi_{U})
. The fact that
f
is an elementary fibration implies that the restriction of
p
to
S^{-1}\otimes\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda
factors through the projection
S^{-1}\otimes\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda\longrightarrow S^{-1}\otimes\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda^{\prime}
. One thus gets a commutative square of dg modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[D^{0},U]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[S^{-1},U]}\penalty\ ,}

and thus a commutative square of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{P}{[D^{0},U]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{P}{[S^{-1},U]}\penalty\ .}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

This square is a pullback square since the underlying square of graded
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras
is a pullback square. Moreover, the map
[D^{0},U]\longrightarrow[S^{-1},U]

is a fibration of dg modules, thus
L^{P}[D^{0},U]\longrightarrow L^{P}[S^{-1},U]

is a fibration of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. This implies that the pullback map
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda^{\prime}
is a fibration.
∎

Lemma 47
.

Let us consider a commutative diagram of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}

where

(1)

p
and
q
are elementary fibrations;

(2)

the map of dg modules induced by
g

\mathrm{Ker}(g):\mathrm{Ker}(p)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ker}(q)

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Then
g
is a weak-equivalence.

Proof.

Let us decompose the underlying graded
\Bbbk
-module of
\Lambda
as

\Lambda\cong U\oplus Z
. We can then decompose the underlying graded
\Bbbk
-module of
\Lambda^{\prime}
as
\Lambda^{\prime}\cong U^{\prime}\oplus Z
in such a way so that the restriction of
g
to
U
targets
U^{\prime}
. The square diagrams built in the proof of Proposition
32
, fit in the following commutative cube diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{P}{[D^{0},U]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{P}{[D^{0},U^{\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{P}{[S^{-1},U]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{P}{[S^{-1},U^{\prime}]}}

The left and the right faces are pullback (homotopy) squares. The two front horizontal maps and the bottom back horizontal map are quasi-isomorphisms. Thus the homotopy pullback map
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}g
is also a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 50
.

Let us consider a commutative diagram of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where

(1)

f
is an equivalence,

(2)

i
and
j
are elementary fibrations,

(3)

the map of dg modules induced by
g

\mathrm{Ker}(g):\mathrm{Ker}(p)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ker}(q)

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Then
g
is a weak-equivalence.

Proof.

Let us consider the following pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

in the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. It yields a pullback square of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

which is also an homotopy pullback square. Thus, the map

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}U\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda^{\prime}

is a quasi-isomorphism. Moreover, the map
\Lambda\longrightarrow U
is a weak-equivalence by Lemma
47
.
∎

6.3.
Coladders

We introduce coladders of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras, which lead to the notion of a cofiltered quasi-isomorphism. We will show that these cofiltered quasi-isomorphism are a subset of weak-equivalences of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. The key example of such coladders is the quasi-planar ladder induced by the canonical quasi-planar filtration of
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 68

(
\beta
-coladder)
.

Let
\beta
be a small ordinal. A
\beta
-
indexed curved
\mathcal{C}
-coladder
is a functor

\Lambda:\beta^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}

that sends every limit ordinal
k\in\beta
to

\Lambda(k)=\lim_{i<k}\Lambda(i)\penalty\ ,

with
\Lambda(-1)=0
, and such that every map

\Lambda(i)\longrightarrow\Lambda(i-1),\quad i\in\beta

is an
elementary fibration
.

A
\mathcal{C}
-coladder
\Lambda
is called
qp-complete
if
\Lambda(i)
is qp-complete for all
i<\beta
. We denote by

\Lambda(\beta)\coloneqq\lim_{i\in\beta}\penalty\ \Lambda(i)\penalty\ ,

the value of the limit of this
\beta
-coladder. It is qp-complete whenever the coladder is.

Remark 53
.

The property about limit ordinal is equivalent to the fact that
the functor

\displaystyle(1+\beta)^{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\mapsto 0

\displaystyle i+1<\omega

\displaystyle\mapsto\Lambda(i)

\displaystyle\omega\leq i<\beta

\displaystyle\mapsto\Lambda(i)

is continuous.

Definition 69

(Associated graded of a coladder)
.

Given a
\beta
-coladder
\Lambda
, we define its
associated graded
as

\mathrm{gr}^{i}\Lambda\coloneqq\mathrm{Ker}(\Lambda(i)\longrightarrow\lim{j<i}\Lambda(j))\penalty\ .

Notice that the derivation squares to zero on this kernel
\mathrm{gr}_{i}\Lambda
, therefore it is a dg module.

The following proposition will allow us to construct
\beta
-coladders in a general setting.

Proposition 51
.

Let
\beta
be a small ordinal, and let

\mathcal{D}^{(0)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(1)}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\cdots

be a
\beta
-indexed cooperad ladder, where we denote
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\mathcal{D}^{(\beta)}
. For every
i\in\beta
, let
F^{i}_{\mathcal{D}}(-)
be the idempotent monad on curved
\mathcal{D}
-algebras related to the reflexive full subcategory made up of curved
\mathcal{D}^{(i)}
-algebras.

Let
\Lambda
be a curved
\mathcal{D}
-algebra which is complete with respect to the cooperad ladder, that is, such

\Lambda\cong\lim_{i\in\beta}F^{i}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda\penalty\ .

Then the diagram

\cdots\longrightarrow F^{i}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow F^{1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda\longrightarrow F^{0}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda

is a
\beta
-indexed coladder of curved
\mathcal{D}
-algebras, which are all complete with respect to the cooperad ladder.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda
be a curved
\mathcal{D}
-algebra that is complete with respect to the cooperad ladder
(\mathcal{D}^{(i)})_{i\in\beta}
. Since
\Lambda
is complete with respect to the cooperad ladder the map

F^{k}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda\longrightarrow\lim_{i<k}F^{i}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda

is an isomorphism for every limit ordinal
k\in\beta+1
.

It remains to show that for every
i<i+1\in\beta
, the transition map
F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda\longrightarrow F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda
is an elementary fibration. Let us denote
K
its kernel. This map fits in the following pushout diagram of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda)^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda)^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{i}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda\penalty\ .}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

In particular, it is a degree-wise epimorphism since the map
(F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda)^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}}\longrightarrow(F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda)^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i)}}
is a degree-wise epimorphism.
Thus proving that it is an elementary fibration amounts to prove that the composite map

\shuffle(F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda,K)^{\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}}\longrightarrow(F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda)^{\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}}\longrightarrow F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda

is zero, since the kernel of the first map is

(F^{i}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda)^{\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}}
. Moreover, the kernel
K
is the image of the map

(F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda)^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}/\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i)}}\hookrightarrow(F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda)^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}}\longrightarrow F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda.

Thus, it suffices to
prove that the composite map

\shuffle(F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda,(F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda)^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}/\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i)}})^{\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}}\longrightarrow((F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda)^{{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}})^{\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}}\longrightarrow F^{i+1}_{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda

is zero. Since degree-wise injections are preserved by the tensor products and by pullbacks, this map is a degree-wise injection. Thus it remains to show that for every
p,q,j
, with
p\geq 1
and
1\leq j\leq p
, the map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes j-1}\otimes\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(q)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes q}\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes p-j}}

factors through the sub-object

\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes j-1}\otimes\overline{\mathcal{D}}^{(i)}(q)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes q}\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes p-j}\penalty\ .

Using coassociativity, such a map rewrites as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p+q-1)\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes p+q-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p)\otimes\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(q))\otimes(F_{i+1}^{\mathcal{D}}W)^{\otimes p+q-1}\penalty\ .}

Since the sequence
(\mathcal{D}^{(i)})_{i\in\beta}
is a cooperad ladder, the map

\Delta_{j}:\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p+q-1)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(p)\otimes\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i+1)}(q)

factors through
\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i)}(p)\otimes\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{pl}}^{(i)}(q)
, which proves the result.
∎

Corollary 9

(Quasi-planar coladder)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. Recall from Subsection
2.10
that since
\mathcal{C}
is quasi-planar, it admits a canonical quasi-planar
\omega
-ladder

F_{0}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow F_{1}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow F_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\cdots

whose colimit is
\mathcal{C}
. For every
i\in\omega
, let
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}(-)
be the idempotent monad of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras that reflects onto the full subcategory of curved
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Let
\Lambda
be a curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra. The diagram

\cdots\twoheadrightarrow F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\longrightarrow\cdots\twoheadrightarrow F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda

is an
\omega
-coladder of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras, whose limit is the qp-completion of
\Lambda
, called the quasi-planar coladder of
\Lambda
.

Proof.

Follows directly from Proposition
51
.
∎

6.4.
Fibrations

Lemma 48
.

A fibration of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is in particular a degree-wise epimorphism.

Proof.

Let
f:V\longrightarrow V^{\prime}
be a fibration of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. We equip
D^{0}
with its canonical counital coassociative cocommutative coalgebra structure. The map
D^{0}\otimes V^{\prime}\longrightarrow V^{\prime}
which is degree-wise surjective factors through
f
which is thus also degree-wise surjective.
∎

Proposition 52
.

A morphism of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is a fibration if and only if it is a degree-wise epimorphism.

Proof.

Let
f:\Lambda\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
be a morphism of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.
On the one hand, let us suppose that
f
is a degree-wise epimorphism and let
K
be its kernel. Since
\Lambda
is qp-complete, the morphism
f
can be recovered as the transfinite backward composition of the sequence

\cdots\longrightarrow F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\times_{F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda^{\prime}}\Lambda^{\prime}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow F^{0}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\times_{F^{0}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda^{\prime}}\Lambda^{\prime}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}.

Since every such morphism is an elementary fibration and since fibration are preserved by backward transfinite compositions, the morphism
f
is therefore a fibration.

On the other hand, let us suppose that
f
is a fibration. Let us consider the following square diagram of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\mathcal{P}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\mathcal{P}}\Lambda^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\prime}}

The bottom horizontal map and the left vertical map are degree-wise epimorphisms. Thus, so is the right vertical map.
∎

6.5.
Cofiltered quasi-isomorphisms

Definition 70

(Cofiltered quasi-isomorphism of coladders)
.

A morphism of
\beta
-indexed curved
\mathcal{C}
-coladders
f:\Lambda\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
is a
cofiltered quasi-isomorphism
if

\mathrm{gr}^{i}(f):\mathrm{gr}^{i}\Lambda\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathrm{gr}^{i}\Lambda^{\prime}

is a quasi-isomorphism for all
i\in\beta
.

Proposition 53
.

Let
f:V\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}V^{\prime}
be a quasi-isomorphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. The morphism of coladders

F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V\longrightarrow F^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}

is a cofiltered quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

For every
i\in\omega
, the map

\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}(\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V)\longrightarrow\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}(\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime})

rewrites as the morphism of dg modules

V^{\mathrm{gr}_{i}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}V^{\prime\mathrm{gr}_{i}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}

which is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 54
.

Let
f:\Lambda\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
be a cofiltered quasi-isomorphism of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coladders indexed by an ordinal
\beta
. The map
f(\beta):\Lambda(\beta)\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}(\beta)
is a weak-equivalence.

Proof.

Notice that the following holds.

(1)

The map
f(0)
is a weak-equivalence, since it is the identity of the zero object
0
.

(2)

If
i\in\beta+1
is a limit ordinal so that
f(j)
is an equivalence
for every
j<i
, then the limits

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda(i)\cong\lim_{j<i}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda(j),\quad\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda^{\prime}(i)\cong\lim_{j<i}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda^{\prime}(j)

are homotopy limits, and therefore the map
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}f(i)
is a quasi-isomorphism. Thus
f(i)
is a weak-equivalence.

(3)

By Proposition
50
,
f(i+1)
is a weak-equivalence whenever
f(i)
is a weak-equivalence.

We conclude by an ordinal induction.
∎

6.6.
Path object

Let
\Lambda
be an qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. Let
P
be a path object of
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda
in the category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. This means that there exists a factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Lambda\oplus\Lambda)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where
\iota
is an acyclic cofibration and
p
a fibration in the category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. Let
D
be the following pushout

D\coloneqq\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P\amalg_{\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(B)}\Lambda.

in the category of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. We have that, for every natural integer
n
, the canonical map

F_{\mathrm{qp}}^{i}D\longrightarrow F_{\mathrm{qp}}^{i}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P\amalg_{F_{\mathrm{qp}}^{i}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(B)}F_{\mathrm{qp}}^{i}\Lambda.

is an isomorphism. Moreover, the qp-completion
\widehat{D}
of
D
is the limit of the diagram

\cdots\longrightarrow F_{\mathrm{qp}}^{i}D\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow F_{\mathrm{qp}}^{0}D\penalty\ .

It can be computed as the pushout
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P\amalg_{\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(B)}\Lambda
in the category of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Our goal is to show that
\widehat{D}
is a natural path object in the category of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. These objects fit in the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Lambda\oplus\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda\oplus\Lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(\iota)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(p)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\Lambda\oplus\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\dagger}\amalg\Delta}

of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Here the morphism
j
is given by
\Lambda
in the pushout. The morphism
p^{\dagger}\amalg\Delta
is induced by the transpose of
p
and by the diagonal
\Delta:\Lambda\longrightarrow\Lambda\oplus\Lambda
. The morphisms
\epsilon
are given by the counit of the complete bar-cobar adjunction
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\dashv\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}
. They are degree-wise epimorphisms and thus fibrations by Proposition
52
. Notice that
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(i)
is a filtered quasi-isomorphism of coherent coladders by Proposition
53
. The first step is to show that
j
is a cofiltered quasi-isomorphism.

Let us choose a particular summand
\Lambda
in the product
\Lambda\oplus\Lambda
. This way, we choose one of the two projections
t:D\longrightarrow\Lambda
and one of the two projections
r:P\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda

in such a way that the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(\iota)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(r)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\Lambda\oplus\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

commutes. The dg module
P
decomposes into
r:P\cong\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda\oplus K
where
K
is the kernel of the map
P\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda
. The dg module
K
is acyclic since
r
is a quasi-isomorphism. Let us take a contracting homotopy
h
of
K
, that is, a degree
1
endomorphism of
K
such that

\partial(h)=d_{K}h+hd_{K}=\mathrm{Id}_{K}.

We can extend
h
to
P
by zero on
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda
. Then
\partial(h)=\pi_{K}
, where
\pi_{K}
is the projection onto
K
. Now let
H
be the degree
1
endomorphism of the graded module

\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P=P^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}=\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n),P^{\otimes n}]

defined as

H\coloneqq\prod_{n\geq 0}\left[\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)},\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\pi_{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}^{\otimes k}\otimes h\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{V}^{\otimes n-k-1}\right]\penalty\ .

One can extend
H
to
\left(P^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
using the same formula

H\coloneqq\prod_{n\geq 0}\left[\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)},\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\pi_{(\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}^{\otimes k}\otimes H\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{V^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}^{\otimes n-k-1}\right].

The same formula mutatis mutandis allows us to extend
H
to
\left(\left(P^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
. One can notice then that
H
commutes with the maps

\left(\left(P^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\rightrightarrows\left(P^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longrightarrow P^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\penalty\ .

Lemma 49
.

The degree
1
graded endomorphism
H
of
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P
projects onto
D
, in the sense that the map

\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P\xrightarrow{H}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P\xrightarrow{q}D

factors through the projection
q:\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P\longrightarrow D
. We also denote by
H
the resulting unique degree
1
graded endomorphism of
D
.

Proof.

We set

(1)

X
to be the pushout of the cospan
\Lambda\longleftarrow\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda\longrightarrow\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P
in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules;

(2)

and
Y
to be the pushout of the cospan
\Lambda^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longleftarrow(\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longrightarrow(\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules.

The diagram of graded
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}

is colimiting both in the category complete graded
\mathcal{C}
-algebras and in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules. Indeed, the forgetful functor from graded
\mathcal{C}
-algebras to graded
\Bbbk
-modules commutes with reflexive coequalisers since those are preserved by the comonad
(-)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
.

Notice the following:

(1)

the degree
1
endomorphism
H
of
\left(P^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
projects onto
X^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
;

(2)

the degree
1
endomorphism
H
of
\left(\left(P^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}

projects onto
Y^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
;

since their restriction to, respectively,
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda
and
(\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda)^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
is zero. Therefore,
H
projects onto the of the coequalizer, which is given by
D
.
∎

Lemma 50
.

The map
H
projects onto the quotients
F^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P
and
F^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}D
for every
i\geq 0
.

Proof.

It is clear that, by definition,
H
that it projects onto
F^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P=P^{F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}}
. Thus
H
also projects onto
F^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}D
since the following square diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

is a pushout in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules and since we already know that
H
projects onto
D
and onto
F^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P
.
∎

Lemma 51
.

For every
i\geq 0
, the endomorphism
\partial(H)
of
\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(P)
is equal to the identity minus the projection onto
\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda
.

Proof.

Both summands
\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}B
and

\prod_{n\geq 0}\left[\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n),\bigoplus_{1\leq k<n}(\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda)^{\otimes k}\otimes K\otimes V^{n-k-1}\right]

of
\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P
are stable through the differential and
H
. Then, a straightforward check shows that
\partial(H)
is zero on the first summand and the identity on the second one.
∎

Proposition 55
.

For every
i\geq 0
, the endomorphism
\partial(H)
of
\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}D
is equal to the identity minus the projection onto
\mathrm{gr}^{n}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda
. Subsequently, the maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}(j)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}(t)}

are quasi-isomorphisms.

Proof.

Let us consider the following commutative diagram of dg modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(\iota)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(r)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\Lambda\oplus\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

where the maps are denoted by the same letter as before, omitting the functor
\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}(-)
applied on them for simplicity. Since
q
commutes with the differential
d
and with
H
, and since the squares above are commutative, we have

\partial(h)q=q\partial(h)=q(\mathrm{Id}-\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(\iota r))=q-q\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(\iota r)=q-jtq=(\mathrm{Id}-jt)q=(\mathrm{Id}-\pi_{\mathrm{gr}^{n}_{\mathrm{qr}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda})q.

Since
q:\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P\longrightarrow\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}D
is a degree-wise epimorphism,
\partial(h)=\mathrm{Id}-\pi_{\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda}
on
\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}D
. Therefore,
\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}\Lambda
is a deformation retract of
\mathrm{gr}^{i}_{\mathrm{qp}}D
.
∎

Proposition 56
.

Let
\Lambda
be a qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. The factorisation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda\oplus\Lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\dagger}\amalg\Delta}

makes
\widehat{D}
a good path object of
\Lambda
, in the sense that

(1)

the map
p^{\dagger}\amalg\Delta:\widehat{D}\longrightarrow\Lambda\oplus\Lambda
is a fibration,

(2)

the map
j:\Lambda\longrightarrow\widehat{D}
is a weak-equivalence.

Proof.

The map
\widehat{D}\longrightarrow\Lambda\oplus\Lambda
is a degree-wise epimorphism since the maps
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(p):\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}P\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Lambda\oplus\Lambda)
and
\epsilon_{\Lambda\oplus\Lambda}:\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Lambda\oplus\Lambda)\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda\oplus\Lambda
are degree-wise epimorphisms. Therefore it is a fibration, since both
\widehat{D}
and
\Lambda\oplus\Lambda
are qp-complete. To conclude, Proposition
55
tells us that the map
j:\Lambda\longrightarrow\widehat{D}
is a cofiltered quasi-isomorphism. Thus it is a weak-equivalence.
∎

Remark 54
.

Actually, the map
j:\Lambda\longrightarrow\widehat{D}
is also a cofibration as the pushout of a cofibration.

7.
A Quillen equivalence,
\infty
-morphisms and homotopy transfer theorems for coalgebras

We show that the complete bar-cobar adjunction induces a Quillen equivalence. This allows us to give another presentation of the homotopy category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras in terms of complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. We introduce
\infty
-morphisms and show that they are invertible. This allows us to prove a homotopy transfer theorem for dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. Finally, we show how another model categories structures on the category of complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras can be obtained by a right Bousfield localization.

7.1.
The Quillen equivalence

The goal of this subsection is to show the following theorem.

Theorem 11

(After
[
GL22
, Section 11]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. The complete bar-cobar adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a Quillen equivalence.

Proof.

The theorem follows directly from Lemma
52
.
∎

Corollary 10
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a cofibrant dg operad. The quasi-planar complete bar-cobar adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathrm{q.p}}_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a Quillen equivalence.

Proof.

It suffices to notice that this Quillen adjunction factors into two Quillen equivalences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

where the first adjunction is induced by the quasi-morphism
\psi:\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{P}
.
∎

Lemma 52
.

For every dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
V
, the unit map
\eta_{V}:V\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
is a quasi-isomorphism.

The result was already proven in
[
GL22
, Section 11]
. The proof here is almost the same. The main difference lays in the fact that leveraging the qp-filtration of
\mathcal{C}
in Lemma
58
makes the cofiltration arguments simpler than in
[
GL22
, Lemma 11.28]
.

In the following paragraphs, our strategy to show Lemma
52
is the following. First, we notice that the unit map
\eta_{V}:V\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
has a canonical left-inverse
\xi_{V}
in the category of dg modules. Let
\pi_{V}=\eta_{V}\xi_{V}
. We define a define a degree
1
map
H
on
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}B
and show that
\partial(H)+\pi_{V}
is an isomorphism in Proposition
40
. Therefore
\pi_{V}
is an quasi-isomorphism since it is homotopic to an isomorphism. This implies that
\eta_{V}
has both a left-inverse
\xi_{V}
and a right-inverse
\xi_{V}(\partial(H)+\pi_{V})^{-1}
in the homotopy category of dg modules. Therefore
\eta_{V}
is a quasi-isomorphism and this concludes the proof.

Notation
.

We abbreviate the dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
by
\mathcal{P}
for the rest of this subsection. We denote:

(1)

by
z:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
the canonical augmentation of the underlying graded operad.

(2)

the pre-differenial
d_{\mathcal{C}}
on
\mathcal{P}
that is induced by the pre-differential of
\mathcal{C}
,

(3)

the pre-differential
d_{\theta}
that results from the curvature of
\mathcal{C}
,

(4)

the pre-differential
d_{\Delta}
that results from the cooperad structure on
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 71

(Operad-cooperad diagrams)
.

The
planar operad-cooperad diagram
is the following one arrow diagram of graded
\mathbb N
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}.}

The
symmetric operad-cooperad diagram
is the following one arrow diagram of graded
\mathbb S
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{C}.}

This is actually the image through the free
\mathbb S
-module functor
-\otimes\mathbb S
of the planar operad-cooperad diagram.

One has the following pre-differentials on the planar operad-cooperad diagram:

(1)

the pre-differential
d_{\theta}
that results from the eponymous derivation on
\mathcal{P}
,

(2)

the pre-differential
d_{CP}
that is induced by the canonical twisting morphism
\iota:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{P}
,

(3)

the pre-differential
d_{\Delta}
that result from the eponymous derivation on
\mathcal{P}
.

These pre-differentials induce pre-differentials the symmetric operad-cooperad diagram. There is an additional pre-differential
d_{C}
that again results from the eponymous derivation on
\mathcal{P}
, which is a priori non-planar.

The restriction-extension diagram
The restriction-extension diagram is a tool that allows one to check whether derivations and homotopies can be restricted to cofree coalgebras, which are very hard to describe in general.

Definition 72

(Restriction-extension diagram)
.

Let
V
be a graded
\Bbbk
-module. The
restriction-extension diagram

\mathrm{RE}
is the following diagram of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{P}V^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\left(V^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(V^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}

shortened into

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{E}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{E}_{2}.}

Lemma 53

(After
[
GL22
, Lemma 11.8]
)
.

The restriction-extension diagram
\mathrm{RE}
is a pullback square.

Proof.

Let us consider the following diagram of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{P}V^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\left(V^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(V^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(V^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\right)^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}.}

Both the top square and the bottom square are pullback squares. Thus, the restriction-extension diagram is a pullback square.
∎

Definition 73

(Extension diagram)
.

We define the
extension diagram

\mathrm{E}
as the sub-diagram of the restriction-extension diagram that only contains
\mathrm{E}_{1}
and
\mathrm{E}_{2}
.

Remark 55
.

Notice that the extension diagram is the image of the planar operad-cooperad diagram and of the symmetric operad-cooperad diagram by the functor
V^{(-)}
, for any graded
\Bbbk
-module
V
.

Let
f
be a degree
k
endomorphism of the extension diagram. If
f
extends to the whole restriction-extension diagram, this extension is necessarily unique since
\mathrm{R}_{1},\mathrm{R}_{2},W
are sub-graded
\Bbbk
-modules of
\mathrm{E}_{2}
. Moreover, the existence of such an extension amounts to the fact that the restriction of
f(\mathrm{E}_{2})
to
\mathrm{R}_{1}
,
\mathrm{R}_{2}
and
W
has its image in respectively
\mathrm{R}_{1}
,
\mathrm{R}_{2}
and
W
.

Definition 74

(Cocone of the restriction-extension diagram)
.

The restriction-extension diagram
\mathrm{RE}
has a canonical cocone towards
V
induced by the unit of
\mathcal{P}
and the coaugmentation of
\mathcal{C}

V^{\mathcal{P}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow V^{\mathcal{I}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathcal{I}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathcal{I}}\cong V.

This cocone restricts to a morphism
\xi_{V}:L^{\mathcal{P}}V^{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow V
. Notice that the graded map
\xi_{V}:\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V\longrightarrow V
is in fact a morphism of dg modules. The morphism
\xi_{V}
admits two sections:

(1)

the unit map
\eta_{V}:V\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
, which is also a morphism of dg modules,

(2)

the map induced by the counit on
\mathcal{C}
and the graded augmentation map on the underlying graded operad of
\mathcal{P}

\zeta_{V}:V\simeq L^{\mathcal{I}}V^{\mathcal{I}}\longrightarrow L^{\mathcal{P}}V^{\mathcal{C}}.

which is a section of
\xi_{V}
in graded
\Bbbk
-modules.

By the universal property of the pullback, such a map
\zeta_{V}
extends to a cone of the restriction-extension diagram with source
V
.
Let us denote
\pi_{V}
the degree
0
endomorphism of the restriction-extension diagram defined as
\pi_{V}=\eta_{V}\circ\xi_{V}
. We can notice that the restriction of
\pi_{V}
onto
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
is a morphism of dg modules. Let us denote
p_{V}
the degree
0
endomorphism of the restriction-extension diagram defined as
\pi_{V}=\zeta_{V}\circ\xi_{V}
.

The coderivation on
W
Let
W\coloneqq\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
. The coderivation
D
on
W
may be decomposed as

D=D_{in}+D_{ex}.

On the one hand,
D_{ex}
is the degree
-1
endomorphism of the extension diagram given by the formula

D_{ex}=V^{d_{CP}};

it restricts to
W
but not to
\mathrm{R}_{1}
nor
\mathrm{R}_{2}
. On the other hand,
D_{in}
is the degree
-1
endomorphism of the restriction-extension diagram that is the sum of the following pre-differentials:

(1)

the pre-differential
D_{V}
which results from the differential on
V
and whose restriction to the extension diagram is
\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{V})^{\mathrm{E}}
,

(2)

the pre-differential
D_{CV}
induced by the structural map of
V
composed with the curved twisting morphism
\iota
, given by
V\longrightarrow V^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\longrightarrow V^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
,

(3)

the pre-differential
D_{C}
which results from the pre-differential on
\mathcal{C}
. Its restriction to the extension diagram is
V^{d_{C}}
,

(4)

the pre-differential
D_{\theta}
which results from the curvature of
\mathcal{C}
. Its restriction to the extension diagram is
V^{d_{\theta}}
,

(5)

the pre-differential
D_{\Delta}
which results from the structure of a cooperad on
\mathcal{C}
. Its restriction to the extension diagram is
V^{d_{\Delta}}
.

The contracting homotopy
H
.
Let
h
be the degree
1
endomorphism of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}=(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pl}}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
described in Section
5.2
. Remember that, as
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
consists in planar trees with nodes labelled by
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
and some top nodes by
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
,
h
consists in taking the leftest most top vertex of the planar tree and

(1)

applying to it the map

s^{-1}x\in s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\mapsto x\in\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}

if the label of this node belongs to
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
;

(2)

sending this labelled tree to
0
if the label of this node belongs to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
.

If the labelled tree is trivial (that is, it has no node), then
h
sends such a tree to zero.
As already shown in Section
5.2
,
h
is pictorially described by the map

s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(3)
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(2)
\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(1)
h
\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(3)
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(2)
\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(1)

One can extend
h
to
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
as follows in a such a way that it commutes with the maps
in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}.}

The graded
\mathbb N
-module

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}

consists in planar trees
t(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots)
whose node are labelled by
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}
and some top nodes are labelled
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}

together with a subtree
t^{\prime}
that contains the root but no node labelled
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
.
In that context, the extension of
h
to such pairs
(t(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots),t^{\prime})
consists in applying the former map
h
onto
t(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots)
and
removing from
t^{\prime}
the node that is relabelled if it belonged to it.

One can define precisely such an extension by induction on the height of the subtrees.
For convenience, let us denote
M=\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}
. Then,

(1)

On
