\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}M

h
is

\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}\circ h}\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}M.

(2)

On
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}M
,
h
is the sum of the map

\displaystyle s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}M\twoheadrightarrow s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}(\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{I})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}(\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}M

\displaystyle s^{-1}x

\displaystyle\mapsto x

with the map

\mathrm{Id}_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n)}\otimes\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\otimes\left(\pi_{\mathcal{I}\circ\mathcal{I}}^{\otimes k}\otimes h\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{M}^{\otimes n-k-1}\right).

(3)

Then on

(\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\mathrm{pl},\leq n+1}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}M\simeq s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}({\mathbb{T}}_{\mathrm{pl},\leq n+1}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}M

h
is given by
induction by
the sum of the map

\displaystyle(\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\mathrm{pl},\leq n+1}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}))\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}M\twoheadrightarrow s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}(\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{I})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}(\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}M

\displaystyle s^{-1}x

\displaystyle\mapsto x

with the map

\mathrm{Id}_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}(n)}\otimes\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\pi_{\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{I}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{I}}^{\otimes k}\otimes h\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}\circ_{\mathrm{pl}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}^{\otimes n-k-1}\right).

Definition 75

(
[
GL22
, Definition 11.16]
)
.

Let
H
be the degree
1
endomorphism of the extension diagram defined
as

H=-V^{h}.

Lemma 54

(
[
GL22
, Proposition 11.17]
)
.

The map
H
extends to the whole restriction extension-diagram.

Definition 76

(The garbage map)
.

Let
G
be the degree
0
endomorphism of the extension diagram given by the formula

G=D_{ex}H+HD_{ex}-\mathrm{Id}+p_{V}.

Lemma 55

(
[
GL22
, Proposition 11.17]
)
.

The maps
\partial_{ex}(H)=D_{ex}H+HD_{ex}
and
G
extend to the whole restriction-extension diagram.

Definition 77

(The boundary map)
.

Let
B
be the degree
0
endomorphism on the restriction-extension diagram
\mathrm{RE}

defined as

B=\partial_{ex}(H)+D_{in}H+HD_{in}+\pi_{V}=G+\mathrm{Id}+D_{in}H+HD_{in}+\pi_{V}-p_{V}.

Lemma 56
.

The restriction of
B
to
W=\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
is equal to
\partial(H)+\pi_{V}
.

Let
gB
be the degree
0
endomorphism on the restriction-extension diagram
\mathrm{RE}

defined as

gB=B+p_{V}-\pi_{V}=\mathrm{Id}+G+D_{in}H+HD_{in}.

Moreover, let
\overline{B}
be the degree
0
endomorphism on the restriction-extension diagram
\mathrm{RE}

defined as

\overline{B}=B-\pi_{V}=G+D_{in}H+HD_{in}-p_{V}.

The map
H
is a contracting homotopy.
Finally, we show that
H
is a contracting homotopy, which will allow us to conclude the proof of Lemma
52
.

Proposition 57
.

The maps
B
and
gB
are degree
0
automorphisms of the restriction-extension diagram
\mathrm{RE}
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Lemma
57
and Lemma
58
.
∎

Lemma 57
.

The maps
gB
and
B
are automorphisms of
\mathrm{E}_{1}
,
\mathrm{E}_{2}
and
\mathrm{R}_{1}
.

Proof.

Let us filter
\mathcal{P}
by the qp-filtration on
\mathcal{C}
. This induces a cofiltration on
\mathrm{E}_{1}
and on
\mathrm{R}_{1}
. On the associated graded object of this cofiltration,
\mathrm{gr}H=0
and
\mathrm{gr}(G)=0
and
\mathrm{gr}(\pi_{V})=\mathrm{gr}(p_{V})
. Thus
\mathrm{gr}(gB)=\mathrm{gr}(B)=\mathrm{Id}
. Subsequently,
gB
and
B
are automorphisms. For
\mathrm{E}_{2}
, one can filter
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}
in a similar fashion and obtain the same result.
∎

Lemma 58
.

The map
B
is an automorphism of
R_{2}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
GL22
, Lemma 11.28]
. The main difference
is the use of the "qp filtration".
One can filter
\mathcal{P}
from the qp filtration on
\mathcal{C}
. This induces a cofiltration
on
\mathrm{R}_{2}=\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\mathcal{P}}
that is preserved by
D_{in}
,
H
,
G
, and
\pi_{V}
.
Let us prove that on the associated graded object of this cofiltration, the map
\mathrm{gr}(B)
is an isomorphism.
The associated graded has the form

\mathrm{gr}(\mathrm{R}_{2})=\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\mathrm{gr}\mathcal{P}}=\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\mathrm{gr}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}}=\prod_{t}\left[t(s^{-1}\mathrm{gr}^{\mathrm{qp}}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}),\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\otimes l(t)}\right]\penalty\ ,

where the product is taken over planar trees
t
and where
l(t)
is the number of leaves of
t
.
On this graded object

(1)

p_{V}=\pi_{V}
, thus
gB=B
;

(2)

G
acts independently on each part of the product over planar trees
t

as

G_{t}=\left[\sum_{k=0}^{a-1}p_{V}^{\otimes k}\otimes G(\mathrm{R}_{1})\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{R}_{1}}^{\otimes n-1-k}\right]\penalty\ ,

where
a
is the number of the last leaf of the leftest top vertex of
t
(
1
if it is the first leaf);

(3)

similarly
H
acts independently on each part of the product over planar trees
t

as

H_{t}=\left[\mathrm{Id},\sum_{k=0}^{a-1}p_{V}^{\otimes k}\otimes H(\mathrm{R}_{1})\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{R}_{1}}^{\otimes n-1-k}\right]\penalty\ ,

where again
a
is the number of the last leaf of the leftest top vertex of
t
;

(4)

D_{in}
is given by the formula

\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},D_{in}(\mathrm{R}_{1}))^{\mathrm{gr}\mathcal{P}}-\left[d_{C}+d_{\Delta},\mathrm{Id}\right]\penalty\ .

For every natural integer
n
,

\mathrm{gr}_{n}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}
is made up planar trees labelled
by
s^{-1}\mathrm{gr}^{\mathrm{qp}}\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pl}}}
such that the sum
of the grading degree over the whole tree is
n
; in particular, such a tree
has at most
n
nodes. One can furthermore filter
\mathrm{gr}_{n}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{pl}}

by the opposite of the number of nodes in trees. On the associated graded object

\mathrm{gr}\mathrm{gr}_{n}\mathcal{P}
, the pre-differential
d_{\Delta}
disappears.

This filtration of
\mathrm{gr}_{n}\mathcal{P}
induces a cofiltration of
\mathrm{gr}^{n}\mathrm{R}_{2}

whose associated graded object is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{-m}\mathrm{gr}^{n}\mathrm{R}_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\mathrm{gr}_{-m}\mathrm{gr}_{n}\mathcal{P}}

\displaystyle\cong\prod_{t}\bigoplus_{i_{1}+\cdots+i_{m}=n}\left[s^{-1}\mathrm{gr}^{\mathrm{\mathrm{qp}}}_{i_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n_{1}(t))\otimes\cdots\otimes s^{-1}\mathrm{gr}^{\mathrm{qp}}_{i_{m}}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{pl}}(n_{m}(t)),\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\otimes l(t)}\right]\penalty\ ,

where
0\leq m\leq n
and where the product is taken over the planar trees
t
with
m
-nodes whose number of inputs are
n_{1}(t),\ldots,n_{m}(t)
. Let us denote by
l(t)
the number of leaves of
t
.

On this a multi-graded object,
D_{in}
,
H
and
G
are still given by the same formula on each tree as above;
but now,
[d_{\Delta},\mathrm{Id}]
is zero. Besides,
[d_{C},\mathrm{Id}]
acts independently on each tree and thus commutes with
H
.
For every planar tree with
m
-nodes
t
, let us denote
D_{in,t}
be the degree
-1
endomorphism of the
t
part of the above product
that is given by the formula

D_{in,t}=\left[\mathrm{Id},\sum_{k=0}^{l(t)-1}\mathrm{Id}^{k}\otimes D_{in}(\mathrm{R}_{1})\otimes\mathrm{Id}^{\otimes l(t)-k-1}\right]\penalty\ .

Since
[d_{\mathcal{C}},\mathrm{Id}]
commutes with
H
on
\mathrm{gr}^{n}\mathrm{E}_{2}
, one has on
\mathrm{gr}^{-m}\mathrm{gr}^{n}\mathrm{R}_{2}
that

D_{in}H+HD_{in}=\prod_{t}D_{in,t}H_{t}+H_{t}D_{in,t}\penalty\ .

Let us prove that for every planar tree
t
, the map
\mathrm{Id}+G_{t}+D_{in,t}H_{t}+H_{t}D_{in,t}

is an isomorphism of the
t
part of
\mathrm{gr}^{m}\mathrm{gr}^{n}\mathrm{R}_{2}
.
Again we denote
l(t)
the number of leaves of
t
and
a

the number of leaves of
t
that are before the leftest top node or on top of its node; thus
l(t)=a+b
where
b
is the
number of leaves after those of the leftest top node. The case
a=0
is clear as then
\mathrm{Id}+G_{t}+D_{in,t}H_{t}+H_{t}D_{in,t}=\mathrm{Id}
.
Let us tackle the case
a>0
.
We can filter
\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\otimes l(t)}
in a finite way as follows

\displaystyle F_{0}\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\otimes l(t)}

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathrm{R}_{1}}\otimes\mathrm{R}_{1}^{l(t)-1}

\displaystyle F_{1}\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\otimes l(t)}

\displaystyle=(F_{0}\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\otimes l(t)})\oplus V\otimes\overline{\mathrm{R}_{1}}\otimes\mathrm{R}_{1}^{l(t)-2}

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle F_{a}\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\otimes l(t)}

\displaystyle=F_{a-1}\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\otimes l(t)}\oplus V^{\otimes a}\otimes\mathrm{R}_{1}^{l(t)-a}.

The associated graded object is

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{k}\mathrm{R}_{1}^{\otimes l(t)}

\displaystyle=V^{\otimes k}\otimes\overline{\mathrm{R}_{1}}\otimes\mathrm{R}_{1}^{l(t)-k-1},\quad 0\leq k\leq a-1\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{a}

\displaystyle=V^{\otimes a}\otimes\mathrm{R}_{1}^{l(t)-a}.

Such a filtration induces a filtration on the
t
part of
of
\mathrm{gr}^{-m}\mathrm{gr}^{n}\mathrm{R}_{2}
that is preserved
by
D_{in,t},G_{t},H_{t}
. On the
k
-th layer of the associated graded object

\mathrm{gr}_{k}(\mathrm{Id}+G_{t}+D_{in,t}H_{t}+H_{t}D_{in,t})=\left[\mathrm{Id},\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{R}_{1}}^{\otimes k}\otimes gB(\mathrm{R}_{1})\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{R}_{1}}^{\otimes l(t)-k-1}\right]\penalty\ ,

if
k<a
and

\mathrm{gr}_{a}(\mathrm{Id}+G_{t}+D_{in,t}H_{t}+H_{t}D_{in,t})=\mathrm{Id}\penalty\ ,

otherwise. In both case, it is an isomorphism. Thus
\mathrm{gr}(\mathrm{Id}+G_{t}+D_{in,t}H_{t}+H_{t}D_{in,t})
is an isomorphism
and so is
\mathrm{Id}+G_{t}+D_{in,t}H_{t}+H_{t}D_{in,t}
. Subsequently

B
is an isomorphism on
\mathrm{gr}^{-m}\mathrm{gr}^{n}\mathrm{R}_{2}

for every
n\in\mathbb N,m\leq n
. Therefore
B
is an automorphism of
\mathrm{R}_{2}
.
∎

7.2.
Cofibrant objects

Cofibrant complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras admit a simple description: they are exactly those whose underlying graded
\mathcal{C}
-algebra is free.

Lemma 59
.

The functor
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}
from dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras to curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras commutes with finite cosifted limits.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that cosifted limits in dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras and in curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras are computed in graded
\Bbbk
-modules and that the endofunctor of graded
\Bbbk
-modules
(-)^{\mathcal{C}}
preserves cosifted limits.
∎

Remark 56
.

From the above fact one can deduce that the adjunction
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\dashv\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}
is bimonadic. See the upcoming article
[
Gri23
]
for more details.

Proposition 58
.

Let
\Lambda
be a qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. The following assertions are equivalent.

(1)

\Lambda
is cofibrant;

(2)

\Lambda
is in the essential image of the functor
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}
;

(3)

\Lambda
is a quasi-free
\mathcal{C}
-algebra that is, its underlying graded algebra is free.

Proof.

For every free graded
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
\Lambda\cong X^{\mathcal{C}}
, the data of a degree
-1
derivation is equivalent to the data of a degree
-1
map
X\longrightarrow X^{\mathcal{C}}
by restricting it to the generators
X
. In turn, any map
X\longrightarrow X^{\mathcal{C}}
is equivalent to a degree
-1
map
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathrm{coEnd}(X)
. Therefore the following data are equivalent.

(1)

A degree
-1
derivation
d
of
\Lambda
.

(2)

A morphism of graded operads
\varphi_{d}:\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathrm{coEnd}(X)
, which induces a graded
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra structure on
X
.

A straightforward computation then shows that the derivation
d
is
curved
, that is,
d^{2}=-\gamma_{\Lambda}(\Lambda^{\theta})
, if and only if
\varphi_{d}
is a morphism of dg operads. This proves the equivalence between assertion
2
and assertion
3
.

Furthermore, we already know that a qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra that is in the essential image of the functor
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}
is cofibrant.

Now, let
\Lambda
be a cofibrant object in qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Let us prove that it is quasi-free. Since
\Lambda
is cofibrant, the counit map
\epsilon_{B}:\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda\longrightarrow\Lambda
which is an acyclic fibration has a section
s
. Let
V
be the equaliser of the coreflexive pair of maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}}

in the category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. By Lemma
59
, the limit of the diagram of curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta_{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\Lambda})}

is
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
. It is also clear that this limit is
\Lambda
. So one has a canonical isomorphism
\Lambda\cong\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
.
∎

7.3.
Infinity morphisms

The notion of
\infty
-morphism extends the usual notion of morphisms of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. Their main advantage is that
\infty
-quasi-isomorphisms are invertible, therefore one can replace a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras with two inverse
\infty
-quasi-isomorphism. This provides a powerful tool to describe the homotopy category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Recall that for every dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
V
, the unit
\eta_{V}:V\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
admits a left-inverse
\xi_{V}:\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V\longrightarrow V
in the category of dg modules. Let
K
be the kernel of this map, we get a decomposition of dg modules

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V=V\oplus K.

Definition 78

(
\infty
-morphism)
.

Let
V,V^{\prime}
be two dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. An
\infty
-morphism

f:V\rightsquigarrow V^{\prime}
amounts to the data of, equivalently:

(1)

a morphism
f:V\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}
of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras,

(2)

a morphism
f^{\dagger}:\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V\longrightarrow\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}
of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Linear part.
Let
f:V\rightsquigarrow V^{\prime}
be an
\infty
-morphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. Its
linear part

f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is the morphism of dg modules given by the following composition:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{V}}

Let us denote
\epsilon:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
and
\mu:\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
the counit and the coaugmentation of the quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
, respectively. The linear part of
f_{\mathrm{dg}}:V\rightsquigarrow V^{\prime}
is equivalently given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V)^{\epsilon}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V^{\prime})^{\mu}}

Homotopy part.
Let
f:V\rightsquigarrow V^{\prime}
be an
\infty
-morphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. Its
homotopy part

f_{\mathrm{h}}
is the morphism of dg modules given by the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{h}}:V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
K
is the kernel of the map
\xi_{V}:\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V\longrightarrow V
. Equivalently, it is given by the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{h}}:V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V)^{\epsilon}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\dagger}}

where
L
is the kernel of the map
(\mathrm{Id})^{\mu}:\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V\twoheadrightarrow V
induced by the coaugmentation of
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 79

(
\infty
-quasi-isomorphism)
.

An
\infty
-morphism
f:V\rightsquigarrow V^{\prime}
is called an
\infty
-
quasi-isomorphism
if its linear part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a quasi-isomorphism of dg modules.

Definition 80

(
\infty
-isotopy)
.

An
\infty
-morphism
f:V\rightsquigarrow V
is called an
\infty
-
isotopy
if its linear part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is an identity map of
V
.

Lemma 60
.

Let
f^{\dagger}:\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V\longrightarrow\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}
be an
\infty
-morphism. If
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a cofibration, then
f^{\dagger}
is the composition of a strict cofibration, that is the image through
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}
of a cofibration between dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, followed by an
\infty
-isotopy.

Proof.

Let
p:V^{\prime}\longrightarrow V
be a left inverse of
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules that is, a map so that
pf_{\mathrm{dg}}=\mathrm{Id}
. We consider the following degree
0
map
\tau:V^{\prime}\longrightarrow(V^{\prime})^{\mathcal{C}}
, defined by
\tau_{\mathrm{dg}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Id}_{V^{\prime}}
and by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\mathrm{h}}\coloneqq V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V^{\prime})^{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{h}}}

It induces a morphism
t
of the graded
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t:(V^{\prime})^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left((V^{\prime})^{\mathcal{C}}\right)^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V^{\prime})^{\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V^{\prime})^{\mathcal{C}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V)^{\Delta}}

where
\Delta:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{C}
is the decomposition of the cooperad
\mathcal{C}
. Notice that
t
is an isomorphism since
\mathrm{gr}^{\mathrm{qp}}(t)=\mathrm{Id}
. We have the equality of morphism of graded
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

t(\mathcal{C}\circ f_{\mathrm{dg}})=f^{\dagger}.

Let us denote
D
the derivation of
(V^{\prime})^{\mathcal{C}}
coming from the dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra structure of
V^{\prime}
and let

\tilde{D}\coloneqq t^{-1}Dt.

This is a derivation of
(V^{\prime})^{\mathcal{C}}
that makes it a complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. Thus it defines the structure of a dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra on
V^{\prime}
such that
t:V^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow V^{\prime}
becomes a
\infty
-isotopy between
(V^{\prime},\tilde{D})
to
(V^{\prime},D)
and thus
\mathcal{C}\circ f_{\mathrm{dg}}
becomes a strict cofibration from
V
to
(V^{\prime},\tilde{D})
.
∎

Proposition 59
.

Let
f:V\rightsquigarrow V^{\prime}
be an
\infty
-morphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. The morphism of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras
f^{\dagger}:\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V\longrightarrow\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}W
is

(1)

a weak-equivalence if and only if the dg part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a quasi-isomorphism;

(2)

an isomorphism if and only if the dg part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is an isomorphism;

(3)

a fibration if and only if the dg part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a degree-wise epimorphism;

(4)

a cofibration if and only if the dg part
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a degree-wise injection.

Proof.

Let us prove these assertions.

(1)

Let us consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(f^{\dagger})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{dg}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in the category of dg modules. The two horizontal maps,
\xi_{V}
and
\xi_{V^{\prime}}
, are quasi-isomorphisms. Thus, the left vertical map is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if the right vertical map is a quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

If
f^{\dagger}
is an isomorphism, then
f_{\mathrm{dg}}=\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{\mathrm{qp}}(f^{\dagger})
is an isomorphism. Conversely, if
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is an isomorphism, then

\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(f^{\dagger})=(f_{\mathrm{dg}})^{\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{qp}}(\mathcal{C})}}

is an isomorphism. Hence,
f^{\dagger}
is an isomorphism.

(3)

Let us suppose that
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a degree-wise epimorphism. Then, it has a section
g
in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules. Let us consider the endomorphism
h
of graded
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V^{\prime})^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V)^{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V^{\prime})^{\mathcal{C}}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g)^{\mathrm{Id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\dagger}}

Its linear part is the identity of
V^{\prime}
. The same arguments as those used to prove point (2) show that
h
is a graded isomorphism. In particular, it is a degree-wise epimorphism. So
f^{\dagger}
is a degree-wise epimorphism, hence a fibration.

Conversely, suppose that
f^{\dagger}
is a fibration. Let us consider the same commutative square diagram shown in point (1). The right vertical map
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(f^{\dagger})
and the bottom horizontal map of this square are degree-wise epimorphisms. Thus the right vertical map
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is also a degree-wise epimorphism.

(4)

One has a functor from dg modules to qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras that sends a dg module
X
to
X
equipped with the trivial complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra structure. This structure is given by the zero structural map
0:X^{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}\longrightarrow X
.

This functor sends acyclic fibrations of dg modules to filtered quasi-isomorphisms that are also degree-wise epimorphisms. They are in particular acyclic fibrations of complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. If
f^{\dagger}
is a cofibration, then it has the left lifting property with respect to all acyclic fibrations, and in particular with respect to such acyclic fibrations of dg modules.
As a consequence of the fact that a square of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\dagger}}

(where
X,Y
are chain complexes equipped with the zero structure of a qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra)
amounts to the data of a square chain complexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{dg}}}

the map
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
has the left lifting property with respect to every acyclic fibration of dg modules. So it is a cofibration of dg modules that is, a degree-wise injection. Conversely, if
f_{\mathrm{dg}}
is a cofibration, then
f^{\dagger}
is a cofibration as a direct consequence of Lemma
60
.

∎

Proposition 60
.

Let
V
and
V^{\prime}
be two dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. There exists a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

V\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\cdot\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\cdot\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\cdots\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\cdot\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}V^{\prime}\penalty\ ,

if and only if there exist
\infty
-quasi-isomorphisms
V\rightsquigarrow V^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow V
which are inverse to each other in homology.

Proof.

Given any zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms between
V
and
V^{\prime}
we get a zig-zag of weak-equivalences between
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
and
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}
. All the objects in the zig-zag are fibrant and cofibrant, therefore they admit homotopy inverses, and we have two homotopy inverse arrows between
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V
and
\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}
. Conversely, if there is a
\infty
-quasi-isomorphism
f^{\dagger}:\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}
, we get the following zig-zag

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}(f^{\dagger})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

∎

7.4.
Homotopy transfer theorem for coalgebras

The goal of this subsection is to prove a homotopy transfer theorem for dg coalgebras over a cofibrant dg operad. The idea is the same as in the algebra case. First, we show that given a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
and a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
V
together with a decomposition
V\cong K\oplus X
, where
K
is acyclic, we can "perturb" the dg
\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra structure on
V
so that this new structure "projects" onto
X
. Then, we give an homotopical meaning to this construction in cases of interest.

7.4.1.
Technicalities

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. As in Section
5.5
, we denote
M
the graded
\mathbb S
-module
\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}
:

M=\mathcal{P}\oplus s\mathcal{I}.

Again, we have a canonical isomorphism of conilpotent graded cooperads
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}=\mathbb{T}(sM)
.

Notation
.

Let
Y
and
Z
be graded modules and let

a:Y\longrightarrow Z^{{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}

be a graded map. For every natural integer
n\geq 1
, we will denote by
a_{\leq n}
the composition

a_{\leq n}:Y\xrightarrow{a}Z^{{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\twoheadrightarrow Z^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n}(sM)}\penalty\ .

We will also denote
\overline{a}
the composition of
a
with the projection
Z^{{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\twoheadrightarrow Z^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}
. We can combine these notations. For instance,
\overline{a}_{\leq n}
refers to the composition of
a
with the projection
Z^{{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\twoheadrightarrow Z^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n}(sM)}
.

Let us also consider a direct sum of dg modules

V=X\oplus K

where
K
is acyclic. Let us choose a contracting homotopy
h
of
K
that we can extend to all
V
by
0
on
X
. Let us denote
\pi_{X},\pi_{K}
the projections endomorphisms of
V
onto respectively
X
and
K
. All these arrows satisfy the following equations:

\left\{\hbox to109.73pt{\vbox to74pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 54.86368pt\lower-37.00002pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-54.86368pt}{-31.84029pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right.

Let us suppose that the dg module
(V,d_{V})
is endowed with a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra structure

\Delta_{V}:V\longrightarrow V^{\mathcal{P}}.

Notice that it induces a dg
\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra structure on
V
by pulling back along the map
\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
.

Definition 81
.

Let
D_{\delta}
be the degree
-1
derivation on the graded
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebra
V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}
that induced by the
\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra structure
\Delta_{V}
on the dg module
V
, in the sense that

(V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}},D_{\delta})\cong\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}V.

Moreover, let
\delta
be the composition of
D_{\delta}
with the inclusion of
V
inside
V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}
. One can notice that the projection of
\delta
onto
V
is the differential
d_{V}
and that its projection onto
V^{\overline{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}}
is given by

V\xrightarrow{\Delta}V^{\mathcal{P}}\xrightarrow{-V^{s}}V^{s\mathcal{P}}\hookrightarrow V^{sM}\hookrightarrow V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}=V^{\overline{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}}\penalty\ ,

where the second map
-V^{s}
sends a sequence to a sequence

(\phi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb N}\in\prod_{n}[\mathcal{P}(n),V^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb S_{n}}\mapsto(\psi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb N}\in\prod_{n}[s\mathcal{P}(n),V^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb S_{n}}\penalty\ ,

and where each
\psi_{n}
is defined as

s\mathcal{P}(n)\xrightarrow{sx\mapsto-x}\mathcal{P}(n)\xrightarrow{\phi_{n}}V^{\otimes n}.

The degree
0
map

V\xrightarrow{-h}V\xrightarrow{\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}}V^{sM}

yields a morphism of graded
\mathbb S
-modules
sM\longrightarrow\mathrm{coEnd}(V)
. Therefore there is a morphism of graded operads
\mathbb{T}(sM)\longrightarrow\mathrm{coEnd}(V)
, which in turn yields a degree
0
map of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

\phi:V\longrightarrow V^{\mathbb{T}{sM}}=V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}.

Let
f
be the related morphism of qp-complete graded
B\mathcal{P}
-algebras

f:V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\xrightarrow{\phi^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}}(V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}})^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\hookrightarrow V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\circ\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}.

One can notice that the composition of
\phi
with the projection onto
V
is the identity and that

\overline{\phi}=f\overline{\phi}_{\leq 1}.

Definition 82
.

Let
\chi
be the degree
-1
morphism from
V
to
V^{\mathbb{T}(sM)}

defined as
\chi\coloneqq f\overline{\delta}
. Notice that
\phi=\mathrm{Id}_{V}-\chi h
.

Recall that the conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
is given by the conilpotent graded cooperad
\mathbb{T}(sM)
endowed with the following pre-differentials:

(1)

the pre-differential
d_{\gamma}
which is induced by the operad structure of
\mathcal{P}
;

(2)

the pre-differential
d_{\mathcal{P}}
which is induced by the differential of
\mathcal{P}
;

(3)

the pre-differential
d_{u}
, which maps
s^{2}\mathcal{I}
to the unit of
\mathcal{P}
.

We will denote the later two pre-differentials by
d_{sM}
, as they are defined on the generators of
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
. We refer to Subsection
2.1
for more details.

Notation
.

We denote by
d_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{root}}
the degree
-1
endomorphism of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
given by applying
d_{\gamma}
only to inner edges of the trees of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)
that touch the root node. We denote by
d_{\gamma}^{\neq\mathrm{root}}
the other component of
d_{\gamma}
, given by applying
d_{\gamma}
to all the other edges that do not touch the root.

Similarly, we denote by
d_{sM}^{\mathrm{root}}
and
d_{sM}^{\neq\mathrm{root}}
the degree
-1
endomorphisms given by applying
d_{sM}
to the root node of a tree (resp. all the other nodes).

Lemma 61
.

The following diagram in the category of graded
\Bbbk
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{sM}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(V^{\mathbb{T}_{\leq 1}(sM)}\right)^{sM}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\phi}_{\leq 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\shuffle(\phi,\chi)^{sM}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{d_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{root}}}}

is commutative.

Proof.

A straightforward check shows these two maps from
V
to
V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq 2}(sM)}
correspond to the same
\mathbb S_{n}
-equivariant maps from
V\otimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq 2}(sM)(n)
to
V^{\otimes n}
.
∎

Lemma 62
.

The map
f{\delta}=f{\delta}_{\leq 1}:V\longrightarrow V^{{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}
is equal to
(\phi\penalty\ d_{V})+{\chi}
.

Proof.

This follows from the equation
\delta={\delta}_{\leq 1}=d_{V}+\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}
.
∎

Definition 83
.

Let
\zeta
be the degree
-1
map from
V
to
V^{B\mathcal{P}}

whose projection onto to
V\cong V^{\mathcal{I}}
is
d_{V}
and whose projection onto

V^{\overline{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}}
is the sum of the two maps

\displaystyle V\xrightarrow{\pi_{X}}V\xrightarrow{\chi}V^{\overline{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}};

\displaystyle V\xrightarrow{h}V\xrightarrow{V^{\theta}}V^{sM}\xrightarrow{f}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}.

Moreover, let
D_{\zeta}
be the unique degree
-1
derivation on
the graded
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebra
V^{{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}}
whose restriction to
V
is
\zeta
.

Lemma 63
.

The degree
-1
map
f\delta-\zeta:V\longrightarrow V^{{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}

is equal to the sum of the two maps

\displaystyle V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}_{\leq 1}}V^{sM}\xrightarrow{-V^{d_{sM}}}V^{sM}\xrightarrow{f}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}

\displaystyle V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}_{\leq 1}}V^{sM}\xrightarrow{\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{V})^{sM}}V^{sM}\xrightarrow{f}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}

where
d_{sM}
denotes the restriction of the coderivation of
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
to
sM
.

Proof.

The projection of the two maps onto
V
are both equal to
0
. Let us prove that their projection onto
V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}
are equal.
Let us first notice that

\overline{f}d_{V}=\overline{\phi}d_{V}=\overline{f}\overline{\phi}_{\leq 1}d_{V}=-\overline{f}\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}hd_{V}

Then, the first map rewrites as

\displaystyle\overline{f}\delta-\overline{\zeta}

\displaystyle=\overline{f}(d_{V}+\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}-\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}\pi_{X}-V^{\theta}h)

\displaystyle=\overline{f}\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}(-hd_{V}+\mathrm{Id}-\pi_{X})-\overline{f}V^{\theta}h

\displaystyle=\overline{f}(\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}d_{V}h-V^{\theta}h).

Noticing that

\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}d_{V}=\overline{(D_{\delta})}_{\leq 1}\delta-\left(\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{V})^{sM}-V^{d_{sM}}\right)\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}=V^{\theta}-\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{V})^{sM}\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}+V^{d_{sM}}\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}

we get

\displaystyle\overline{f}\delta-\overline{\zeta}

\displaystyle=\overline{f}(V^{\theta}-\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{V})^{sM}\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}+V^{d_{sM}}\overline{\delta}_{\leq 1}-V^{\theta})h

\displaystyle=\overline{f}(\shuffle(\mathrm{Id},d_{V})^{sM}\overline{\phi}_{\leq 1}-V^{d_{sM}}\overline{\phi}_{\leq 1}).

∎

Proposition 61
.

One has an equality of degree
-1
maps from
V
to
V^{B\mathcal{P}}

D_{\zeta}\phi=f\delta.

Thus
D_{\zeta}f=fD_{\delta}
.

Proof.

Let us prove the result on the height of the trees that make
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}=\mathbb{T}(sM)
. More precisely, let us prove that for every natural integer
n

D_{\zeta,\leq n}\phi=f_{\leq n}\delta.

First, for
n=0

D_{\zeta,\leq 0}\phi=d_{V}=f_{\leq 0}\delta

Let us assume that the equality is verifies for some natural integer
n
. The map
\overline{D_{\zeta,\leq n+1}}\phi
is given on larger trees
V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n+1}(sM)}
as the following sum of maps

\displaystyle V\xrightarrow{\phi_{\leq 0}=\mathrm{Id}_{V}}V\xrightarrow{\overline{\zeta}_{\leq n+1}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n+1}(sM)}\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\xrightarrow{\shuffle(\mathrm{Id}_{V},\zeta)^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}}(V^{{\mathbb{T}}(sM)})^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\to V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\twoheadrightarrow V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n+1}(sM)}\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\xrightarrow{-V^{d_{sM}^{\mathrm{root}}}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\twoheadrightarrow V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n+1}(sM)}\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\xrightarrow{-V^{d_{sM}^{\neq\mathrm{root}}}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\twoheadrightarrow V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n+1}(sM)}\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\xrightarrow{-V^{d_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{root}}}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\twoheadrightarrow V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n+1}(sM)}\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\xrightarrow{-V^{d_{\gamma}^{\neq\mathrm{root}}}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\twoheadrightarrow V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n+1}(sM)}\penalty\ ,

where the four last map are actually equal to the composition

V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}V^{\overline{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}}\xrightarrow{-V^{d_{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}}}V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}(sM)}\twoheadrightarrow V^{\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\leq n+1}(sM)}\penalty\ .

We can the notice that the first map is just
\overline{\zeta}_{\leq n+1}
and that the sum of the second map, the fourth map and the sixth map is the composition

V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}_{\leq 1}}V^{sM}\xrightarrow{\shuffle(\phi_{\leq n},D_{\zeta,\leq n}\phi)^{sM}}(V^{\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)})^{sM}.

By the induction hypothesis,
D_{\zeta,\leq n}\phi=f_{\leq n}\delta
and by Lemma
62

f\delta=\phi d_{V}+\chi
. So this composition is equal to the composition

V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}_{\leq 1}}V^{sM}\xrightarrow{\shuffle(\phi_{\leq n},\phi_{\leq n}d_{V}+\chi_{\leq n})^{sM}}(V^{\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)})^{sM}.

using Lemma
61
, its sum with the fifth map is

V\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}_{\leq 1}}V^{sM}\xrightarrow{\shuffle(\phi_{\leq n},\phi_{\leq n}d_{V})^{sM}}(V^{\mathbb{T}_{\leq n}(sM)})^{sM}.

To conclude Lemma
63
tells that the sum of all the six maps is equal to
\overline{f}_{\leq n+1}\delta
.
∎

Proposition 62
.

The derivation
D_{\zeta}
makes
V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}
a qp-complete curved
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra.

Proof.

Since
D_{\zeta}f=fD_{\delta}f
and since
f
is an isomorphism (by a standard filtration argument):

D_{\zeta}=fD_{\delta}f^{-1}.

Thus, the derivation
D_{\zeta}
makes
V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}
a curved
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebra because so does the derivation
D_{\delta}
.
∎

Proposition 63
.

The derivation
D_{\zeta}
projects onto the quotient graded
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebra
X^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}
in the sense that there exists a (necessarily unique) derivation on
X^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}
, also denoted
D_{\zeta}
, such that the projection map

V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\twoheadrightarrow X^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}

commutes with the derivations. In particular,
(V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}},D_{\zeta})
is a curved
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebra.

Proof.

Actually,
X^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}
is the quotient/kernel in
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebras of the idempotent endomorphism
\pi_{X}^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}
on
V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}
. One can notice that the restriction to
V
of
\pi_{X}^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\penalty\ D_{\zeta}\penalty\ \pi_{X}^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}
and
\pi_{X}^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}D_{\zeta}
are equal:

\pi_{X}^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\penalty\ \zeta\penalty\ \pi_{X}=\pi_{X}^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\penalty\ \zeta.

Thus

\pi_{X}^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\penalty\ D_{\zeta}\penalty\ \pi_{X}^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}=\pi_{X}^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\penalty\ D_{\zeta}

which proves the result.
∎

To conclude, we have a composition of morphisms of qp-complete curved
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebras

(V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}},D_{\delta})\xrightarrow{f}(V^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}},D_{\zeta})\twoheadrightarrow(X^{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}},D_{\zeta}).

7.4.2.
The cooperad version of the homotopy transfer theorem for coalgebras

Theorem 12
.

Let
p:V\twoheadrightarrow X
be an acyclic fibration of dg modules and let
\Delta_{V}:V\longrightarrow V^{\Omega\mathcal{C}}
be a dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra structure on
V
. There exists another dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra structure

\zeta_{V}:V\longrightarrow V^{\Omega\mathcal{C}}

which projects onto
X
, together with an
\infty
-isotopy

(V,\Delta_{V})\rightsquigarrow(V,\zeta_{V})

of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Proof.

Since
p
is an acylic fibration of dg modules, it has a section
i
and one can decompose
V
as
X\oplus K
where
K
is the kernel of
p
. The paragraph just above gives us a diagram of qp-complete curved
\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras

(V^{\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}},D_{\delta})\xrightarrow{f}(V^{\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}},D_{\zeta})\twoheadrightarrow(X^{\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}},D_{\zeta}).

Applying the left adjoint functor from qp-complete
\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras to qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra that results from the unit map
\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{B}\Omega\mathcal{C}
, we get diagram of curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

(V^{\mathcal{C}},D_{\delta})\xrightarrow{\tilde{f}}(V^{\mathcal{C}},D_{\zeta})\twoheadrightarrow(X^{\mathcal{C}},D_{\zeta}).

In that context,
D_{\zeta}
is the derivation on
V^{\mathcal{C}}
that induces the expected dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra structure
\zeta_{V}
on
V
and
\tilde{f}
is the expected
\infty
-isotopy.
∎

7.4.3.
The homotopy transfer theorem for coalgebras

Theorem 13
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be a cofibrant dg operad, let
p:V\twoheadrightarrow X
be an acyclic fibration of dg modules and let
\Delta_{V}:V\longrightarrow V^{\mathcal{Q}}
a dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebra structure on
V
. There exists a dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebra structure
\zeta_{X}
on
X
, together with a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms

(V,\Delta_{V})\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\cdots\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}(X,\zeta_{X})

of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebras. Furthermore, the maps in this zig-zag are homotopic to
p
in the model category of dg modules.

Proof.

Taking
\mathcal{C}
to be the quasi-planar conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{E})
, Theorem
12
yields a dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra structure on
X
together with a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras

(V,\Delta_{V})\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,\zeta_{X})\xleftarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}(X,\zeta_{X}).

Moreover, the acyclic fibration of dg operads
\Omega\mathcal{C}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
has a section since
\mathcal{Q}
is cofibrant. Applying the related left adjoint forgetful functor from dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras to dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebras yields the expected zig-zag.
∎

Remark 57
.

This last result also follows from model-categorical arguments, as developed in
[
Fre10
]
.

7.5.
Further localisations and divided powers operations

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be an coadmissible dg operad and let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. Let us consider a morphism of dg operads
f:\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
. We have two Quillen adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{Q}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Let us denote
\widehat{\Omega}_{f}
the composite left adjoint and
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{f}
the composite right adjoint.

Proposition 64
.

There exists a combinatorial model structure on qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras, called the
f
-model structure, transferred from that of dg
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebras, determined by the following sets of morphisms

(1)

the set of
f
-fibrations is given by morphisms
g
such that
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{f}(g)
is a fibration,

(2)

the set of
f
-weak-equivalences is given by morphisms
g
such that
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{f}(g)
is a weak equivalence.

(3)

the set of
f
-cofibrations is determined by left-lifting property against all acyclic fibrations.

Moreover, this is a right Bousfield localisation of the canonical model structure transferred from dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. Meaning that the identity functor of qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebra, where at the source they are endowed with the canonical model structure, and at the target with the
f
-model structure, is a right Quillen functor.

Proof.

Any fibration and weak-equivalence in model structure transferred from dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras is an
f
-fibration and an
f
-weak-equivalence. Hence, every object is fibrant and a natural path object is given by Proposition
56
. This proves the existence of the transferred model structure. To prove that this is a right Bousfield localisation of that transferred from dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, it suffices to notice that fibrations are in particular degree-wise epimorphisms, in a similar way as shown in Propostion
52
.
∎

Localizing at quasi-isomorphisms.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent
differential graded
cooperad. The cobar construction
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is augmented since
\mathcal{C}
has zero curvature. Let us denote
\nu:\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
the canonical morphism of dg operads given by the augmentation. We have the following adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \text{-}\mathsf{mod}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Prim}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Triv}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

where the adjunction
\nu^{*}\dashv\nu_{!}
is in fact given by the primitive elements functor
\mathrm{Prim}
(which is
\nu_{!}
) and by the trivial structure functor
\mathrm{Triv}
(which is
\nu^{*}
). Notice that since
\mathcal{C}
has zero curvature, curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras in pdg modules are precisely given by dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proposition 65
.

The set of
\nu
-weak-equivalences is precisely the set of quasi-isomorphisms of qp-complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proof.

The composition
\mathrm{Prim}\penalty\ \widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}
is isomorphic to the forgetful functor from qp-complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras to dg modules.
∎

Corollary 11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent dg cooperad. The set of weak-equivalences in the canonical model structure on dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras is contained in the set of quasi-isomorphims.

Proof.

It suffices to apply Proposition
64
to the morphism of dg operads
\nu:\Omega\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
, combining it with Proposition
65
.
∎

Divided power operations in the homotopical setting.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent dg cooperad. By Proposition
64
, the category of qp-complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras admits a model category structure where

(1)

the set of fibrations is given by degree-wise epimorphisms;

(2)

the set of weak-equivalences is given by quasi-isomorphisms;

(3)

the set of cofibrations is given by maps with left lifting property with respect to acyclic fibrations.

Let
(\Lambda,\gamma_{\Lambda},d_{\Lambda})
be a qp-complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. The structural map

\gamma_{\Lambda}:\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),\Lambda^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb S_{n}}\longrightarrow\Lambda\penalty\ ,

comes from the invariants on the left-hand side, therefore divided power operations should appear. Nevertheless, since
\mathcal{C}
is quasi-planar, there is a natural isomorphism

\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),\Lambda^{\otimes n}]_{\mathbb S_{n}}\cong\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),\Lambda^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb S_{n}}\penalty\ ,

of dg modules induced by the norm map (Proposition
2
). Therefore no divided power operations appear at the algebraic level.

These divided power operations do not disappear at the
\infty
-categorical level. The reason is that
\mathcal{C}(n)
is a quasi-free dg
\Bbbk[\mathbb{S}_{n}]
-module, which is furthermore
projective
by Proposition
10
. Therefore we have that

\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),\Lambda^{\otimes n}]_{h\mathbb S_{n}}\not\simeq\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),\Lambda^{\otimes n}]_{\mathbb S_{n}}\cong\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),\Lambda^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb S_{n}}\simeq\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),\Lambda^{\otimes n}]^{h\mathbb S_{n}}\penalty\ ,

where on the upmost left-hand side we consider
homotopy coinvariants
and on the upmost right-hand side we consider
homotopy invariants
. Indeed, the dg
\Bbbk[\mathbb{S}_{n}]
-module
[\mathcal{C}(n),\Lambda^{\otimes n}]
is automatically cofibrant in the injective model structure by Proposition
3
, since
\mathcal{C}(n)
is projective. This means that the
\infty
-category of qp-complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms behaves like an
\infty
-category of algebraic objects with
divided power operations
.

8.
Linear duality

The two bar-cobar Quillen adjunctions constructed so far are intertwined by linear duality adjunctions: they form a commuting square of Quillen adjunctions called the duality square. This allows us to show that for any cofibrant dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras with degree-wise finite dimensional bounded below (resp. bounded above) homology and the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras with degree-wise finite dimensional bounded above (resp. bounded below) homology are equivalent. This section is based on the results of
[
RiL22a
]
, which we extend to a positive characteristic setting. They play a key role in the companion paper
[
GRiL23a
]
about formal moduli problems and will extended into the context of mapping coalgebras in
[
GRiL24
]
.

8.1.
Lifting linear duality

Linear duality lifts to (co)algebras over an operad and to (co)algebras over a cooperad, always sending types of coalgebras to types of algebras.

The linear duality functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{gr}\penalty\ \Bbbk\text{-}\mathsf{mod}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}\coloneqq[X,\Bbbk]\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}

lifts to pdg
\Bbbk
-modules and to dg modules.

The Sweedler dual functor.
The linear duality functor lifts to (co)algebras over an operad and admits an adjoint which generalizes the Sweedler dual functor of
[
Swe69
]
.

Lemma 64
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. The linear dual functor lifts to a functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}

between dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras and dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras.

Proof.

Let
C
be a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra. Any
\mu
in
\mathcal{P}
gives a decomposition map

\Delta_{\mu}:C\longrightarrow C^{\otimes n}\penalty\ .

And any such map induces the following composition map

\gamma_{\mu}:(C^{*})^{\otimes n}\rightarrowtail(C^{\otimes n})^{*}\xrightarrow{(\Delta_{\mu})^{*}}C\penalty\ .

It can be checked that the collection of
\{\gamma_{\mu}\}
induces a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure on
C^{*}
, and that this defines a functor.
∎

Proposition 66
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. There is a contravariant adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras and dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proof.

This follows directly from the adjoint lifting theorems of Appendix
A.1
.
∎

Topological dual functor.
The linear dual functor also lifts to (co)algebras over a cooperad. It admits an adjoint, which can be though as a
topological dual
type of functor.

Lemma 65
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. The linear dual functor lifts to a functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\right)^{\mathrm{op}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}

between pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras and qp-complete pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.
Moreover, such a functor sends curved pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras to qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

Proof.

Let
W
be a pdg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra. The following map

\gamma_{W^{*}}:\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),(W^{*})^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb S_{n}}\rightarrowtail\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),(W^{\otimes n})^{*}]^{\mathbb S_{n}}\xrightarrow{(\Delta_{W})^{*}}W^{*}

can be shown to induce a pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra structure on
W^{*}
. Let us check that
W^{*}
is qp-complete.

It follows from Corollary
6
that
W
can be written as the following colimit

W\cong\underset{i\in\omega}{\mathrm{colim}}\penalty\ F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}W

where
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}W
is the image of
W
by the idempotent comonad induced by the inclusion
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{C}
. Therefore

W^{*}\cong\lim_{i\in\omega}\penalty\ (F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}W)^{*}\penalty\ .

If we show that
(F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}W)^{*}
is qp-complete for every
i\in\omega
, then it is clear that
W
is qp-complete. But this follows from the fact that since
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}W
is a curved
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra, its linear dual
(F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}W)^{*}
is naturally a curved
F_{i}^{\mathrm{qp}}\mathcal{C}
-algebra. Therefore it is qp-complete.

Finally, the assertion about curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras follows by direct inspection.
∎

Proposition 67
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad. There is an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\right)^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras and qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Proof.

by the adjoint lifting theorem (see Appendix
A.1
) the composite functor

\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\to\left(\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\right)^{\mathrm{op}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathrm{op}}

has a right adjoint. The expected right adjoint is just its restriction to complete algebras.
∎

Remark 58
.

It can be shown that the adjunction in the above proposition is given by the same functors as the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{pdg}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\right)^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Showing this amounts to showing that the topological dual functor
(-)^{\vee}
at the level of qp-complete pdg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras sends qp-complete curved
\mathcal{C}
-algebras to curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. This is the case since the arguments of
[
RiL22a
, Propositions 2.13 and 2.14]
generalize easily to positive characteristic, under the assumption that
\mathcal{C}
is quasi-planar.

Algebraic duality square.
These two linear duality adjunctions intertwine the standard bar-cobar adjunction with the complete bar-cobar adjunction in the following way.

Proposition 68

(After
[
RiL22a
, Theorem 2.16]
)
.

Let
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
be a curved twisting morphism between a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
. The following square of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\alpha}^{\mathsf{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\alpha}^{\mathsf{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is commutative in the following sense: right adjoints from the top right to the bottom left are canonically naturally isomorphic. This yields a canonical mate isomorphism between the left adjoints going the other way round.

Proof.

It follows from the fact that, for any curved
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
W
, there is a natural isomorphism

(\Omega_{\alpha}W)^{\circ}\cong\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}W^{*}\penalty\ ,

which comes from the natural isomorphism

(\mathcal{P}\circ W)^{\circ}\cong L^{\mathcal{P}}(W^{*})\penalty\ ,

at the level of graded
\Bbbk
-modules. This later isomorphism is a formal consequence of the construction of
(-)^{\circ}
.
∎

8.2.
Homotopical duality squares

Let us fix a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
. When we consider the duality square which corresponds to the canonical curved twisting morphism
\iota:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}
, this square can be promoted in the following way: all the adjunctions in the square are Quillen adjunctions.

Proposition 69

(After
[
RiL22a
, Theorem 2.22]
)
.

The following square of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathsf{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathsf{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is made of Quillen adjunctions, when one considers the right/left transferred structure from dg modules on the left hand side, and where one considers the transferred structures along the bar-cobar adjunctions on the right hand side.

Proof.

We already know that the left vertical adjunction and the two horizontal adjunctions are all Quillen adjunctions. The only thing left to check is that the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\right)^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is also a Quillen adjunction. This follows from the fact that the model structure on
\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathrm{op}}
is transferred from that on
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}^{\mathrm{op}}
, and therefore it suffices to notice that the composite left adjoint functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \Omega\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\circ}}

is left Quillen.
∎

Remark 59
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a cofibrant dg operad. There is an analogue homotopical duality square where the bar-cobar adjunctions are the quasi-planar bar-cobar adjunctions of Subsection
3.14
.

Proposition 70
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a cofibrant dg operad. The adjunction induced at the level of
\infty
-categories on the localisations of the model categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathrm{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathrm{Q.iso}^{-1}]^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

restricts to an equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathrm{Q.iso}^{-1}]_{\mp}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathrm{Q.iso}^{-1}]_{\pm}^{\mathrm{f.d.}\mathrm{op}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between the full sub-
\infty
-category
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}[\mathrm{Q.iso}^{-1}]_{\mp}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}
of
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathrm{Q.iso}^{-1}]
spanned by coalgebras with degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded above (resp. bounded below) homology and the full sub-
\infty
-category
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathrm{Q.iso}^{-1}]_{\pm}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}
of
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathrm{Q.iso}^{-1}]
spanned by algebras with degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded below (resp. bounded above) homology.

Proof.

Since the weak-equivalence of cofibrant dg operads
\Omega B(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathcal{P}
yields Quillen equivalences between their respective the categories of algebras and coalgebras by Theorem
31
, we can restrict to the case where
\mathcal{P}=\Omega\mathcal{C}
for
\mathcal{C}
a quasi-planar conilpotent curved cooperad, without any loss of generality.

It suffices then to show that the two derived functors associated to the Quillen adjunction
(-)^{\ast}\dashv(-)^{\circ}
interchange objects with degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded above homology with objects with degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded below homology. One the one hand, it is clear that the (derived) functor
(-)^{\ast}
does so. On the other hand, let
A
be a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra with degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded below homology (the bounded above case is analogue).

Let us first assume that
A
is degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded below. Let us denote
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}_{+}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}
and
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}_{-}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}
respectively the full subcategory of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras who are degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded below, and the full subcategory of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras spanned by objects who are degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded above. The linear duality functor from dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras to dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras restricts to an equivalence of categories

\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}_{-}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}\xrightarrow{(-)^{\ast}}\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}_{+}^{\mathrm{f.d.}\mathrm{op}}

whose pseudo-inverse is also a lifting
(-)^{\ast}
of the linear duality functor of dg modules. The following square diagram is commutative

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}_{+}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}_{-}^{\mathrm{f.d.}\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{cog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{curv}\penalty\ \mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{qp}\text{-}\mathsf{comp}}\right)^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\ast}}

This gives a sequence of natural isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}B_{\mathcal{C}}A)^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{B}_{\mathcal{C}}((B_{\mathcal{C}}A)^{\ast})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}(A^{\ast})\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Notice that
\eta_{A^{*}}:A^{\ast}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\widehat{B}_{\mathcal{C}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}A^{\ast}
is a quasi-isomorphism of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras. Since
(\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}B_{\mathcal{C}}A)^{\circ}
is weakly-equivalent to the value of the left derived functor of
(-)^{\circ}
taken on
A
, then the derived unit of adjunction is a quasi-isomorphism for any
A
which is degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded below.

In the general case where
A
has degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded below homology, the homotopy transfer theorem endows the dg module
\mathrm{H}(A)
(with zero differential) with the structure of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra and provides a zig-zag of weak-equivalences of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras relating
A
to
\mathrm{H}(A)
. Thus the image of
A
through the derived functor of
(-)^{\circ}
is equivalent to that of
\mathrm{H}(A)
.
∎

Appendix A
Adjoint lifting theorems, right and left transferred structures

A.1.
Adjoint lifting theorem

The goal of this appendix is to give recollections on the adjoint lifting theorem. We mainly follow the work of P. T. Johnstone in
[
Joh75
]
.

Let us consider two categories
\mathsf{C},\mathsf{D}
. Let
M
be a monad on
\mathsf{C}
and let
N
be a monad on
\mathsf{D}
. Moreover, let us consider a commutative diagrams of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Alg}_{\mathsf{C}}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Alg}_{\mathsf{D}}(N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}

where
U^{N}
and
U^{M}
are the monadic forgetful functors and where
R
is a right adjoint functor between the underlying categories. Notice that the commutativity of this diagram induces a natural transformation
\lambda:NR\longrightarrow RM
that satisfies some coherence conditions.

We define the natural transformation
\xi:MLN\longrightarrow ML
as follows. First we consider the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi:L\penalty\ N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\penalty\ N\penalty\ R\penalty\ L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\penalty\ R\penalty\ M\penalty\ L\penalty\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\penalty\ L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\penalty\ N\penalty\ \eta_{RL}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\penalty\ \lambda\penalty\ L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{LR}\penalty\ M\penalty\ L}

where
\eta_{RL}:\mathrm{Id}\longrightarrow R\penalty\ L
is the unit of adjunction and
\epsilon_{LR}:L\penalty\ R\longrightarrow\mathrm{Id}
the counit. Now simply notice that
M\cong U_{M}\penalty\ F_{M}
, where
F_{M}
is the free
M
-algebra functor. Therefore by adjunction, we get a natural transformation

\xi:F_{M}\penalty\ L\penalty\ N\longrightarrow F_{M}\penalty\ L\penalty\ ,

by taking the transpose of
\varphi
.

Theorem 14

(Adjoint lifting theorem)
.

Let us suppose that
R
has a left adjoint
L
. Then
R_{m}
has a left adjoint
L_{m}
if and only if for every
N
-algebra
(A,\gamma_{A})
, the reflexive pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{M}\penalty\ L\penalty\ N\penalty\ (A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{M}\penalty\ L\penalty\ (A)\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{M}\penalty\ L\penalty\ \gamma_{A}}

has a coequaliser in
\mathsf{Alg}_{\mathsf{C}}(M)
. If this is the case, then the image of the functor
L_{M}
on
A
is given by the above coequalizer.

Proof.

If such coequalisers exists, then the natural construction that sends
A
to this coequaliser can be checked to be the right adjoint of
R_{m}
. Conversely, if
R_{m}
has a left adjoint
L_{m}
, then the universal property satisfied by
L_{m}(A)
makes it the expected coequaliser.
∎

Now, let
M,N
be two monads on a category
\mathsf{C}
and let us consider a morphism of monads
f:N\longrightarrow M
which leads to a functor
U_{f}:\mathsf{Alg}\left(M\right)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Alg}\left(N\right)
above the identity functor of the category
\mathsf{C}
.

Proposition 71
.

Let us suppose that
\mathsf{C}
has reflexive coequalisers and these coequalisers are preserved by
M
, thus also by the forgetful functor
U_{M}:\mathsf{Alg}\left(M\right)\longrightarrow\mathsf{C}
. Let us also suppose that the map
f(X):N(X)\twoheadrightarrow M(X)
is an epimorphism for every object
X
in
\mathsf{C}
. Then the functor
U^{f}:\mathsf{Alg}\left(M\right)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Alg}\left(N\right)
above
\mathsf{C}
has a left adjoint
T_{f}
and the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{f}(A).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{A}}

is a pushout diagram in
\mathsf{C}
, for every
N
-algebra
(A,\gamma_{A})
.

Proof.

We can notice that this pushout in
\mathsf{C}
is canonically isomorphic to the image the coequaliser of the reflexive pair of Theorem
14
by the forgetful functor
U_{M}
, which creates and reflects reflexive coequilisers.
∎

A.2.
Right and left transfers of model structures

The goal of this appendix is to review the different results that allow one to transfer a model category structure along an adjunction. For the rest of this appendix, we consider an adjunction between presentable categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

A.2.1.
Right transfer

Let us suppose that
\mathsf{C}
is endowed with a cofibrantly generated (thus combinatorial) model structure. The sets of maps in
\mathsf{C}
that form its model structure induce a sets of maps in
\mathsf{D}
via the adjunction
L\dashv R
.

Definition 84

(Sets of maps in
\mathsf{D}
)
.

Let
f:X\longrightarrow Y
be a morphism in
\mathsf{D}
. Let us call it

•

a
fibration
if
R(f)
is a fibration;

•

a
weak-equivalence
if
R(f)
is a weak equivalence;

•

an
acyclic fibration
if
R(f)
is an acyclic fibration (equivalently if
f
is both a fibration and a weak-equivalence);

•

a
cofibration
if it has the left lifting property with respect to acyclic fibrations;

•

an
acyclic cofibration
if it is both a cofibration and a weak equivalence;

•

a
generating cofibration
if it is the image through
L
of a generating cofibration of
\mathsf{C}
;

•

a
generating acyclic cofibration
if it is the image through
L
of a generating acyclic cofibration of
\mathsf{C}
;

•

a
left fibration-lifting map
is it has the left lifting property with respect to fibrations.

We will refer to them as
the sets of maps in

\mathsf{D}

induced by the adjunction

L\dashv R
.

Lemma 66
.

The sets of maps in
\mathsf{D}
induced by the adjunction
L\dashv R
satisfy the following properties.

(1)

The weak-equivalences, the cofibrations and the fibrations are stable through composition and retracts. Furthermore, they contain all isomorphisms.

(2)

The weak-equivalences follows the 2-out-of-3 rule and the 2-out-of-6 rule.

(3)

Every commutative square in
\mathsf{D}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

admits a lifting whenever

(a)

f
is a cofibration and
g
is an acylic fibration,

(b)

or
f
is a left fibration-lifting map and
g
is a fibration.

(4)

Every map
f:X\longrightarrow Y
may be factored in a natural way as either

(a)

the composition of a cofibration followed by an acyclic fibration,

(b)

or the composition of a left fibration-lifting map followed by a fibration.

(5)

Cofibrations are retracts of transfinite compositions of pushouts of generating cofibrations.

(6)

Left fibration-lifting maps are retracts of transfinite compositions of pushouts of generating acyclic cofibrations.

Proof.

It manly follows from a straightforward check and from the small object argument.
∎

Theorem 15

(Right acyclicity condition,
[
Hov99
]
)
.

The following assertions about the sets of maps in
\mathsf{D}
induced by the adjunction
L\dashv R
are equivalent.

(1)

These maps define a combinatorial model category structure on
\mathsf{D}
.

(2)

The set of left fibration-lifting maps of
\mathsf{D}
is equal to the set of acyclic cofibrations.

(3)

The set of left fibration-lifting maps of
\mathsf{D}
is contained in the set of acyclic cofibrations of
\mathsf{D}
.

(4)

The set of left fibration-lifting maps of
\mathsf{D}
is contained in the set of weak-equivalences of
\mathsf{D}
.

Proof.

Clearly, (1) implies (2). Conversely, if (2) is satified, then by Lemma
66
, the sets of maps in
\mathsf{D}
induced by the adjunction
L\dashv R
form a model category structure. Therefore (1) and (2) are equivalent.

It is clear that (2) implies (3) and that (3) implies (4). Let us assume (4) and prove (2). Let
f:U\to Y
be an acylic cofibration. Let us decompose
f:X\longrightarrow Y
as a left fibration-lifting map
a:X\longrightarrow U
followed by a fibration
b:U\longrightarrow Y
. By the 2-out-of-3 rule and since
f
and
a
are weak equivalences,
b
is an acyclic fibration. Thus the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}

has a lifting
i
, which yields the following retract commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}

Since
f
is a retract of
a
, it is a left fibration-lifting map.
∎

Proposition 72

(
[
Hov99
]
)
.

Let us suppose that

(1)

every object in
\mathsf{D}
has a natural fibrant replacement functor, that is, there exists an endofunctor
F
of
\mathsf{D}
together with a natural transformation
e:\mathrm{Id}\longrightarrow F
so that for every object
X
,
F(X)
is fibrant and the map
X\longrightarrow F(X)
is a weak-equivalence;

(2)

every fibrant object
X
has a path object, that is, the diagonal map
X\longrightarrow X\times X
can be factored by a weak-equivalence followed by a fibration.

Then the sets of maps in
\mathsf{D}
induced by the adjunction
L\dashv R
endow
\mathsf{D}
with a model category structure.

Proof.

Let
f:X\longrightarrow Y
be a left fibration-lifting map. Its lifting property gives us a map
p:Y\longrightarrow F(X)
such that
pf=e(X)
. Let
P
be a path object of
Y
. It fits in the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)\times F(Y)\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F(f)p,e(Y))}

This square has a lifting
g:Y\longrightarrow P
. Since each of the two projections
P\longrightarrow F(Y)
are weak-equivalences and since the map
e(Y):Y\longrightarrow F(Y)
is a weak-equivalence, the 2-out-of-3 rule tells us that
g
and
F(f)p
are also weak equivalences. Then
pf=e(X)
is also a weak-equivalence. The 2-out-of-6 rule implies that the three maps
f,p,F(f)
are also weak-equivalences. We conclude by Theorem
15
.
∎

A.2.2.
Left transfer

Let us suppose that
\mathsf{D}
is endowed with a cofibrantly generated model structure. The sets of maps in
\mathsf{D}
that form a model structure induce sets of maps in
\mathsf{C}
via the adjunction
L\dashv R
.

Definition 85

(Sets of maps in
\mathsf{C}
)
.

Let
f:X\longrightarrow Y
be a morphism in
\mathsf{C}
. Let us call it

•

a
cofibration
if
L(f)
